| 1Tim | 1,588 | 6.30 (10) | 6.30 (10) | 3.78 (6) | 8.82 (14) |
| 1John | 2,136 | 1.87 (4) | 1.40 (3) | 2.81 (6) | 3.75 (8) |
| Acts | 18,393 | 1.41 (26) | 4.57 (84) | 2.01 (37) | 1.36 (25) |

Revelation reaches the three writings outside the canon (2.8 to 4.6) as it reaches the letters inside it (0 to 6.3). The instrument is seeing real kinship: Matthew to the Didache is 15.5, the known relation. Read: 1 Clement's links are the doxology with κράτος again (1 Clem. 64–65), which now runs Revelation, 1 Peter, Jude, 1 Timothy, 1 Clement; the Didache's are the coming on the clouds (ch. 16; Rev 1:7) and the list of vices (Rev 21:8; 22:15); Hermas's gather on Rev 2:10, the devil, fear, faithfulness, tribulation.

What this does to 2E.3. I proposed the edge as a test and it shows nothing either way: writings of the next generation share Revelation's wording whether they drew on it or it on them. It does bear on the phrase "that precise canon": the set of texts tied to Revelation is wider than the canon. For the forward reading that is more borrowers. For the reverse it is more books in the seer's hands, the Didache and 1 Clement among them, which makes that reading dearer and not different in kind. The argument from the shape of the graph is where it was: unpriced.

2F.3. **Philo and Hebrews: Hebrews has Philo's state apart from Revelation.** Checked in the seats.

- The cutter. Philo, Her. 140: God "sharpened the cutter of all things, his own Logos" (τὸν τομέα τῶν συμπάντων αὑτοῦ λόγον) and divided. Heb 4:12: the Logos of God is τομώτερος, "more cutting than any two-edged sword", piercing to the division of soul and spirit. τομ- of the Logos is in Hebrews and Philo and is not in Revelation.
- The high priest. Philo, Fug. 108: the high priest is "not a human being but a divine Logos". Hebrews has ἀρχιερεύς sixteen times, and "a great high priest … Jesus" at 4:14. Revelation has the word not once: it has the garment, the ποδήρης (1:13, its one place in the New Testament), and no title.
- What Revelation and Hebrews share that Philo lacks: δίστομος. In the New Testament it stands at Rev 1:16, 2:12 and Heb 4:12 and nowhere else; in Revelation the two-edged sword comes from the mouth of the one whose name is the Logos of God (19:13, 15), and in Hebrews the Logos of God is sharper than any two-edged sword. (In the seated Septuagint, Ps 149:6.)

(The manna of Heb 9:4, counted at v0.7 as the one locus going the other way, is withdrawn: 2B.7.) So on the test of 2B.7, run honestly: Hebrews did not get the cutter or the high priest through Revelation, since Revelation has neither. Hebrews and Revelation each draw on Philo, and they share one further thing, the Logos of God with the two-edged blade. That is two siblings with a tie between them, and the tie's direction is open: Hebrews joining Philo's cutter to Revelation's sword (Newton's reading), or Revelation turning Hebrews' comparison into a vision. This is the second locus, after the vote in Acts, where Philo cuts against a simple arrow through Revelation; the manna of Heb 9:4 is the one that goes with it. Philo is decisive here in the sense that he can be made to decide; so far he decides none for and two against a route that runs through Revelation alone.

2F.4. Where the file stands after these. Standing, with runs behind them: forty-seven transforms (of the forty-eight then entered; fifty-one of fifty-two now), none matched by a control, forty-one unique in the New Testament; four knots (Matthew's tomb and wedding, Mark's supper of Herod, John's passion); the partitions as relations that the Septuagint does not supply; Matthew's lean to Revelation's vocabulary, twice measured; the conceded letters, seven. Not standing as tested: a law per Gospel (2F.1); direction from cost (2D.5); the canon's edge (2F.2); a route through Revelation for Hebrews' Philo (2F.3). The author's argument on direction (2E.1) is recorded as the author's, with these results beside it.

## 2G. Two reviews of v0.6, verified and worked in

Both were written before v0.7 and ask for tests v0.7 had run: the held-out test of the laws (2F.1, not passed) and the canon boundary (2F.2, no edge). The second review said of that outcome in advance that links beyond the canon need not count against the thesis, since descendants may lie outside the collection; what the test cannot do is tell descendants from antecedents. Agreed, and that is how 2F.2 reads.

2G.1. **An error of mine, caught.** The review asked for Hebrews' "covered manna" to be set against Exodus. In the seat, Heb 9:4 reads τὴν κιβωτὸν τῆς διαθήκης περικεκαλυμμένην πάντοθεν χρυσίῳ, ἐν ᾗ στάμνος χρυσῆ ἔχουσα τὸ μάννα: the ark is covered on every side with gold, and the manna is in a golden jar, which is Exod 16:33. The participle was never the manna's. The derivation `rev2.17→heb9.4` is corrected in the engine to what the text has (the manna kept, the hiddenness dropped), and a control now fits it. With it goes the one case counted for a Philonic state reaching a letter through Revelation.

2G.2. **Two verses of the Septuagint** (both reviews). For each emission, the best pair of Septuagint verses. Over the 43 Revelation-seeded derivations: forward 1234 bits; one verse 1893; two verses 1968. Forward is cheaper in 41 of 43. Two cautions the run itself raises. The members are short, so a pair of unrelated verses often holds them all (in 24 of 43): the best pair for Matt 24:30 is Gen 12:3 with Isa 19:1, which nobody proposes. And the tariff charges for choosing a verse by the size of the book it is chosen from, which favours a source of 405 verses over one of 28,933; that term, and not the fit, is most of the difference. The honest form of this test is the received pair itself, checked directly: Dan 7:13 with Zech 12:10 in the seat hold two of Matthew's four members, the clouds and the mourning. They do not hold "they will see" (Zechariah has ἐπιβλέψονται) or "all the tribes of the earth" (Zech 12:11–14 has the land mourning tribe by tribe). Those two are in Rev 1:7 and in Matt 24:30. So the two-verse account needs a third text for the phrase (Gen 12:3) and one change of verb, made alike in both books.

2G.3. **The grammar fitted where direction is not excluded, then tried on the Gospels** (the second review, §5). The seven late-letter derivations use seven operators: PARTITION, REORDER, SUBSTITUTE, SUBSTITUTE-OBJECT, ADD, EVENT, CITE-AS-PROMISE. Of the 34 Gospel derivations, 23 can be stated in those seven without addition: nine of the ten partitions, all five of John's promises and place-names, four of Matthew's five. The eleven that cannot need the operators of narrative: REVERSE-DOER (4), UNDO (4), TRANSFER-ATTRIBUTE (2), NEGATE (1), CITE-AS-SCRIPTURE (1). This is the first result in the file where something fitted on one set is tried on another, and two-thirds carries over. Its limit is that all forty-eight were written by one hand in one afternoon; so what it reports is coverage, that one reader's seven names suffice for 23 more of the same reader's cases, and not that the letters' grammar predicts the Gospels'.

2G.4. **Reuse** (the second review, §10: a grammar should get cheaper as it is used). It has not yet. New operators kept arriving: the thirteenth and fourteenth names entered at derivations 38 and 41 of 48. And the share of derivations needing no member supplied from outside the seed does not rise: 8 of the first twelve, then 5, 7, 4. Half of all derivations (24 of 48) still need a word brought in from the target. So there is no economy of scale to report; each new passage has cost about what the first ones did. The review's fuller tariff, access plus grammar plus transforms plus residue, with the library priced and the grammar credited once, is adopted as the design and is not built.

2G.5. **CITE-AS-PROMISE** (the second review, §6) is now its own operator: a change of who speaks. Revelation says "I will give you the crown of life" (2:10) and "Behold, I make all things new" (21:5); James has "the crown of life which he promised" (1:12) and 2 Peter "new heavens and a new earth according to his promise" (3:13). The later text sets the utterance before itself. Both are in the engine (`rev2.10→jas1.12`, added; `rev21.1→2pet3.13`). Isaiah 65:17 is the rival for 2 Peter and is one of its controls. A census of every promise the letters refer to, against what Revelation says in the first person, is owed, and is directional at the level of discourse, as the review says. With it, the review's form for the argument of 2E.6, to be built: a genealogy of necessary innovations, one row an innovation (the Logos named Jesus; the high priest named Jesus; the Lamb as the one slain; the figure given a life), with its earliest extant wording, the earlier pieces it joins, the operation, the later texts that keep it, and what each account must suppose that is not extant.

2G.6. Taken as design and not done: scenes all four Gospels share (the tomb, the Baptist's announcement, the feeding, the entry, the passion) as the surface for comparing them, the station held and the Gospel varied; the Gospel named from the transform alone, on read loci and with the operators as features, which 2F.1 did not test; the library priced.

## 2H. The shared scenes, and the figure

2H.1. **The station held, the Gospel varied** (`scenes.py`). Five scenes that all four Gospels tell, with the usual bounds entered from memory and printed in the output: the Baptist's announcement, the feeding, the entry, the cross, the tomb. For each Gospel's telling, only the wording that is that Gospel's alone within the scene is looked up in Revelation.

Result. The Baptist, the feeding and the entry give nothing: three hits in all, each a commonplace ("in those days", "a great crowd", "a great voice"). The instrument is strict, and it misses what two Gospels share (it does not return Mark 1:7, since Luke has the same words) and what is built from common words. With that said, three of the five scenes are empty under it. The cross and the tomb are not: every hit of 2C.7 and 2C.6 returns (Matthew's earthquake, white as snow, as dead; Mark's white robe; Luke's "place called"; John's place in Hebrew and city of the crucifixion). So what the Gospels take from Revelation by their own wording, at stations they share, sits at the end of the story.

2H.2. **Revelation 11 divided four ways.** Reading the cross and tomb hits, one region of Revelation kept returning: 11:2–13, the two witnesses, who are killed in "the great city … where their Lord was crucified", whose bodies are not allowed a tomb, who after three days and a half stand up, while great fear falls on those who watch, a great voice calls them up, there is a great earthquake, and "the rest became afraid and gave glory to God". I set each member of that passage against the four Gospels' cross and tomb (Matt 27:32–28:10; Mark 15:21–16:8; Luke 23:26–24:12; John 19:16–20:18):

| Member of Rev 11 | Matthew | Mark | Luke | John |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the city where their Lord was crucified (11:8) | — | — | — | 19:20, 19:41 |
| μνῆμα, a tomb (11:9) | — | 15:46, 16:2 | 23:53, 24:1 | — |
| three days (and a half) (11:9, 11) | 27:40, 27:63 | 15:29 | 24:7 | — |
| those who watch, θεωρέω (11:11, 12) | 27:55, 28:1 | 15:40, 15:47, 16:4 | 23:35, 23:48 | 20:6, 20:12, 20:14 |
| great fear (11:11) | 27:54, 28:8 | — | — | — |
| they stood up (11:11) / raised | 27:52, 27:64, 28:6 | 16:6 | 24:6 | — |
| a great voice (11:12) | 27:46, 27:50 | 15:34, 15:37 | 23:46 | — |
| a great earthquake (11:13) | 27:51, 28:2, 28:4 | — | — | — |
| ἔμφοβοι, afraid (11:13) | — (27:54 has ἐφοβήθησαν σφόδρα: the sense, not the word; moved to the row of great fear, where it already stood) | — | 24:5 | — |
| gave glory to God (11:13) | — | — | 23:47 | — |
| the holy city (11:2) | 27:53 | — | — | — |

Checked across the New Testament. "The holy city" stands at Matt 4:5 and 27:53 and four times in Revelation, and nowhere else. A great earthquake at the death and the rising is Matthew's alone. ἔμφοβος stands five times: Luke 24:5 and 24:37, Acts twice, and Rev 11:13. μνῆμα for the tomb is Mark's and Luke's word and Rev 11:9's; Matthew and John say μνημεῖον. "The city where … was crucified" is John's and Rev 11:8's (2C.6). All four have the watchers.

Read. The received commentary already holds that the two witnesses are told on the pattern of the passion, so the relation is not in dispute; its direction on that account runs from the passion to Revelation. What the table adds is how the wording lies: no one Gospel has the passage's members, each has some that the others lack, and Matthew's are the ones Mark does not have (the earthquake, the holy city, the fear). That is the shape of 2.3 at the scale of a scene. As with 2.3, a division and a gathering describe it equally (2D.5), and on the gathering the seer has all four passion narratives open at once.

2H.3. What the scenes do to the four laws. They do not restore them. They do show where the Gospels differ from each other in their use of Revelation, by a method that did not pick the verses: at the cross and tomb, and in Matthew most. The fair test of a law remains as at 2G.6.

2H.4. **The figure: a genealogy of necessary innovations, begun.** ("First found" below means earliest in the texts that survive and are seated. An earlier joining in a text that does not survive is not excluded by a table of extant wording; for the rows whose order is in dispute the table gives attestation only.) One row an innovation; the wording as seated; what it joins. The dated texts fix only the first two rows: the Septuagint is before Philo and Philo before the rest. Which of the later rows is earliest is the question in dispute and the table does not assume it.

| Innovation | Wording, as seated | Joins | Also in | Not in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A high priest named Ἰησοῦς, crowned, and "Ἀνατολή is his name" | Zech 6:11–12 (Septuagint) | priest, crown, the name Ἀνατολή | Zech 3 | — |
| The high priest is the Logos; Ἀνατολή is the Logos's name, the addressee's name cut | Philo, Fug. 108; Conf. 62 (#1758 §3–4) | Logos + high priest + Ἀνατολή | — | the name Ἰησοῦς |
| The Logos is a cutter | Philo, Her. 140 | Logos + division | Heb 4:12 | Revelation |
| One like a son of man in the ποδήρης in the midst of the lampstands, who is Ἰησοῦς | Rev 1:13 with 1:1, 1:5, 22:16 | the priest's robe (Zech 3:4; Ezek 9:2) + son of man (Dan 7:13) + Philo's one in the midst + the name | — | the title high priest; Hebrews has the title and not the robe |
| The one named the Logos of God, with a blade from the mouth | Rev 19:13, 15; 1:16 (δίστομος) | Logos + the two-edged sword | Heb 4:12 (the Logos of God and the two-edged sword) | Philo (no sword), John (no sword) |
| "A great high priest … Jesus the Son of God" | Heb 4:14 | the title + the name | — | Revelation; Philo (no Jesus) |
| The Lamb, slain, who bought with his blood | Rev 5:6, 9 | lamb + slaughter + purchase | 1 Pet 1:18–19 (ἀμνός; ransom); 1 Cor 6:20 (bought); John 1:29 (ὁ ἀμνὸς τοῦ θεοῦ) | — |
| "The Logos became flesh"; grace and truth "through Jesus Christ" | John 1:14, 17 | Logos + a life + the name | — | Revelation and Hebrews give the Logos no birth |

What the table shows without a chronology. The pieces Philo has apart (Logos, high priest, the withheld name, the one in the midst) are first found *joined to the name Ἰησοῦς* in three later places, and each joins a different set: Revelation the robe, the midst and the name; Hebrews the title, the cutter and the name; John the Logos, a life and the name. No one of the three contains another's join, which is why 2F.3 came out as siblings. Hebrews and Revelation share one join beyond Philo (the Logos of God with the two-edged blade); John and Revelation share one (the Logos as a name for the figure; the Lamb). On Revelation First the reading is that Revelation makes the joins of rows four, five and seven and the others develop them. The table does not show that; it shows that the figure is assembled across these three and that the Septuagint and Philo hold every piece but the name's attachment and the life. What each account must suppose beyond the extant texts, row by row, is the column still owed, and the Slavonic Josephus is not entered until its station is argued.

## 2I. Revelation 11 in the engine; the Gospel named from the transform

2I.0. The author, 14:28: "thats where we ran the frozen sappho 31 operators and found 8/9". Checked in the record: #1495, *The Sign Under Test*, ran the twelve frozen clauses on Revelation 12, the woman and the dragon, and passed eight of nine stations. The witnesses are 11:2–13, the passage before it; the two stand side by side with 11:14–19 between. So this stretch of the book, chapters 11 and 12, is where two instruments built apart have each found structure: upstream, Sappho 31's reception operator in chapter 12; downstream, the four passion narratives in chapter 11.

2I.1. **One seed region, four targets.** `rev11→matt`, `→mark`, `→luke`, `→john`. The seed is Rev 11:2 with 11:8–13, ten members: the city of the crucifixion, the holy city, μνῆμα, three days, those who watch, great fear, a great voice, a great earthquake, ἔμφοβοι, gave glory to God. The table of 2H.2 has an eleventh row, the rising, which is not entered: Rev 11:11 has ἔστησαν ἐπὶ τοὺς πόδας αὐτῶν and the Gospels have ἠγέρθη, so there is no shared form for a member to hold, and the engine is an engine of wording. Two members are entered by more than one form and that is a loosening, stated: great fear passes on φόβος μέγας or ἐφοβήθησαν σφόδρα (Matt 27:54), and the glory on ἔδωκαν δόξαν τῷ θεῷ or ἐδόξαζεν τὸν θεόν (Luke 23:47). ἔμφοβοι is by the word alone, so Matthew does not have it. Each Gospel's derivation is one PARTITION: the members it keeps in its cross and tomb. The test of each is double: its own telling must have every member kept and none of the members dropped; and no other Gospel's telling may do the same.

| Gospel | keeps | own telling | the other three, scored on this Gospel's part |
|---|---|---|---|
| Matthew | the holy city, three days, great fear, those who watch, a great voice, a great earthquake | 6 of 6; 4 of 4 absent | Mark 0.6; Luke 0.3; John 0.4 |
| Mark | three days, a tomb (μνῆμα), those who watch, a great voice | 4 of 4; 6 of 6 absent | Matt 0.6; Luke 0.7; John 0.6 |
| Luke | a tomb (μνῆμα), those who watch, a great voice, afraid (ἔμφοβοι), gave glory to God | 5 of 5; 5 of 5 absent | Matt 0.3; Mark 0.7; John 0.5 |
| John | the great city … where their Lord was crucified, those who watch | 2 of 2; 8 of 8 absent | Matt 0.4; Mark 0.6; Luke 0.5 |

All four fit their own part entire; no Gospel fits another's above 0.7. So the four tellings are four different subsets of one passage, by the engine's own check. Limits: the target bounds and the forms a member may take were set by reading (2H.2), and the order of members is not tested, since a scene spreads them.

2I.2. **The Gospel named from the transform alone** (`named.py`; the fair form of 2F.1, on read loci). The 38 derivations with a Gospel for target, each described only by which operators it uses. One is left out, a model is fitted on the other 37, and it names the Gospel of the one left out.

| | accuracy | always the commonest | labels shuffled, mean (95th) | p |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| operators only | 0.526 | 0.368 | 0.287 (0.447) | 0.005 |
| the same without EVENT | 0.526 | 0.368 | 0.288 (0.447) | 0.010 |
| operators and seed region | 0.474 | 0.368 | 0.287 (0.447) | 0.027 |

(The p is for the whole classification. No test was run for Matthew or for John singly, so "above chance for Matthew and John" in the version note overstated it; and see 2J, which withdraws the result.) It names Matthew 8 or 9 times in 12 and John 9 or 10 in 14; Mark never (0 of 4) and Luke twice in 8. What carries it: Matthew's derivations are almost all a bare PARTITION, with EVENT and TRANSFER-ATTRIBUTE his alone, and no substitution of object in twelve; John's are where the wording is changed, SUBSTITUTE-OBJECT (4 of 14), UNDO (3), ADD (2), all his or nearly. So two of the four laws have something behind them in this form: **Matthew keeps Revelation's words and moves them; John keeps the frame and changes what stands in it.** That agrees with the two measures that needed no reading (2C.2, 2D.3), which found Matthew's vocabulary leaning to Revelation's. Mark and Luke are not told apart from the rest.

The limit is the one stated in the script: the operators were assigned by the reader who knew each target's Gospel, and thirty-eight is few. EVENT is the operator most at that reader's discretion, and the result holds without it. An assignment of operators by a second hand, blind to the Gospel, is the test that would settle whether this is the Gospels' difference or the reader's. **Run in another form at 14:42, section 2J: the operators computed from relations do not name the Gospel. The sentence in bold above is not supported as it stands.**

## 2J. Members as relations; the operators computed

2J.0. The author, 14:42: "lets run it". The instrument owed since 2F.1: a member is no longer a piece of wording with an operator set on it by the reader. It is a relation in six slots, and the operator is whatever differs between the seed's relation and the target's.

2J.1. **The form** (`relations/relations.py`). Agent, action, object, recipient, polarity, mode. Thirty-four loci with a Gospel for target (the four scene derivations of 2I.1 are not relations and are left out): Matthew 11, John 13, Luke 7, Mark 3; forty pairs of relations. Every slot that names wording gives the form as it stands in the verse, and the run checks each in the seat: none missing. Three rules of entry, fixed first: the relation is the clause that holds the shared wording; a party's class (Christ, God, angel, the faithful, the seer, the Baptist; adversary, ruler, guard; all, thing, figure of a parable) is given from the cited verses alone; a minus must show a negative particle in the verse. Example, Rev 5:4 → Luke 4:17: *no one · opens · the book · negative · vision* against *(he) · opens · the book · positive · narrative*. The difference is a negative removed and an agent supplied. Nobody assigned UNDO; it is what the two relations differ by.

2J.2. **The rules that turn a difference into an operator**, written before the run. EVENT: vision to narrative with the action, the polarity and the agent's side kept. REVERSE-DOER: action kept, agent's side reversed. UNDO and NEGATE: the negative removed or set. SUBSTITUTE-OBJECT: action kept, object changed. SUBSTITUTE: action changed, object kept. TRANSFER-ATTRIBUTE: action kept, agent changed within a side. PARTITION, REORDER and ADD state wording, not relation, and stay with the engine, which checks them in the seat.

2J.3. **Computed against assigned**, over the 34 loci.

| operator | both | computed only | assigned only |
|---|---|---|---|
| EVENT | 4 | 5 | 2 |
| REVERSE-DOER | 2 | 1 | 2 |
| UNDO | 3 | 0 | 1 |
| NEGATE | 1 | 2 | 0 |
| SUBSTITUTE-OBJECT | 4 | 4 | 1 |
| SUBSTITUTE | 2 | 4 | 3 |
| TRANSFER-ATTRIBUTE | 2 | 10 | 0 |
| CITE-AS-SCRIPTURE | 1 | 0 | 0 |

The two agree where the difference is a particle: UNDO three of four, and the fourth (Rev 18:21 → John 11:41) is a case where the verse the reader cited does not hold the negative the reader had in mind. They part where the reader used judgment. EVENT was assigned six times and is computed nine: the reader gave it to Matthew five times in eleven and to the others once in twenty-three; computed, it falls on Matthew 3, John 3, Luke 2, Mark 1. TRANSFER-ATTRIBUTE, as ruled, fires twelve times, in every Gospel.

2J.4. **The naming test again**, same model, same shuffles.

| described by | accuracy | commonest | shuffled, mean (95th) | p | named: Matt, John, Luke, Mark |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| operators as assigned (2I.2, these 34) | 0.529 | 0.382 | 0.294 (0.471) | 0.01 | 6/11, 10/13, 2/7, 0/3 |
| the assigned, relation operators only | 0.500 | 0.382 | | 0.04 | |
| the assigned, wording operators only (PARTITION, REORDER, ADD) | 0.382 | 0.382 | | 0.31 | |
| operators as computed | 0.265 | 0.382 | 0.293 (0.471) | 0.66 | 4/11, 5/13, 0/7, 0/3 |
| differences of slots, unruled | 0.324 | 0.382 | 0.290 (0.441) | 0.42 | 2/11, 9/13, 0/7, 0/3 |
| differences and modes | 0.294 | 0.382 | 0.292 (0.471) | 0.54 | 2/11, 8/13, 0/7, 0/3 |
| computed, with the wording operators | 0.206 | 0.382 | | 0.85 | |

2J.5. **What this shows.** The naming of 2I.2 sat in the relation operators as assigned (0.50 alone), and those are the ones the reader set knowing the Gospel. The wording operators, which the engine checks, do not name it (0.38, the base rate). Computed from relations entered slot by slot, the operators name the Gospel no better than chance, and below the commonest guess. So the result of 2I.2 was the reader's hand: EVENT went to Matthew because the reader was reading Matthew. **No law for any one Gospel is shown.** The statement in 2I.2 that Matthew keeps the words and John changes what stands in the frame goes back to a description of the loci read, with one count behind it: the object is changed in 5 of John's 13 and in 4 of the other 21 (Fisher, p = 0.25), which is not a difference.

The limits cut the other way too, and are stated. The relations were entered by the same reader, so this is not a blind test either; it moves the reader's choice from the operator to the slot, where the seat can check the wording. The rules of 2J.2 are coarse (TRANSFER-ATTRIBUTE at twelve). Thirty-four loci with Mark at three cannot show a small difference. What the run supports is narrow: on these loci, the Gospels do not differ in the kind of change they make to a relation of Revelation's.

2J.6. **What the relations found that the reading had not.** Stated as loci, to be read, not counted.

a. Matt 22:6. Rev 19:21: οἱ λοιποὶ ἀπεκτάνθησαν, the rest were killed, by the sword of the one on the horse. Matthew: οἱ δὲ λοιποὶ … ἀπέκτειναν, the rest killed, the king's slaves. Same subject, same verb, the voice turned: the party that suffers the deed in the vision does it in the parable. The reader had entered this as wording kept (PARTITION). It is a reversal, and the only one in the file made by voice alone.

b. Luke 10:19. Two differences in one verse. Authority *was given to them*, the locusts (ἐδόθη αὐτοῖς ἐξουσία), becomes *I have given you the authority* (δέδωκα ὑμῖν τὴν ἐξουσίαν): recipient reversed, agent supplied. And they are to harm (ἀδικήσουσιν) becomes nothing shall harm you (οὐδὲν ὑμᾶς οὐ μὴ ἀδικήσει): negative set. The reader had one operator for the two.

c. Matt 28:4. The seer falls *as dead* (ὡς νεκρός) before the figure; in Matthew the guards become *as dead* (ὡς νεκροί). The posture passes from the seer to the adversary's watch, and the *fear not* that follows passes from the seer to the women. One scene of Revelation divided between two parties by side.

d. Side reversed, of agent or recipient: seven loci of thirty-four (Matthew 3, Luke 2, Mark 1, John 1). Not a Gospel's mark. It is the commonest strong difference in the set after the change of agent within a side.

## 2K. Two reviews of v0.10, verified

2K.0. Pasted by the author at 14:53, unsigned. Both read v0.10; neither had 2J. Each claim checked before anything was changed.

2K.1. **The first review.** It calls 2I.2 "the real finding" and says it is "the first result here with a p-value behind it that isn't the same hand picking both the operators and the target". The file said the opposite at 2I.2 (the operators were assigned by the reader who knew the Gospel), and 2J has since run the check: computed from relations, the operators do not name the Gospel. So the review's central finding is the one withdrawn. Its "two laws, not four" does not stand; there are none shown. It also says 2G.3 is buried and belongs in the state table. It was in the table (the row on the letters); the row is now worded as coverage, with the second review. Its remarks on the corrections and on Rev 11 are taken as read.

2K.2. **The second review, §4**, asked for what 2J did, before it was done: annotate the source and target relations apart, derive the transformation from the annotations, and do not trust blindness to the Gospel's name, since a reader knows the passages. Two parts of its design are still owed. More than one reader, with agreement measured before any classifier is run. And whole seed regions held out, not single derivations: with the four parts of Rev 11 in the set, leaving one out leaves three of its siblings in. (2J left the four scene derivations out for another reason, so its 34 do not have that fault in the same degree; paired loci such as Rev 3:5 → Matthew and → Luke do.)

2K.3. **§6**, that several operators are one typed change of a slot. That is the form of 2J.2. Its test, whether the permitted substitutions become predictable from source, Gospel and station, is the one 2J failed for Gospel.

2K.4. **§9, the local repairs**, each checked in the file:

| Matt 27:54 under ἔμφοβοι | so; Matthew has ἐφοβήθησαν σφόδρα | the row of 2H.2 corrected: the sense is Matthew's, the word is not |

2K.5. **What the second review proposes next**, entered at section 6 and not begun: Rev 11 as one complete demonstration with every member of the episode in it, the residue included, and both genealogies made executable; Rev 11:8 treated apart, since "where their Lord was crucified" supposes a crucifixion told and does not by itself suppose any extant Gospel; the passage between, 11:14–19, the seventh trumpet, the temple opened and the ark seen, read as the joint of chapters 11 and 12; an inventory of the architecture the passion members stand in (two witnesses, measured space, bounded time, exposure, judgment), so that each genealogy has to say how the members came into it. Its distinction at §7, that independent access to Philo tells against exclusive mediation and not against Revelation First, is the author's position at 2E and is kept there. Its further operation, an ancestral member restored into a derived configuration, needs first a configuration shared with Revelation and with nothing earlier; the review says none is yet shown, and that is right.

## 2L. Revelation 11–12, whole

2L.0. The author, 14:55: "agreed. lets run 11-12". The operation is 2K.5: every member of the episode, the residue with the rest; 11:8 apart; 11:14–19 as the joint; the architecture; both genealogies. Scripts and output: `rev1112/members.py`, `census.py`, `joint.py`, `ties.py`.

2L.1. **The received reading, first.** The two witnesses are Zechariah's two anointed with the powers of Elijah and Moses; their death, exposure and rising are told on the pattern of the passion, which the seer knew. Chapter 12 begins a new vision, built from Genesis 3, Isaiah 26 and 66, Psalm 2 and Daniel 7. On that reading chapter 11 receives from the passion as told, chapter 12 owes the Gospels nothing, and the two chapters are joined only by the seventh trumpet. Each of those three statements is tested below, and two of the three hold.

2L.2. **The inventory.** 123 members: the witnesses (11:1–13) 55, the joint (11:14–19) 23, the sign (12:1–17) 45. Entered verse by verse from Revelation, each checked in its own verse (none missing). Each has its own form, as Revelation words it, and where reading suggested another wording of the same thing a loosened form, searched and reported apart. Each is searched through the Septuagint, the four Gospels with cross and tomb apart from the rest (the ranges of 2H.2), Acts, the letters and the rest of Revelation.

| By its own form, the member is found | witnesses | joint | sign |
|---|---|---|---|
| in the Septuagint and in a Gospel | 18 | 1 | 9 |
| in the Septuagint, in no Gospel | 12 | 6 | 9 |
| in a Gospel, not in the Septuagint | 3 | 1 | 0 |
| in Revelation only | 22 | 15 | 27 |
| … of which nowhere by the loosened form either | 12 | 6 | 13 |
| at a Gospel's cross or tomb | 10 | 1 | 0 |

The residue, then, is most of the passage. Of the witnesses' 55 members 34 are in no Gospel by their own form. The twelve found nowhere by either form: the measuring of the temple, forty-two months, 1,260 days, the two lampstands, the finished testimony, the beast from the abyss, peoples-tribes-tongues-nations, three days and a half, the prophets' tormenting, "Come up here", the enemies beholding, the tenth of the city. Those are the passage's own: its numbers, its beast, its formulae.

The three found in a Gospel and not in the Septuagint: ἔκβαλε ἔξω, cast out outside (Luke 13:28; John 6:37; Acts 7:58, where it is Stephen, cast outside the city and killed); ἐν ἐκείνῃ τῇ ὥρᾳ (Matt 10:19, 18:1, 26:55; Mark 13:11; Luke 7:21); and "where … was crucified" (John 19:20, 19:41). The one in the joint: ἡ βασιλεία τοῦ κόσμου (2L.7).

The ten at a cross or tomb by their own form: the reed (Matt 27:48; Mark 15:36), the holy city (Matt 27:53), πτῶμα (Mark 15:45, of Jesus' body; Mark alone), where crucified (John), three days, μνῆμα (Mark, Luke), the watchers (all four), a great voice (Matthew, Mark, Luke), a great earthquake (Matt 28:2), ἔμφοβοι (Luke 24:5). The reed and πτῶμα are new to the file; 2H.2 did not have them. Nine of the ten are also in the Septuagint as bare forms. So no member decides anything by being available; what there is to explain is that they stand together.

2L.3. **Where each section lands.** A window of forty verses slid through every book; the count is of distinct members of the section standing in it (members too common to mean anything left out: more than 60 verses of the Septuagint, 30 of the New Testament).

| Section | forms | best in the Septuagint | best in the New Testament outside Revelation |
|---|---|---|---|
| witnesses | own | 3 (six stretches tie); Ezek 37:5–38:19 holds 4 when the seat's misprint at 37:5 is read through | **Mark 15:6–46: 5. Matt 27:29–28:2: 5.** Then 3 (Acts 1–2, Acts 7, Luke 17, Luke 23:22–24:5) |
| witnesses | own and loosened | 3 (4 Kgdms 1:1–2:22: fire devours, three days, taken up) | **Mark 15:26–16:19: 7. Matt 27:29–28:2: 7. Luke 23:22–24:5: 5** |
| joint | own and loosened | 2 (Ps 1–5; Ps 143–145; Jer 23–25; Ezek 37–38) | Acts 3:20–4:33: 3. Then 2 (1 Thess 2–4; Matt 3:15–5:12; Matt 23:31–24:31; Matt 27:29–28:2) |
| sign | own | Gen 3:13–5:2: 3. Jer 13–14: 3 | 2 (1 Thess 2:15–5:3; Gal 3–4; Mark 12:22–13:17; Matt 1:1–2:15; Matt 23:19–24:19) |
| sign | own and loosened | Gen 3:13–5:2: 4. Jer 8–10: 4. Ps 1–5, Isa 64:3–66:7, Dan 5:27–7:7: 3 | **Matt 1:1–2:15: 4. Matt 23:29–24:29: 4.** Then 3 (1 Thess 2:15–5:3; John 11:49–12:31; John 14:2–15:10; Mark 12:22–13:17) |

And inside each Gospel, with a window the length of its cross and tomb: Matthew's cross and tomb hold 6 of the witnesses' members and the next best stretch of Matthew holds 3; Mark's 6, the next 3; Luke's 4, the next 3; John's 2, and 251 of John's 835 windows hold as many. The sign puts nothing at any cross or tomb.

Read. (a) The witnesses' passage has no home in the Septuagint. Its ancestors are scattered: no forty verses of the Greek scriptures hold more than four of its members. The place in the seated literature where most of its members stand together is the cross and tomb of Mark and of Matthew. That holds on either direction, and it is the first measure in this file that sets the passion beside the Septuagint as a neighbour of Revelation 11 and finds the passion nearer. (b) The joint lands nowhere. (c) The sign does not touch the passion at all. It lands on Matthew's birth narrative and on the Synoptic apocalypse, and by loosened forms on John 12 and John 14–16. So the third statement of 2L.1, that chapter 12 owes the Gospels nothing, is true of the cross and not shown of the rest.

Limits. The members were entered by one reader. Ten of the witnesses' were known from 2H before this run, and the alternate forms of "three days" were written with the Gospels' datives in view. The loosened forms are by reading. The caps on commonness differ for the two corpora because their sizes do.

2L.4. **The architecture.** What the passion members stand in, function by function, each antecedent read in the seat.

| Function | Revelation 11 | Antecedent, seated | In the Gospels |
|---|---|---|---|
| a space measured, a court left to the nations | 11:1–2 κάλαμος, μέτρησον; ἐδόθη τοῖς ἔθνεσιν, πατήσουσιν | Ezek 40:3 κάλαμος μέτρον | Luke 21:24 Ἰερουσαλὴμ ἔσται πατουμένη ὑπὸ ἐθνῶν (sense; another form of the verb) |
| a time bounded | 42 months; 1,260 days; three days and a half | Dan 12:7 OG εἰς καιρὸν καὶ καιροὺς καὶ ἥμισυ καιροῦ; 7:25 | none by the number; see 2L.7 for Elijah's years |
| two, as witnesses | 11:3 δυσὶν μάρτυσιν | Deut 17:6, 19:15 | Matt 18:16 (the rule) |
| two, as olive trees before the Lord of the earth | 11:4 | Zech 4:3 δύο ἐλαῖαι; 4:14 παρεστήκασιν κυρίῳ πάσης τῆς γῆς | none |
| fire that devours | 11:5 | 4 Kgdms 1:10 (Elijah) | none (Luke 9:54 is the request refused; not searched as a member) |
| heaven shut, no rain | 11:6 | 3 Kgdms 17:1 (Elijah); 8:35 | Luke 4:25 ἐκλείσθη ὁ οὐρανός |
| water to blood, the earth struck | 11:6 | Exod 7:17–20; Ps 104:29; Mal 4:5 πατάξω τὴν γῆν (the seat's line is corrupt with apparatus; the phrase is found in it) | none |
| war made on them by the beast | 11:7 ποιήσει μετ' αὐτῶν πόλεμον | Dan 7:21 Θ ἐποίει πόλεμον μετὰ τῶν ἁγίων | none |
| bodies exposed, no burial | 11:8–9 | Ps 78:2–3 … κύκλῳ Ἰερουσαλήμ, καὶ οὐκ ἦν ὁ θάπτων | the contrary: he is laid in a tomb |
| the gloating, gifts sent | 11:10 | Esth 9:19 ἀποστέλλοντες μερίδας (sense; the seat's line is corrupt) | John 16:20 ὁ δὲ κόσμος χαρήσεται (sense) |
| breath enters, they stand | 11:11 πνεῦμα ζωῆς … ἔστησαν ἐπὶ τοὺς πόδας αὐτῶν | Ezek 37:5, 37:10 | none: the Gospels say ἠγέρθη |
| taken up in the cloud | 11:12 | 4 Kgdms 2:11 ἀνελήμφθη Ἠλειοὺ ὡς εἰς τὸν οὐρανόν | Acts 1:9 νεφέλη ὑπέλαβεν αὐτόν |
| the earthquake | 11:13 σεισμὸς μέγας | Ezek 38:19 σεισμὸς μέγας; 37:7 σεισμός | Matt 27:51, 28:2 |
| seven thousand | 11:13 | 3 Kgdms 19:18 (Elijah; the seven thousand spared) | Rom 11:4 |

Every function has a Septuagint antecedent, and they are four: Elijah's cycle (fire, drought, ascent, the seven thousand, and Malachi's Elijah), Zechariah's two, Ezekiel 37–38, Daniel's time and war. So the frame of the passage is not from the passion on any account. What lies inside the frame and is shared with the passion is the run from killing to rising: a body, a city, three days, a tomb, watchers, a voice, fear, an earthquake.

2L.5. **Ezekiel 37–38 stands under both.** Rev 11:11–13 has from Ezekiel the breath of life (37:5), the standing on the feet (37:10), the earthquake (37:7; 38:19, great). Matt 27:51–53 has from Ezekiel what Revelation does not: τὰ μνημεῖα ἀνεῴχθησαν, the tombs opened (37:12 ἀνοίγω ὑμῶν τὰ μνήματα, in no other book of the Septuagint and at no other verse of the New Testament), the dead brought out, and their entry (37:12 εἰσάξω ὑμᾶς εἰς τὴν γῆν; Matthew, εἰς τὴν ἁγίαν πόλιν). Each has a part of Ezekiel's scene that the other lacks. That is the shape of 2F.3 again: siblings on a third text. What Matthew and Revelation share here that Ezekiel does not give is the holy city as the place, and the earthquake set at a death and a rising of the Lord's own.

2L.6. **11:8, apart.** ὅπου καὶ ὁ κύριος αὐτῶν ἐσταυρώθη. In the seated New Testament "Lord" and "crucify" stand in one verse three times: here, 1 Cor 2:8 (τὸν κύριον τῆς δόξης ἐσταύρωσαν) and Gal 6:14. No Gospel verse has the two together. So the clause supposes what Paul's sentence supposes, a Lord crucified, and no more: no governor, no hill, no named place, no burial. The place it gives is its own and is against the others: on the street of the great city. John has it near the city (19:20 ἐγγὺς … τῆς πόλεως); Hebrews has it outside the gate (13:12); Revelation itself treads the winepress outside the city (14:20). On the received dates Paul's sentence is before every Gospel, so the clause's anteriority to the Gospels is available on either account, and it is not evidence of a Gospel read. It is the one member of the passage that needs a crucifixion told somewhere, and the only wording it shares is John's ὅπου ἐσταυρώθη.

2L.7. **Four forms that are not the Septuagint's.** To be read, not counted.

a. *After three days.* Rev 11:11 μετὰ τὰς τρεῖς ἡμέρας καὶ ἥμισυ. In the New Testament μετὰ τρεῖς ἡμέρας of a rising stands at Mark 8:31, 9:31, 10:34 and at Matt 27:63 in the mouth of the priests; Matthew and Luke elsewhere say τῇ τρίτῃ ἡμέρᾳ, with 1 Cor 15:4 and Acts 10:40. So Revelation's interval has Mark's preposition. In Revelation the count has a reason, the half of seven that governs the whole passage. In Mark it is the form the others replace.

b. *Three years and six months.* Rev 11:3, 6: the two shut the heaven for the days of their prophecy, 1,260. Luke 4:25: ἐκλείσθη ὁ οὐρανὸς ἐπὶ ἔτη τρία καὶ μῆνας ἕξ. Jas 5:17: ἐνιαυτοὺς τρεῖς καὶ μῆνας ἕξ. The Septuagint has no such figure: 3 Kgdms 18:1 has the rain return "in the third year", and the phrase is in no verse of the seat. So Elijah's drought lasts Daniel's half-week in three texts, Revelation, Luke and James, and in Kings it does not. The usual account is a tradition about Elijah outside scripture (from memory; not seated). In Revelation the figure is made, from Daniel, in the passage itself; in Luke and James it is given as known. James is the letter the programme named.

c. *The kingdoms of the world.* Rev 11:15 ἐγένετο ἡ βασιλεία τοῦ κόσμου τοῦ κυρίου ἡμῶν. Matt 4:8: the devil shows πάσας τὰς βασιλείας τοῦ κόσμου. Nowhere else in the New Testament or the Septuagint; Luke 4:5 has τῆς οἰκουμένης. The same kingdom is offered by the devil in Matthew and announced as the Lord's at the seventh trumpet.

d. *A woman, when she bears.* Rev 12:4 τῆς γυναικὸς τῆς μελλούσης τεκεῖν, ἵνα ὅταν τέκῃ. John 16:21 ἡ γυνὴ ὅταν τίκτῃ λύπην ἔχει, ὅτι ἦλθεν ἡ ὥρα αὐτῆς. The only two in the New Testament. The verse before it in John (16:20) is ὁ δὲ κόσμος χαρήσεται, the world will rejoice while you mourn, which is Rev 11:10. So John 16:20–21 has, in the order of Revelation, the earth's rejoicing over the dead and the woman in labour: the end of the witnesses and the opening of the sign, in two consecutive verses.

2L.8. **The joint, 11:14–19.** By the census it is the thinnest of the three: fifteen of its 23 members are Revelation's alone, and it lands nowhere. What it has outside itself is Psalm 2 (11:15 τοῦ κυρίου … καὶ τοῦ χριστοῦ αὐτοῦ, Ps 2:2; 11:18 the nations raging, Ps 2:1 in sense; Acts 4:25–26 quotes both), and Psalm 2 returns in the sign (12:5 the rod of iron, Ps 2:9). The ark seen in the opened temple has one New Testament neighbour, Heb 9:4. The temple opened (ἠνοίγη ὁ ναός) stands where the Synoptics have the veil of the temple torn; the wording is not shared.

And by vocabulary the two chapters are less bound than neighbours in this book usually are. Word-stems shared by 11:1–13 and 12:1–17 and found in no more than fourteen of Revelation's verses: eight. The same count at every other position in the book, for stretches of the same lengths: mean 12.4; 326 of 369 positions have as many. So the reviews' junction is a seam, and the received reading's second statement holds. What crosses it is specific: the number (1,260 days at 11:3 and 12:6; the half at 11:9, 11 and 12:14), the rod (11:1; 12:5), war made (11:7; 12:7, 17), rejoicing turned (11:10 the earth's; 12:12 the heavens'), and across the joint the raging (11:18 τὰ ἔθνη ὠργίσθησαν; 12:17 ὠργίσθη ὁ δράκων: the stem's only two verses in the book) and "of his Christ" (11:15; 12:10). And one thing found on checking a sentence of this section, which the members as first entered did not catch. A place prepared, τόπος with ἑτοιμάζω, stands twice in the Septuagint, both of one thing: 1 Chr 15:1 ἐποίησεν αὐτῷ οἰκίας ἐν πόλει Δαυείδ, καὶ ἡτοίμασεν τὸν τόπον τῇ κιβωτῷ τοῦ θεοῦ, and 15:3, the place David prepared for the ark. Rev 11:19: the ark is seen. Rev 12:1: the woman. Rev 12:6: she has τόπον ἡτοιμασμένον ἀπὸ τοῦ θεοῦ. So the place prepared for the ark in Chronicles is, six verses after the ark is seen, the place prepared for the woman. That is a hinge across the seam, made of one Septuagint verse, and it is the answer the run gives to the review's question of what the ark prepares. John 14:2 has the same verse's two words, οἰκία and ἑτοιμάσαι τόπον, so John may stand to Chronicles directly, as a sibling again. One thing more crosses it that the count cannot see: the witnesses go up to heaven in the cloud (11:12) and the child is snatched up to God (12:5), a death and rising and then a birth, each ending in an ascent.

2L.9. **The sign, 12:1–17.** Against #1495: the sources that record fixed before its test come up here by machine. Gen 37:9 (the stars, loosened form), Isa 66:7 (the male), Ps 2:9 (the rod of iron), Dan 7:7 (the ten horns), and with them Gen 3:15 (her seed), Exod 19:4 (eagles' wings), Num 16:32 (the earth's mouth), Dan 12:7 OG (time, times, half). And βασανιζομένη, the torment #1495 found set back into Isaiah's verse, is in the class *Revelation only*: no Septuagint verse and no New Testament verse has it of a woman bearing. The two instruments agree where they overlap.

Where the sign lands in the Gospels:

- *Matthew 1–2.* ἐν γαστρὶ ἔχουσα (1:18, 1:23); ἔτεκεν υἱόν (1:25); ποιμανεῖ (2:6; Rev 12:5 ποιμαίνειν); φεῦγε εἰς Αἴγυπτον (2:13; Rev 12:6 ἔφυγεν εἰς τὴν ἔρημον), with the ruler who seeks the child to destroy it. Every member is also Septuagintal (Isa 7:14; 2 Kgdms 5:2; Exod 1–2 for the child sought and the flight), so the Septuagint suffices for Matthew here and for Revelation here; the two need not have met.
- *Matthew 24 and Mark 13.* The birth-pangs (24:8), her that is with child (24:19), flee (24:16), the stars fall (24:29), the sign in heaven and the great power (24:30), the trumpet (24:31). Rev 11:15–12:6 has these in a run: trumpet, great power, sign in heaven, with child, pangs, stars cast down, flight. The mourning tribes of 2.1 stand in the same verse, Matt 24:30.
- *John 12:25–32.* ὁ φιλῶν τὴν ψυχὴν αὐτοῦ (12:25; Rev 12:11 οὐκ ἠγάπησαν τὴν ψυχὴν αὐτῶν); a voice from heaven, taken for thunder or an angel (12:28–29; Rev 12:10 φωνὴν μεγάλην ἐν τῷ οὐρανῷ); νῦν … νῦν ὁ ἄρχων τοῦ κόσμου τούτου ἐκβληθήσεται ἔξω (12:31; Rev 12:9–10 ἐβλήθη … ἄρτι ἐγένετο). Seven verses of John with four members of Rev 12:9–11. None is a shared form; all are by sense, and are entered as loosened.
- *John 14–16.* ἑτοιμάσαι τόπον ὑμῖν (14:2–3; Rev 12:6 τόπον ἡτοιμασμένον; the phrase is in these three verses of the New Testament and, in the Septuagint, at 1 Chr 15:1 and 15:3 only, on which see 2L.8); keeping the commandments (15:10; Rev 12:17); and 16:20–21 (2L.7 d).
- *Luke.* 10:18 ἐθεώρουν τὸν Σατανᾶν … ἐκ τοῦ οὐρανοῦ πεσόντα, the verse before the locusts' authority of 2J.6 b; 1:33 βασιλεύσει … εἰς τοὺς αἰῶνας (Rev 11:15).
- *1 Thessalonians 4:16–5:3.* The trumpet, ἁρπαγησόμεθα ἐν νεφέλαις (4:17; Rev 12:5 ἡρπάσθη, 11:12 ἐν τῇ νεφέλῃ), ἡ ὠδὶν τῇ ἐν γαστρὶ ἐχούσῃ (5:3). A letter, and by received dates the earliest text in this list.

So the two chapters fall at the two ends of Matthew, the sign at his opening and the witnesses at his close; in the Synoptic apocalypse; and in John's last discourses, where they are sayings.

2L.10. **The two genealogies, row by row.** What each must suppose that is not in a seated text.

| | Revelation first | Revelation last |
|---|---|---|
| The frame (2L.4) | from the Septuagint | from the Septuagint |
| The crucified Lord (2L.6) | the datum as 1 Cor 2:8 has it; nothing further | the same, or John 19:20 |
| The run from killing to rising | made here from Ps 78, Ezek 37–38 and the datum; nothing supposed | from the passion as told |
| That its members stand together at the Gospels' cross and tomb (2L.3) | four writers went to these verses for that scene and kept different parts; or Mark did and the others, with Mark open, did again | the seer had the passion in the forms of Mark (πτῶμα, after three days), Matthew (the holy city, the great earthquake), Luke (ἔμφοβοι) and John (where crucified): four books, or one passion narrative not extant that had them all |
| What is not carried | the Gospels take the frame's contents nowhere: no olive trees, no beast, no number. They leave Ezekiel's standing for ἠγέρθη, and the watchers change sides | the seer, with the passion open, takes no cross, no governor, no burial (he denies one), no women, no stone, and writes Ezekiel's standing for the Gospels' raising |
| Elijah's three years and six months (2L.7 b) | made here from Daniel; Luke and James receive it | a tradition about Elijah not in the seat, known to Luke and James; Revelation reaches the same figure from Daniel |
| The sign and Matthew 1–2 | Matthew tells the sign as a birth | no relation needed: both from the Septuagint |
| The sign and John 12–16 | John turns 11:10 and 12:2–11 into sayings | one school's idiom |

Neither column supposes much. The right column supposes one thing the left does not: either four Gospels on the seer's desk or a lost passion narrative that held what the four hold severally. The left column supposes one thing the right does not: that four writers, or one and then three, drew on thirteen verses of a vision for the telling of a death. Each has to explain an absence, and the absences are of the same size. That is the state 2D.5 reached by counting, now reached for one whole passage by reading it through; the count of what must be supposed does not separate them. The author's argument at 2E is about the shape of the whole and is not touched by this.

2L.11. **What the run adds that stands on either account.**

1. Revelation 11:1–13 is nearer to the passion narratives than to any passage of the Greek scriptures, by the number of its members standing together (2L.3). The frame is Septuagintal and scattered; the death is the passion's neighbour.
2. Its frame is Elijah's, Zechariah's, Ezekiel's and Daniel's, entire (2L.4). Nothing in the frame is from the Gospels and nothing in the frame goes to them.
3. Matthew and Revelation are siblings on Ezekiel 37 at the death (2L.5), as Hebrews, John and Revelation were on Philo (2F.3).
4. 11:8 needs no Gospel (2L.6).
5. Chapters 11 and 12 are a seam by vocabulary and are crossed by a few exact things, one of them the ark's prepared place become the woman's (2L.8).
6. Chapter 12 puts nothing at the cross; it lies along Matthew 1–2, Matthew 24 and John 12–16 (2L.9).
7. Three forms shared outside the Septuagint that were not in the file: Mark's "after three days", the three years and six months of Luke and James, Matthew's kingdoms of the world; and John 16:20–21 holding the end of chapter 11 and the opening of chapter 12 in consecutive verses (2L.7).

Seat defects met and not repaired here: Ezek 37:5 reads πνεῖμα for πνεῦμα; Ezek 37:10, Mal 4:5 and Esth 9:19 carry apparatus inside the line. They are added to the list at 2B.6.

## 2M. The other way; the shared members as relations; the order

2M.0. The author, 15:09: "lets run it the other way, build as relations, and ordered run". The three operations owed from 2L. Scripts: `rev1112/mark_members.py`, `other_way.py`, `rel1112.py`, `ordered.py`.

2M.1. **The passion read the other way.** Every member of Mark 15:21–16:8, entered verse by verse from Mark: 81, each found in its own verse. Each searched in Revelation and in the Septuagint, by its own form and apart by a loosened one.

| By its own form, Mark's member is found | |
|---|---|
| in Revelation and in the Septuagint | 7 |
| in Revelation, not in the Septuagint | 1 (clothed in a white robe: Mark 16:5; Rev 7:9) |
| in the Septuagint, not in Revelation | 27 |
| in neither | 46 |

So 73 of Mark's 81 members are nowhere in Revelation. Simon, Golgotha, the garments and the lot, the robbers, the mockery, the hours, "my God, my God", the sponge and vinegar, the veil, the centurion, Joseph, Pilate, the linen, the stone, the young man, ἠγέρθη, Galilee: none. Set beside 2L.2: 34 of the 55 members of Rev 11:1–13 are in no Gospel. The residue is large from both ends, and larger from Mark's.

The eight that are in Revelation: a great voice (nineteen verses; 11:12), the reed (11:1), from afar (18:10, 15, 17), watching (11:11, 12, and nowhere else in the book), πτῶμα (11:8, 9, and nowhere else), μνῆμα (11:9, and nowhere else), the white robe (7:9), and πετρ-, which is rocks. Five of the eight are in 11:1–13, and three of those five are words Revelation uses there only.

Where Mark's members stand together in Revelation, by a window of thirteen verses, the length of 11:1–13:

| forms | 11:1–13 holds | next best stretch of Revelation | windows of 393 holding as many |
|---|---|---|---|
| own | 4 (corpse, μνῆμα, reed, watching) | 2 (6:14–7:9) | 3, all overlapping it |
| own and loosened | 7 (those, and crucified, three days, laid in a tomb) | 2 | 3, all overlapping it |

And against the Septuagint, forty verses each:

| forms | best in Revelation | best in the Septuagint |
|---|---|---|
| own | 4 (9:4–11:11) | 4 (Judith 13:12–15:12: corpse, Galilee, a great voice, trembling); then 3 |
| own and loosened | 8 (9:18–12:6) | 5 (Exod 14:7–15:15, by common words); 4 at **Ps 19:4–21:19: the lot, the garments divided, forsaken, the heads wagged**; 4 at four other places |

Read. From Mark's end the answer is the same as from Revelation's (2L.3) and sharper. Mark's cross and tomb have one place in Revelation, the witnesses, and no second. And Revelation 11 holds as many of Mark's members as any stretch of the Septuagint, Psalm 21 included, which is the passion's acknowledged scripture: four against four by Mark's own forms, eight against four or five with the loosened. So each is the other's nearest neighbour in the seated literature. A limit on the eight: two of the loosened forms, "laid in a tomb" and "three days", were written with Rev 11 in view; without them it is six.

One thing from Mark's residue, to be read. Mark 15:35–36: "he calls Elijah" … a sponge on a reed … "let us see if Elijah comes to take him down." Elijah's name is in no verse of Revelation. Rev 11 has the reed (11:1) and Elijah's works (the fire, the shut heaven, the ascent, the seven thousand) and not his name; Mark has the reed and his name and, at the cross, none of his works. He is expected at Mark's cross and does not come. In Revelation 11 the ones with his powers are the ones who die.

2M.2. **The shared members as relations.** The form and the rules of 2J, unchanged; 37 pairs, Revelation's clause and the clause of the Gospel that holds the member (two are from Acts and 1 Thessalonians). Every form found in the seat. The differences computed:

| Difference | these 37 | the 34 loci of 2J |
|---|---|---|
| agent changed, side kept | 0.51 | 0.47 |
| agent changed, side reversed | 0.11 | 0.15 |
| action changed | 0.38 | 0.21 |
| object changed | 0.22 | 0.26 |
| object's party changed | 0.14 | 0.09 |
| recipient changed | 0.16 | 0.35 |
| negative removed | 0.08 | 0.09 |
| negative set | 0.00 | 0.09 |
| mode changed | 0.49 | 0.44 |

The two sets were entered apart, the second after the first had been run, and their profiles lie close: the agent changes in about three pairs of five, the side is reversed in about one of eight, the negative is removed in about one of twelve. That is one reader twice, so it shows a steady hand at least; whether it shows a steady grammar needs the second reader of 2K.2.

What the pairs say, read:

a. *The witnesses' deeds become Jesus's, one for one.* They stand after the three days and a half; "after three days I am raised" (Matt 27:63). They go up into heaven in the cloud; he is lifted and a cloud takes him (Acts 1:9). Their πτῶμα is refused a μνῆμα; his πτῶμα is laid in a μνῆμα (Mark 15:45–46). In each the action or its object is kept and the agent passes from the two to the one. Computed: agent changed within a side, in eleven of the scene's nineteen pairs.

b. *The tomb is an undoing.* οὐκ ἀφίουσιν τεθῆναι εἰς μνῆμα against κατέθηκεν αὐτὸν ἐν μνήματι: same verb, same noun, the negative removed, and the one who acts changes from the peoples to a friend. Mark and Luke both; computed UNDO in both. This is the operator John was found to use on the plagues (2A), here in Mark.

c. *The watchers change sides.* ἐθεώρησαν αὐτοὺς οἱ ἐχθροὶ αὐτῶν; in Mark and Matthew γυναῖκες … θεωροῦσαι, in John Peter θεωρεῖ. Same verb; enemies become the women and the disciple. Computed REVERSE-DOER in Mark, Matthew and John. Luke alone keeps a neutral watcher (ὁ λαὸς θεωρῶν, 23:35).

d. *The holy city.* The nations will trample it (Rev 11:2); the holy ones, raised, enter it (Matt 27:53). The object is kept and both agent and deed are turned.

e. *What the hostile or the neutral do in the vision, the centurion does.* Great fear falls on the watchers; the centurion and those with him fear greatly (Matt 27:54). The rest give glory to God; the centurion glorified God (Luke 23:47). A figure Revelation does not have receives two of its members in two Gospels.

f. *The voice turns round.* A great voice out of heaven speaks to them; Jesus cries with a great voice to God (Mark 15:34; Luke 23:46). Speaker and addressee both change, and the direction is from below.

g. *The sign's members change mode.* Of the fifteen pairs from chapter 12, twelve change mode: what is seen in the vision is foretold (Matt 24), commanded (Matt 2:13; 24:16), promised (John 14:2), made a likeness (John 16:21) or cited (Matt 2:6). Matt 1:18 and 1:25 alone tell it as an event.

2M.3. **The order.** Every member of a stretch of Revelation that has a hit in a stretch of a Gospel, placed at its first verse there; Kendall's tau between Revelation's order and the Gospel's; the orders shuffled 20,000 times. No member chosen by hand.

| Revelation | Gospel | forms | placed | tau | p |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 11:1–13 | Matthew's cross and tomb | own | 6 | +0.33 | 0.46 |
| 11:1–13 | Mark's cross and tomb | own | 6 | −0.07 | 1.00 |
| 11:1–13 | Luke's cross and tomb | own | 4 | +0.33 | 0.75 |
| 11:1–13 | each, with loosened | | 8, 7, 6 | +0.36, 0.00, 0.00 | 0.28, 1.00, 1.00 |
| 11:14–12:17 | Matthew 24 | own and loosened | 6 | −0.60 | 0.14 |
| 11:14–12:17 | Mark 13 | own and loosened | 4 | −0.67 | 0.34 |
| 11:10–12:17 | John 12:20–16:33 | own and loosened | 7 | −0.40 | 0.30 |
| 12 | Matthew 1–2 | own and loosened | 4 | +1.00 | 0.08 |
| 11:1–12:17 | the whole of Matthew | own | 24 | −0.03 | 0.84 |

By their own forms alone the stretches outside the passion place too few members to test (two each).

Read. No order is kept and none is shown reversed. At the cross and tomb the Gospels have the witnesses' members and not their sequence: Revelation goes city, body, three days, tomb, breath, fear, watchers, voice, earthquake; Mark goes three days, voice, reed, watchers, body, tomb. So the scene was not followed; its members were used. Across the whole of Matthew there is no order at all, so the remark at 2L.9 that the two chapters fall at Matthew's two ends describes four birth-members and the passion, and is not an arrangement of the book.

Matthew 24 is the one place that leans, and it leans backwards: Revelation has trumpet, great power, with child, pangs, stars, flight; Matthew has pangs (24:8), flight (24:16), with child (24:19), stars (24:29), great power (24:30), trumpet (24:31). As run, tau −0.60, p = 0.14, which is not a result. The sign in heaven of Matt 24:30, noted at 2L.9 before this run, was missed by the pattern as written (it allowed 24 letters between the two words and the verse has 27). With it, seven members, tau −0.70, p = 0.04. That figure comes from a pattern widened after the run and is entered as such: a lean to be tested on a stretch not yet read, and not a finding. Mark 13 has four of the seven and the same lean. The three Matthew has beyond Mark here, the sign in heaven, the mourning tribes and the trumpet, are Rev 12:1, 1:7 and 11:15; that agrees with 2D.3 and the Septuagint has the trumpet too (Isa 27:13, from memory; not checked in the seat).

2M.4. **Where this leaves the passage.**

1. Revelation 11:1–13 and the passion are each other's nearest neighbour, measured from both ends, and nearer than Psalm 21 is to Mark by the same count. Standing on either account.
2. The residue is most of both: 34 of 55 from Revelation's side, 73 of 81 from Mark's. Whichever was written second took little and left nearly everything, so the relation is of a few members used, not of a text followed; the order confirms it.
3. Read as relations, the shared members differ in a regular way: the deed or its object is kept and the doer changes. The two become the one; the enemies become the friends; the refusal becomes the gift. That is a description of the difference and holds whichever way it ran. Run forward, it is a vision's figures given to a life. Run backward, it is a life's last scene given to two figures and turned against the watchers.
4. The order test is null. Nothing here decides direction, and 2L.10 stands as written.

## 2N. The lean tested; order by machine; the four passions by machine

2N.0. The author, 15:21: "lets proceed". The two operations entered at the end of 2M, and one added because the first needed it. Scripts: `order/members20.py` (hashed before the search), `order/proceed.py`.

2N.1. **The backward lean of Matthew 24, tested where it had not been read.** Rev 20:11–15 against Matt 25:31–46, as entered in the list. Sixteen members from Revelation, own form and loosened, the file hashed before Matthew was searched. (Two own forms failed in Revelation's own verses and were corrected before the search: the gap in "the book … of life", and ᾅδης, which the seat spells with its iota. Final hash in `members20.sha256`.) The reader knows Matthew 25 from memory; the freeze guards against tuning and not against knowledge. Prediction, from 2M.3: tau below zero.

| forms | placed | tau | p |
|---|---|---|---|
| own | 0 | | |
| own and loosened | 8 | **+0.76** (22 pairs with, 3 against) | 0.016 |

The prediction failed, and by a margin. The eight (throne and the one seated, 25:31; before him, 25:32; the least, 25:40; what you did, 25:40; into the fire, 25:41; eternal punishment and eternal life, 25:46) stand in Revelation's order, not its reverse. So Matthew does not have a habit of turning Revelation's sequence, and the lean at Matthew 24 is one stretch, widened after the run, and is withdrawn as a lead.

What the forward order here is worth: little. No member is shared by its own form; all eight are by sense. And a judgment has an order of its own, the seat before the sentence; any two judgment scenes will agree in it. It is entered as the failure of a prediction and not as a finding for order kept.

2N.2. **Order by machine.** To ask the question of the whole and without a reader: every chapter of Revelation against every chapter of each Gospel; the word-stems the two share that stand in no more than forty verses of the New Testament; each placed at its first word in each chapter; Kendall's tau and its z. If the Gospels keep or turn Revelation's order anywhere, the z's will be off centre or too widely spread.

Chapter pairs with eight or more such stems: 111. Mean z +0.01; spread 0.91, where 1 is what no order gives; beyond 1.96 either way: 2, where chance gives 5 or 6. The most kept is Rev 21 with Matt 26 (z 2.2), the most turned Rev 16 with Matt 24 (z −1.7), and neither is outside what 111 draws give. Rev 11 with Matt 24: +0.24 on ten stems. Rev 11 with Matt 27 and with Mark 15: +0.29 and +0.21 on eight.

So there is no order relation between Revelation and the Gospels at the scale of a chapter, kept or turned, by this measure. With 2M.3 that closes the question as far as these instruments reach: **the Gospels and Revelation share members and do not share sequence.** Whichever used the other used it as a store and not as an outline.

2N.3. **The four cross-and-tomb narratives, by machine.** 2M.1 entered Mark's by hand. To set the four on equal terms no hand is used: every word-stem of the stretch that is rare in the New Testament is a member; those found in Revelation at all (in twelve verses or fewer) are searched by window of thirteen verses. The control is the same Gospel: some 290 other stretches of the same length, and how much of each stands at Rev 11:1–13.

| Cross and tomb of | rare stems | in Revelation | at 11:1–13 | best window in Revelation | windows of 393 with as many as 11:1–13 | other stretches of the same Gospel with as large a share at 11:1–13 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Matthew | 174 | 58 | **13** | 13, at 11:1 | **1** | **none** (mean there 6.1) |
| Mark | 154 | 45 | 9 | 10, at 11:8 | 11 | 0.10 (mean 5.2) |
| Luke | 159 | 50 | 6 | 10, at 16:10 | 48 | 0.56 (mean 6.8) |
| John | 154 | 54 | 6 | 8, at 7:1 | 73 | 0.30 (mean 3.9) |

Read. With no reader in it, the relation of 2L and 2M is **Matthew's**. Of everything in Revelation, the thirteen verses of the witnesses are where Matthew's cross and tomb stand most, alone at the top; and no other stretch of Matthew puts as much of itself there. Mark's does so weakly (one stretch in ten does as well). Luke's and John's do not: their cross and tomb are no nearer Rev 11 than the rest of their books are. So the four-way division of 2H.2 and 2I.1 was right about what each Gospel holds and wrong in its weight. Luke's part (ἔμφοβοι, the glory) and John's (where he was crucified, the watching) are a few members a reader found; they are there, and a machine counting everything does not see them above the level of the book. Matthew's part is seen by any count. This is the third measure in the file to single out Matthew (2C.2; 2D.3) and the first to do it for one scene.

A limit: stems of five letters are coarse, and the thirteen include weak ones (ἔδωκα-, μέγας, πόδας). The control absorbs that, since the same coarseness is in every other stretch.

2N.4. **Where Matthew's own part stands: a knot the reading had not found.** The stems of Matthew's cross and tomb that are not in Mark's, 35 of them in Revelation. Their three best windows:

| Revelation | holds | stems |
|---|---|---|
| 20:2–14 | 10 | holy ones, seized, upon, white, the rest, great, dead, deceive, first, gave |
| 1:17–2:8 | 8 | laid, last, dead, feet, first, fear, rich, left |
| 11:11–19 | 8 | lightning, stood, the rest, feet, earthquake, three, fear, gave |

The second and third are in the file: Matthew's tomb from Rev 1:14–17 (2B.3) and the witnesses. The first is new. Read in the seat, it is the guard at the tomb, which is Matthew's alone (27:62–66; 28:2–4, 11–15):

| | Revelation 20:1–6 | Matthew 27:62–28:9 |
|---|---|---|
| an angel coming down from heaven | 20:1 ἄγγελον καταβαίνοντα ἐκ τοῦ οὐρανοῦ | 28:2 ἄγγελος γὰρ κυρίου καταβὰς ἐξ οὐρανοῦ. In the New Testament only here and Rev 10:1, 18:1, 20:1 |
| sealed | 20:3 ἔκλεισεν καὶ ἐσφράγισεν ἐπάνω αὐτοῦ | 27:66 σφραγίσαντες τὸν λίθον. The verb's only place in the Synoptics |
| upon him, upon it | 20:3 ἐπάνω αὐτοῦ | 28:2 ἐκάθητο ἐπάνω αὐτοῦ. Eight verses of the New Testament have it |
| the deceiver | 20:3 ἵνα μὴ πλανήσῃ ἔτι τὰ ἔθνη; 20:10 ὁ πλανῶν | 27:63 ἐκεῖνος ὁ πλάνος; 27:64 ἡ ἐσχάτη πλάνη |
| the first | 20:5 ἡ ἀνάστασις ἡ πρώτη | 27:64 χείρων τῆς πρώτης |
| the dead | 20:5 οἱ λοιποὶ τῶν νεκρῶν οὐκ ἔζησαν | 27:64 ἠγέρθη ἀπὸ τῶν νεκρῶν; 28:4 ὡς νεκροί |
| seized | 20:2 ἐκράτησεν τὸν δράκοντα | 28:9 ἐκράτησαν αὐτοῦ τοὺς πόδας |
| the holy ones | 20:6 μακάριος καὶ ἅγιος; 20:9 | 27:52 σώματα τῶν κεκοιμημένων ἁγίων |

The received reading, first: Matthew's guard answers the charge that the disciples stole the body (28:13–15 says so), and its seal is Daniel's. Dan 6:17 in the seat, both versions: a stone is brought and set on the mouth of the pit, καὶ ἐσφραγίσατο ὁ βασιλεύς. That gives the stone and the seal. It does not give the deceiver, the angel coming down from heaven, ἐπάνω αὐτοῦ, or the first and the last.

As relations (2J's form, not yet run in the engine): in Revelation an angel comes down from heaven, seizes the deceiver, and seals over him, so that he deceive no more; then the first resurrection. In Matthew the priests name Jesus the deceiver and seal over him, so that the last deception be not worse than the first; then an angel comes down from heaven, undoes it, and sits on the stone. The deed and its words are kept. The one who seals changes from the angel to the adversaries (the doer's side reversed), the one sealed from the dragon to Jesus (the object's party reversed), and the seal holds in the one and is broken in the other (undone). Three differences at once, and all three are of the kinds 2M.2 found at the cross: the doer changes, the side turns, the refusal is undone.

On either account it is exact. Revelation first: Matthew tells the sealing of the deceiver as a slander enacted, the priests doing to Jesus what the angel does to Satan, and the angel answering. Revelation last: the seer takes the tomb's seal and the priests' word and gives them their proper object. Neither is shown. What is shown is that a search with no reader in it, asked only where Matthew's additions to Mark's passion stand in Revelation, returned three places, two of which this file had already found by reading and the third of which holds up line by line. (Limited at 2O.3: that step of the search had no control, and with one, under a stricter rule, the guard block does not stand out. The knot rests on the lines above and on the engine's run at 2O.1.)

2N.5. **Where the file stands after these.**

1. No order is kept or turned between Revelation and the Gospels (2M.3; 2N.1; 2N.2). The relation is of members.
2. At the cross and tomb the relation to Revelation 11 is Matthew's, weakly Mark's, and not Luke's or John's above their books' level (2N.3). The four-way division stands as a description and loses its weight as evidence.
3. Matthew's own passion material stands at three places in Revelation: 1:17, 11:11–13, 20:1–6 (2N.4). The third is new and is the guard at the tomb.
4. Direction: not decided; nothing in this section bears on it.

## 2O. The guard in the engine; Matthew's own blocks searched

2O.0. The author, 15:28: "next up". The two operations entered at the end of 2N. Scripts: `engine/derivations.py` (one derivation added), `relations/guard.py`, `order/blocks.py`.

2O.1. **The seal on the deceiver, in the engine.** `rev20.1-5→matt27.63-28.9`. Seed Rev 20:1–3 with 20:5, eleven members. One PARTITION: Matthew keeps seven (an angel coming down from heaven, seized, sealed, over him, deceive, the dead, the first) and drops four (the key of the abyss, the chain, bound, the thousand years). Unordered, as a scene. Target Matt 27:63–66 with 28:2 and 28:9.

| | kept members present | dropped absent | score |
|---|---|---|---|
| Matthew's guard and tomb | 7 of 7 | 4 of 4 | 1.0 |
| Dan 6:17, Theodotion (the stone, the king's seal) | 1 of 7 (sealed) | 4 of 4 | 0.45 |
| Dan 6:17, Old Greek | 1 of 7 | 4 of 4 | 0.45 |
| Mark's tomb (15:46–16:8) | 0 of 7 | 4 of 4 | 0.36 |
| Luke's tomb (23:53–24:9) | 1 of 7 (the dead) | 4 of 4 | 0.45 |
| John's tomb (20:1–17) | 2 of 7 (the dead, the first) | 4 of 4 | 0.55 |

No verse of the New Testament outside Revelation and no verse of the Septuagint holds the seven. So Daniel gives Matthew the seal and nothing else of this; the other three tombs have nothing of it. The bench now runs fifty-three, and fifty-two pass with no control passing. The derivation was entered after the passage was read (2N.4), as all of them were; the controls carry the weight.

2O.2. **As relations** (the rules of 2J, unchanged; every form found in the seat).

| Relation | Revelation 20 | Matthew | computed |
|---|---|---|---|
| the sealing | the angel seals the deceiver (ἐσφράγισεν … ἵνα μὴ πλανήσῃ) | the chief priests seal "that deceiver" (σφραγίσαντες … ὁ πλάνος) | agent's side reversed; object's party changed: REVERSE-DOER |
| the descent | ἄγγελον καταβαίνοντα ἐκ τοῦ οὐρανοῦ | ἄγγελος … καταβὰς ἐξ οὐρανοῦ | nothing changed but the mode: EVENT |
| what the angel does at the closure | he shuts (ἔκλεισεν … ἐπάνω αὐτοῦ) | he rolls away, and sits ἐπάνω αὐτοῦ | action changed |
| the deceiving | that the dragon not deceive the nations | lest the disciples tell the people, and the last deception be worse | agent's side reversed; object changed: REVERSE-DOER |
| the seizing | the angel seized the dragon (ἐκράτησεν) | the women seized his feet (ἐκράτησαν) | agent changed within a side; object's party changed |

Two reversals of the doer's side in five relations, and in both the object passes from the adversary to Jesus. The rules have no operator for "the same agent does the opposite deed", which is what the third row is (shut against open); it comes out only as action changed. That is a gap in 2J.2 and is entered as one.

2O.3. **Matthew's own blocks, searched by machine.** The search of 2N.4 made general and given the control it lacked. For each of eighteen blocks of Matthew: the word-stems rare in the New Testament, in no verse of Mark, and in Revelation in twelve verses or fewer; where they stand together in Revelation (thirteen verses). Control: the same number of stems drawn at random, 400 times, from all of Matthew's such stems (171).

| Block | own stems in Revelation | most together | random draws do as well in |
|---|---|---|---|
| the birth (1:18–2:23) | 13 | 5, at Rev 7:17–8:12; 4 at Rev 12:5–17 | 0.13 |
| the Sermon, ch. 5; 6; 7 | 16; 14; 12 | 4; 5; 3 | 0.78; 0.17; 0.93 |
| the mission, ch. 10 | 14 | 3 | 0.97 |
| the parables of ch. 13 | 14 | 4, at Rev 18:13–19:1 (merchant, pearl, wheat) | 0.59 |
| the community, ch. 18 | 8 | 3 | 0.61 |
| the labourers (20:1–16) | 6 | 2 | 0.96 |
| the wedding (22:1–14) | 9 | 3, at Rev 19:7–19 (wedding, called, gathered) | 0.72 |
| the woes, ch. 23 | 16 | 5 | 0.25 |
| the discourse, ch. 24 | 21 | 6, at Rev 1:3–15 (mourn, all, tribes, trumpet) | 0.21 |
| the virgins; the talents | 7; 8 | 2; 2 | 0.99; 0.99 |
| the sheep and the goats | 6 | 3, at Rev 20:10–21:7 | 0.31 |
| the guard and the tomb (27:62–28:15) | 7 | 3 | 0.46 |
| the keys; Judas's end; the commission | 3; 2; 1 | too few | |

No block stands together in Revelation more than a random draw of Matthew's own stems does. Not one of the eighteen reaches one in twenty; the nearest is the birth at 0.13.

Read, in two parts.

a. *It is a null, and it corrects 2N.4.* That section said the search "returned three places". It did, but that step had no control, and its rule was looser: stems not in Mark's cross and tomb, where this run's rule is stems in no verse of Mark. Under the stricter rule and with a control, the guard block does not stand out (0.46). The words that carry the knot are lost to the rule: πλαν- and πρωτ- and νεκρ- are in Mark elsewhere, and σφραγ- is in too many verses of Revelation to count as rare there. So the knot of 2N.4 does not stand on machine concentration. It stands on what 2O.1 and the reading show: an angel coming down from heaven at four places in the New Testament, the seal's one place in the Synoptics, and a control, Daniel 6, that supplies one member of seven. The sentence in 2N.5 that the third place was "found by the search and not by reading" is kept with this limit on it: the search pointed, and the pointing was not tested.

b. *The measure is too coarse to see what is known.* The wedding is a knot of this file since 2.5 (Matt 22:1–14 from Rev 19), and its best window is the right one, Rev 19:7–19. Its control figure is 0.72. Matthew 24's best window is Rev 1:3–15, the mourning tribes, the first locus of the whole file, at 0.21. So the instrument finds the right place and cannot tell it from chance, because a block yields six to twenty usable stems and a knot is three or four of them. Its silence on the other blocks therefore says little either way. What it does say: Matthew's own vocabulary is not gathered into places in Revelation at a strength a count of stems can see. The passion (2N.3) is seen by that count; nothing else in Matthew is.

2O.4. **Where the file stands.**

1. The guard at the tomb is the fifty-third transform, with Daniel 6 as a failed rival (2O.1). As relations it is two reversals of side and an undoing the rules cannot yet name (2O.2).
2. By a count of stems with a control, Revelation 11:1–13 and Matthew's cross and tomb is the one relation between a block of Matthew and a place in Revelation that a machine sees unaided (2N.3). The other knots are real to the engine and to reading and are below that instrument's sight (2O.3).
3. So the file has two kinds of result and should keep them apart. Counted without a reader: the instrument finds relatives (2D.4); Matthew's vocabulary leans to Revelation (2C.2, 2D.3); Matthew's passion stands at Revelation 11 (2N.3); no order anywhere (2N.2). Read and then checked against controls: fifty-three transforms, the knots, the reversals. The first kind is small and holds. The second is large and was made by one reader.

## 2P. An instrument of pairs; five loci it pointed to; the opposite deed; the author on stemmatics

2P.0. The author, 15:34: "yes, lets proceed. stammatics itself and text critical reconstructive will probably bear the mark of suppressed revelation". The two operations entered at the end of 2O, and the statement, recorded at 2P.6. Scripts: `pairs/pairs.py`, `relations/pairs_rel.py`; five derivations added to `engine/derivations.py`.

2P.1. **The instrument.** A member is a pair: two word-stems within four words of each other in one verse, neither among the 150 commonest. A pair is rare if it stands in six verses of the New Testament or fewer. Two passages are linked by each rare pair both hold, and each link is marked by whether the Septuagint has the pair too. Nothing is entered by hand. It sits between the single stem (2O.3), which is too common, and the run of three words (section 1), which is too exact.

2P.2. **Calibration: it fails as the stem count did.** Sixteen blocks of Matthew, each against its best thirteen verses of Revelation, with 300 other stretches of Matthew as control.

| Block | links | at | other stretches of Matthew do as well in |
|---|---|---|---|
| the wedding | 2 (wedding + called; killed + the rest) | Rev 19:9–21 | 0.21 |
| the cross and the tomb | 2 | Rev 11:13–12:6 | 0.78 |
| the birth | 2 | Rev 12:2–14 | 0.56 |
| the labourers | 3 | Rev 17:12–18:6 | 0.07 |
| the other twelve | 0 to 3 | | 0.38 to 1.00 |

It finds the right place for the wedding and the right place for the birth and cannot tell either from chance. So as a test of whether a named block is tied to Revelation it is no better than the stems. A knot is two or three pairs, and two or three pairs is what any forty verses of Matthew share with some thirteen of Revelation.

2P.3. **Discovery, and the null.** The same instrument run blind over everything: every twelve verses of each Gospel against every thirteen of Revelation, and then with other books in Revelation's place.

| In the seed's place (405 verses each) | window pairs with three or more links | of them with three or more the Septuagint lacks |
|---|---|---|
| Revelation | 274 | 64 |
| Acts, from 1:1 | 536 | 174 |
| Romans and 1 Corinthians | 86 | 36 |
| Hebrews, James, 1 Peter | 132 | 4 |

Revelation stands between Acts and the letters, which is 2D.4 again by a third instrument: it behaves as a relative of the Gospels, less than Luke's second volume and more than Paul. Acts leads on the list of the twelve and on Luke's own phrases. The nulls also show the instrument's false positives: Rom 6:3–4 with Matt 14:2 (baptized, raised, the dead) is three links the Septuagint lacks and is nothing.
