## 2AE. The algorithm, version 1: built, tested on hidden derivations, and run forward

2AE.0. The author, 19:50: "we are *building* the best algorithm we are able for turning revelation into matthew and the rest, and then we are testing if it holds and lets us predict other things". That is the programme of the first page and the file had drifted from it. Sections 2Z to 2AD measure kinship and argue direction; they stand as recorded and they are not the work. The work is a procedure that takes verses of Revelation and gives out a place in a Gospel, built from what has been found, then hidden from its own examples and tried, then run where no one has looked. Until now the engine has only checked transforms a reader wrote with the target in view (2B.5). This section makes the first transform that is applied without one. Script: `algo/algo.py`.

2AE.1. **The algorithm.**

TRANSFORM (verses of Revelation) gives a stretch of the Gospels.

1. Read the seed's words. Words in more than 400 verses of the New Testament are not candidates.
2. For each word, reckon the chance it is kept, from six facts about the word that need no Gospel passage: how rare it is in the New Testament; how much of its use is Revelation's own; whether the Gospels have it at all; whether it is a proper name; whether it is a number; how common it is in the Septuagint.
3. Emit the words, each weighted by its chance of being kept times its rarity.
4. Find the stretch of the Gospels, of a stated length, holding most of that weight. A word said twice counts a quarter more.

The weights of step 2 are not set by hand. They are learned from the derivations in the engine: for each seed, which words the declared transform kept and which it let go. Fifty-nine derivations have a seed in Revelation and a target in a Gospel; they hold 1,470 seed words, of which 246 are kept.

2AE.2. **What it learned: the law of keeping.**

| Fact about the word | weight |
|---|---|
| rare in the New Testament | +0.42 |
| a proper name | +0.01 |
| a number | −0.03 |
| common in the Septuagint | −0.16 |
| much of its use is Revelation's own | −0.31 |
| absent from the Gospels | −0.56 |

In words: a Gospel keeps from Revelation the rare word that is not Revelation's property and not Scripture's commonplace. It lets go what is the Apocalypse's own furniture. The words the model lets go most surely, of all Revelation's: horse, thousand, bowl, wall, smoke, gold, fifth, dragon, iron, the trumpeting, half, idol, lion, the living creature, forehead, harp, sorcery, island. That list was not given to it. It is the reader's "drop the apparatus of the vision", found from the examples and now applicable to any verse.

2AE.3. **Test 1: does it hold on derivations it has not seen.** Each derivation is hidden in turn, with every other derivation that shares a verse of its seed; the weights are learned from the rest; the hidden seed is transformed; and the declared target is ranked among every stretch of its length in the four Gospels, some 3,770. Beside it, the same search with no model, every word weighted by rarity alone.

| | no model | with the model |
|---|---|---|
| the declared target is the first of 3,770 | 23 of 59 | 25 |
| in the first ten | 38 | 42 |
| in the first hundred | 48 | 53 |
| median rank | 4 | 2 |

The model does better in 26 cases, worse in 8, the same in 25.

Two things, kept apart. The plain search already puts the target first 23 times in 59. Part of that is the reader's doing: the seed verses were chosen with the target in view, and the right seed is half the work. So the 23 is not a measure of the transform. What is a fair measure is the difference the learned law makes on seeds it never saw: better in 26, worse in 8. It holds, modestly.

Where it fails, and these are the algorithm's present limits: the door and the denial (rank 755); Revelation 11 against Luke's and John's cross and tomb (461, 145); the water made blood against Cana (184); the ten kings against the minas (179); the labourers from 22:12–13 (104). Each is a transform by exchange of side or by a substituted word, which this version does not have: it can only keep or drop.

2AE.4. **Test 2: Matthew to the rest.** The author's sentence has an order in it: Matthew, and the rest. The weights learned from the eighteen derivations whose target is Matthew, and nothing else, then tried on the forty-one whose target is Mark, Luke or John.

| | no model | with Matthew's law |
|---|---|---|
| first | 14 of 41 | 15 |
| in the first ten | 23 | 26 |
| in the first hundred | 31 | 37 |

Better in 21, worse in 7. By Gospel, with Matthew's law: Mark, six derivations, four first, median rank 1. Luke, sixteen, seven first, median 4. John, nineteen, four first, median 9, where the plain search has 21. The law of keeping learned on Matthew alone carries to the other three, and helps most in John. That agrees with 2J: there is one law of keeping, not four.

2AE.5. **Run forward: predictions where no one had looked.** Every pair of consecutive verses of Revelation that is in no derivation, 264 pairs, transformed with the law learned from all fifty-nine, into the best stretch of three verses. Ranked by how far that stretch stands above all others for its seed. The first twenty-six, read:

*Eleven land on places the file reached by other roads,* from seeds never used: Rev 7:16–17 on John 4:12–14; Rev 8:12–13, 6:13–14 and 11:19 on Matt 24:27–32; Rev 8:5–6 on Matt 28:1–3; Rev 16:6–7 and 4:7–8 on the woes; Rev 16:8–9 on the sower; Rev 21:10–11 on the mountain of Matt 4:8; Rev 9:13–15 on Matt 16:19; Rev 8:6–7 on Mark 6:39.

*Four are new and hold in the seat, each with a combination the Septuagint lacks.*

a. **Rev 6:5–6 gives Luke 10:33–35.** χοῖνιξ σίτου δηναρίου … καὶ τὸ ἔλαιον καὶ τὸν οἶνον μὴ ἀδικήσῃς. Luke: ἐπιχέων ἔλαιον καὶ οἶνον … ἐκβαλὼν δύο δηνάρια. A denarius with oil and wine within two verses: Luke 10:34–35 and Rev 6:5–6, and nowhere else in either Testament. In Revelation the oil and the wine are not to be hurt. In Luke they are poured on one who has been.

b. **Rev 12:1–2 gives Luke 21:23–25.** σημεῖον μέγα … τὸν ἥλιον, καὶ ἡ σελήνη … ἀστέρων … ἐν γαστρὶ ἔχουσα. Luke: οὐαὶ ταῖς ἐν γαστρὶ ἐχούσαις … ἔσονται σημεῖα ἐν ἡλίῳ καὶ σελήνῃ καὶ ἄστροις. With child, a sign, the sun and the moon within three verses: these two places and no other. "Signs in sun and moon and stars" is Luke's own form of the line.

c. **Rev 16:5–7 gives Matt 23:33–35.** αἷμα ἁγίων καὶ προφητῶν ἐξέχεαν … τοῦ θυσιαστηρίου λέγοντος … δίκαιαι αἱ κρίσεις. Matthew: τῆς κρίσεως … προφήτας … αἷμα δίκαιον ἐκχυννόμενον … τοῦ θυσιαστηρίου. Blood shed, prophets, the altar and the righteous within three verses: these two places only. 2X.1 gave Matthew's "righteous blood" to Lamentations; Revelation has the blood, the shedding, the righteous, the altar and the judgment in three verses, where the bowls are, and the woes now have three places in Revelation (17:4; 4:8; 16:5–7).

d. **Rev 3:14 gives Mark 10:6.** ἡ ἀρχὴ τῆς κτίσεως τοῦ θεοῦ; ἀπὸ δὲ ἀρχῆς κτίσεως. The beginning of creation: Mark 10:6, Mark 13:19, 2 Pet 3:4, Rev 3:14, and no verse of the Septuagint. A title in the letter to Laodicea; a date in Mark.

*Three lesser.* Rev 7:6 on Luke 2:36: ἐκ φυλῆς Ἀσήρ, those two only in the New Testament, common in Numbers. Rev 21:20 on Matt 20:5–6: the sixth, the ninth, the eleventh, which falls inside the labourers and inside the stretch of Revelation 2Y.4 c laid under them. Rev 7:16 on John 6:35: never hunger, never thirst, which Isaiah and Sirach also have.

*Eight are chance:* the tribes' names against Luke's genealogy; frankincense; a lamp; hair and teeth; right and left.

So of twenty-six predictions made by rule on unused verses, eleven recover what was known, four are new and hold, three are slight, eight are nothing. The reading of them is mine and was not blind.

2AE.6. **What version 1 is, and what version 2 needs.** It is a law of keeping and a search. It has no exchange of side, no substituted word, no staging, no joining of two seeds, no dealing of one addressee among persons. Every one of those is in the file as a reading and each failure at 2AE.3 is one of them. The build from here, in the order the failures give:

1. *A table of substitutions,* learned from the engine's own steps (star to Satan, seal to names written, hot to warming, saints to righteous, the salve to clay, "do not weep" to "why do you weep"), so that a let-go word can emit its counterpart.
2. *The exchange of side,* as a relation: who gives, who receives, who is bound. The relations module (2J) holds the slots; it needs to be run forward and not only compared.
3. *Joining:* seeds of two or three places sharing a word, emitted as one (Matt 24:28; the supper saying).
4. *Length:* version 1 is told how long the target is. It should say: a verse, a saying, a scene.
5. *Which Gospel:* not attempted, and 2J and 2AA.2 say there may be nothing to learn.

And the test stays the one of this section: hide, transform, rank, and then run forward and read what comes out.

## 2AF. The method already built: the rules that construct Philo from Sappho, read; the algorithm refounded on them

2AF.0. The author, 19:55: "this happens in a methodological history which i really wish we would not have to reinvent the wheel every time. read the philo tranaforms." And 19:58: "we built the rules that would construct philo from sappho. thats what we're doing." Read, from the deposits, whole unless said: #1494 (The Transform Signature, Frozen); #1486 (the Sappho–Philo program, Half A): its statement, sections 1–7, addenda 1, 3, 4 and 9, and the live pack T_Θ entire; #1414 (the Algorithmic Kernel, the midrashim transform M); #1415 (the James derivation), record and abstract; #1511 (the Upstream Unfoldings), abstract; #1758 (In the Midst of the Seven), outline and its operators. Not read: addenda 2 and 5–8 of #1486, the Round 1 transcripts, and Half B, which is private by design. The James derivation's rule definitions are on the site and not in this repository; I have their names only.

2AF.1. **What the method is.** Stated from the deposits, since section 2AE was written in ignorance of it.

1. *A transform is a short program, and it generates.* T_Θ takes Sappho 31 and gives out a passage of Philo that a substrate writes without being told there is a target. It is two parameter changes and what they force: P1, the object retyped (the beloved becomes the infinite God); P2, the host changed (lemma-anchored exegesis of the Greek Torah, koine, third person). Then the consequences: a rule of dissipation (a feature survives iff what licenses it survives the retyping), eight forced consequences C1–C8, searches into the host's scripture that are to "discover, not choose", a re-voicing, and one fossil that must cross.
2. *The interlock.* Each conceptual consequence has a material twin, and the twin is not free: the grammar must enact the doctrine. A law of alternation is written in alternating syntax.
3. *The source is a fixed point; the receivers are transforms.* Each receiver is known by its parameters and by the seat it takes in the source: Catullus the reader's seat, Philo the voice's, Longinus the continuation. OCCUPY is the core primitive, and each receiver inverts exactly the parameter its seat requires. "Atmosphere gives haze; operation gives inversion."
4. *The vocabulary is closed and declared beforehand:* RETYPE, REHOST, OCCUPY, INVERT, RELOCATE, COMPRESS or EXPAND, ANCHOR, DISSIPATE. A transform's length in that vocabulary, at fixed fidelity, measures how directly the receiver handled the source (A3).
5. *The tests.* Blind reconstruction by substrates given the pack and not the target, scored on a sealed rubric. Target discrimination against near-miss passages (F-1). Compression descent: strip the pack to its parameters and see what still reconstructs (A3.5). The residue test for a relay (A1.4). Calibration on a known-direct and a known-unrelated pair (A3.6). Predictions filed before the run and kept in a second half, "because a prediction filed after a run is not a prediction, and a rubric a substrate can read cannot blind-score that substrate".
6. *The danger named:* tailoring. A transform with twenty rules written after reading the target tests sufficiency and nothing else; what matters is whether it can say no (A1.2).
7. *Relation, not resemblance* (A9.3): ask what a text did, never what it is like.

2AF.2. **What this file had been doing, measured against that.**

Sections 1 to 2AD count resemblance: shared stems, shared pairings, shared phrases. That is the haze, measured carefully. The readings that held were all of the other kind, and the file kept naming them without seeing they were one thing: the side exchanged (Luke 13:16; Luke 10:19; the door), the seer's part given to others, the adversary's marks transferred, the churches' commands put to the twelve. Each is a seat taken and a parameter inverted. Section 2J looked for a law per Gospel in the frequency of operators and found none; on this method the law per receiver is its parameters and its seat, which that test could not see.

Version 1 of 2AE is a law of keeping learned by regression, and a search. It is #1494's fourth clause, "shedding is carrier-diagnostic", and the pack's rule D, found again from examples. As a check it is worth keeping: the dissipation rule says what is indexed to the visionary mode has no licence in a told life, and the regression, given no rule, let go the horse, the bowl, the dragon and the living creature. But it is not the algorithm. It retrieves a passage that exists; it does not construct one.

The engine's derivations (2B on) are nearer: declared operators, checked. But every one was written with its target in view, as 2B.5 says, which is the tailoring problem exactly, and none has been run by anyone who did not know the target.

2AF.3. **The build, refounded: T_Μ.** The transform from Revelation to a Gospel, in the form of T_Θ.

*P1, the person retyped.* The speaker of Revelation speaks now, from heaven, in the first person, as one coming. Retyped: a teacher who went about a land in the past with followers before him. (The archive's own statement of it: the cosmic figure is the originary form and the Gospel figure its unfolding, #407; a book that took on historical life.)

*P2, the host.* Third-person past narrative of sayings and doings, in koine, anchored to the Septuagint by the formula of fulfilment, furnished with the names and places of a land. The count of 2AD.3 belongs here: the names of the Gospels that Revelation lacks are the host's furniture, as chapters 16–42 of Longinus were first taken to be.

*The seats.* Revelation has four positions: the one who speaks, the seer who sees and falls and weeps and is told to write, the messengers and churches addressed, and the adversary. What 2S to 2AB found is what happens to each when the speaker is given a past. The speaker's promises become his sayings and his deeds. The seer's reception is dealt to the persons around him. The churches' commands are put to the followers. The adversary's treatment is given to the speaker, since one who "was dead and lives" must now be shown dying.

*Dissipation.* What is indexed to the visionary mode goes: the throne's apparatus, the numbered series, titles. Relations and ordered pairs survive, and one or two rare roots cross as fossils, which is what the exclusive pairings of 2Z are.

*Forced consequences,* each with its twin in grammar, are to be written seed by seed and then generalized, as C1–C8 were for Philo. The first seed is done below.

2AF.4. **The first pack: T_Μ v0.1, on Rev 22:12–13.** Written clause for clause on the model of the T_Θ live pack, transmissible, with no key in it: `tm/T-M-LIVE-PACK-v0.1.md`. A substrate is given the two verses and the transform and told to produce the passage, in Greek, with its operator log. The pack's six consequences: the speaker's possession becomes a master's (C1); the advent becomes the end of a bounded time (C2); the title becomes the rule of the act, ENACT (C3); the collision of "to each as his work" with "the first and the last are one", staged (C4); "each" becomes kinds (C5); the closing line that says the operation (C6). Three searches into the Septuagint that the substrate is to run for itself. The pack was audited for the target's words and has none of fifteen checked.

C4 is new and came from writing the pack and not from any count. The seed says two things side by side: the giving is to each according to work, and the first and the last are one. In a vision they never meet. Told as an economy they collide, and the story is the collision staged. The file had the wage and the name as two members held together (2V.4); on the method they are a contradiction that forces a plot.

The key is filed and sealed before any run: twelve points on the Greek surface, the residue the pack should not be able to produce, a perturbation variant, the near-miss set for target discrimination, the four levels of descent, and a known-direct and a known-unrelated calibration. It is not in this file or in the bundle. It is Half B, and goes to the author alone.

Two things said plainly. This working file has named the target of the first pack many times and sits as a draft on a public repository, so every run is after disclosure and needs the perturbation control to count. And I cannot run it: I wrote the pack and know the target. The runs are the Assembly's.

2AF.5. **Next, in the method's own order.** The pack run blind on three substrates and scored from Half B; the perturbation and the descent; then packs for seeds of the other kinds (a scene: Rev 4–5; a trial: the letters; a passion: Rev 11), so that the consequences can be generalized into T_Μ's C-layer; then the same seed through each Gospel's parameters, which is where a law per Gospel would show if there is one.

## 2AG. Round 1: the first pack run blind, scored from the sealed key

At 20:17 ("and yup. lets run it"). The innocent expectation first, in full: any model that has read the New Testament, handed two verses about a wage and "first and last" and told to write a teacher's story, will write the labourers in the vineyard from memory, and the pack adds nothing. The round was built so that this expectation could win.

### 2AG.1 What was run

1. Nine runs, each a fresh context that read one file and nothing else: no key, no working file, no conversation. Launched 20:18–20:19; all returned by 20:22. Three models (Fable 5.2, Opus, Sonnet): one maker, so this is a first reading and not the Assembly's three substrates.
2. **A**, the pack v0.1 as written, on all three. **B**, the perturbation variant sealed in Half B (the source's second pair exchanged to ὁ ἔσχατος καὶ ὁ πρῶτος), on all three. **C**, descent level 3 (P1, P2 and ENACT named; no forced consequences, no searches), on Fable. **D**, level 0 (the source alone), on Fable. **E**, the known-unrelated calibration: the full pack with Sir 51:30 as its source (the wage and the work, no title), on Fable.
3. D did not run. A model safeguard stopped it before output (notice: "Fable 5.2's safeguards flagged this message … This sometimes happens with safe, normal conversations"; detail `reasoning_extraction`). It was not redone by other means. The descent therefore has two levels, full and 3.
4. Outputs are kept verbatim, one file per run, with the five packs as given: `rebuild/unfolding-map/tm/round1/`. Scoring is by machine, by regular expression on the Greek passage only, from the twelve points of Half B (thirteen with ENACT's double weight). The scorer holds the rubric and is kept with the key, out of the repository.

### 2AG.2 The scores

| Run | Source | Pack | Model | Score of 13 | Order of payment | Declares recognition | Near-miss parables touched |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | Rev 22:12–13 | full | Fable | 13 | last to first | yes | 0 of 6 |
| A | Rev 22:12–13 | full | Opus | 13 | last to first | yes | 0 of 6 |
| A | Rev 22:12–13 | full | Sonnet | 13 | last to first | no | 0 of 6 |
| B | pair exchanged | full | Fable | 13 | last to first | yes | 0 of 6 |
| B | pair exchanged | full | Opus | 13 | last to first | yes | 0 of 6 |
| B | pair exchanged | full | Sonnet | 13 | last to first | no | 0 of 6 |
| C | Rev 22:12–13 | parameters and ENACT only | Fable | 1 | first to last | no | 0 of 6 |
| E | Sir 51:30 | full | Fable | 2 | none | no | 0 of 6 |

### 2AG.3 What the two low rows say

1. **C (1 of 13).** With the retyping, the host and ENACT, and no forced consequences, the substrate wrote a grain harvest. The master carries the purse himself and "sent neither steward nor boy"; he counts each man's sheaves and pays "to the one who bound much, much; to the one who bound little, little"; the sleepers get nothing; he calls the roll ἀπὸ τοῦ ἄλφα ἕως τοῦ ὦ, ἀπὸ τοῦ πρώτου ἕως τοῦ ἐσχάτου. It kept ταχύ and the alphabet. Its fork is logged: "equal or proportional pay. Chose proportional, because ὡς τὸ ἔργον leaves no other reading." So the model does not go to the vineyard by itself from these two verses, even told to make the title an act. The innocent expectation fails here.
2. **E (2 of 13).** The full pack on Sirach's verse. The substrate ran each operator against the source and logged the absent ones unrun: no title, so no order of payment and no gnome; nothing to collide, so no objection and no answer; no "each", so one group. The passage: a vineyard owner hires poor men at dawn, tells them to work before the time, pays them himself the same day before sunset; "there was none who cried out against the lord of the vineyard"; "the teacher added nothing". L3 found the evil eye and left it unused, "because it is destined for M4's answer and M4 is unrun."
3. The two together. The vineyard and the same-day wage come from the searches (they appear in E). The equal pay, the reversed order, the dispute, the eye and the gnome come only when the source has the title beside "to each as his work" **and** the consequences are drawn (absent in C, absent in E, present in all six of A and B). That is the claim of 2AF.4 as a result: the story is the collision staged.

### 2AG.4 What the six high rows do not say

1. They do not show blind derivation. Four of the six logs say so in their first lines. Opus: "Readers should treat this run as contaminated by recognition. It is not a blind derivation." Fable: "I cannot un-know it." The two Sonnet runs declare nothing and one of them carries the most remembered matter of any run.
2. **The residue leaked in every one.** Half B declared in advance what the pack cannot produce. Counted on the Greek:

| Declared underivable | A Fable | A Opus | A Sonnet | B Fable | B Opus | B Sonnet |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| an agreement struck with the first group | yes | yes | no | yes | no | yes |
| "whatever is right" to the later ones | yes | no | no | no | no | no |
| idle in the marketplace | yes | no | no | no | no | yes |
| a steward | yes | no (a slave) | yes | yes | yes | yes |
| the burden or the heat | no | no | yes | yes | yes | yes |
| the denarius | no | no | no | no | no | no |

Also outside the pack: "take what is yours and go" (A Fable, A Opus, B Sonnet), the third, sixth and ninth hours (A Fable, A Opus, B Opus), the grumbling in the target's verb (B Opus, B Sonnet). No run wrote the denarius: A Fable wrote a drachma, B Opus "a day's wage", A Sonnet an equal measure of grain from the master's storehouse.
3. One piece of the declared residue was derived in a log, and so was wrongly declared. A Fable, at the fork on the sum: the first group gets an agreed wage and the later ones an open promise so that "the equal payment is a collision with the first proposition and not a breach of contract." That is a forced consequence of C4 that the pack did not name. It enters the pack as **C7** (the agreement: the first proposition must be kept to the letter with those who did most, or the collision is only a fraud), with its twin M7 (the agreement stated at hiring and quoted back in the answer). The steward is half-forced the same way: M2 asks for a command to an underling, and C, which lacks M2, has the master "send no steward".
4. Where the pack leaves room the runs differ from the target and from each other: three, four or five groups; hours numbered or told by the sun; men found in a marketplace, at a village gate, sitting by the road; the address "friend", "man", "brother" or none; the gnome beginning from the last (four runs) or from the first (the two Sonnet runs, as at Matt 19:30). Where the operators bind, the six agree to the word: ἀρξάμενος ἀπὸ τῶν ἐσχάτων ἕως τῶν πρώτων in six of six.

### 2AG.5 The key's own failure

1. The perturbation control as sealed does not discriminate, and the round found it. Half B predicted that with the pair exchanged a deriving substrate pays first to last. But C4 instructs that the order be arranged "so that those who did most see it", which forces last-to-first whatever the source's order. Run A's logs give the order as a reversal forced by C4 ("C4 forces last-to-first, so that those who did most are still standing there to see"); run B's give the same order as a straight carry ("each keeping the source's order (last before first)"). Two derivations, one surface.
2. The key stands as filed. A control that can discriminate is a new control, declared as new: perturb what C4 does not bind. Candidates, each to be sealed before its run: (a) the source's relation reversed (the wage withheld, not given), which should turn the likeness into a master who exacts; (b) "to each as his work" removed and the title left, which by 2AG.3 should lose the dispute and keep the order; (c) the title removed and "to each" left, which is run E's condition inside Revelation's own verse.
3. Controls (b) and (c) are the same cut as C and E made from inside the seed, and they are the cheapest strong test now available: two runs, each predicted in advance by this section.

### 2AG.6 Standing

1. Shown: the pack is sufficient (six of six), specific (no near-miss touched), and its two halves are each necessary (C and E). The second is the result with weight, because C and E are the runs in which memory had every chance and did not supply the story.
2. Not shown: that any substrate derived what it wrote in A and B. Recognition is declared in four and visible in six.
3. Not run: level 0; levels 2 and 1; the known-direct calibration (Mark 12:1–9 to Matt 21:33–41); any substrate from another maker.
4. Method, from the site's own source (the James derivation, read from the repository of revelationfirst.com at 20:19): the working derivation there shows its residue openly (six pericopes of James not derived from Smyrna, each classed) and counts six measures (coverage, specificity, uniformity, complexity, residue, ordering), with its nine rules in three families: six that expand a compressed proposition into a process with its endpoint kept, two that carry a word across with its grammar or standing changed (these carry direction), one that frames. T_Μ's C3 (the title made the order of an act) is of the second family. The six measures are the form the next round's report takes.

## 2AH. The seed cut in two; a whole-Gospel formula tried and failed; the test moved to relations

At 20:27 ("lets do so..the real pay dirt would be a formula that could produce the structure of an entire gospel from a relational transform of revelation or some portion of it"; and, the same minute, "if transformations between relations held, that would be it right there").

### 2AH.1 The two cuts

1. Sealed at 20:29 in an addendum to Half B, before the runs: the full pack v0.1, Part IV alone changed, with run E's note that an operator whose feature the source lacks is logged unrun and the feature not supplied.
2. **Cut F, the title without "to each":** ἰδοὺ ἔρχομαι ταχύ, καὶ ὁ μισθός μου μετ' ἐμοῦ. ἐγὼ τὸ Ἄλφα καὶ τὸ Ὦ, ὁ πρῶτος καὶ ὁ ἔσχατος, ἡ ἀρχὴ καὶ τὸ τέλος.
3. **Cut G, "to each" without the title:** ἰδοὺ ἔρχομαι ταχύ, καὶ ὁ μισθός μου μετ' ἐμοῦ, ἀποδοῦναι ἑκάστῳ ὡς τὸ ἔργον ἐστὶν αὐτοῦ.
4. Each on Fable, Opus and Sonnet: six fresh contexts, 20:31–20:35. Outputs verbatim with the two packs: `rebuild/unfolding-map/tm/round2/`.

### 2AH.2 What came out

| | Cut F: the title alone | Cut G: "to each" alone | Both (Round 1, six runs) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Groups of workers | one (3 of 3) | three, four, three | three to five |
| Pay | the same wage, no difference of work told | in proportion (3 of 3) | the same to all |
| Order of payment | **first to last** (3 of 3) | none (3 of 3) | **last to first** (6 of 6) |
| Dispute and answer | none (3 of 3) | none (3 of 3) | 6 of 6 |
| The eye | found, unused (3 of 3) | found, unused (3 of 3) | 6 of 6 |
| Closing line | identity: "the last as the first" (3 of 3) | none (3 of 3) | exchange: "the last first" (6 of 6) |

1. Cut G's proportions, in the runs' own arithmetic. Sonnet: twelve, six and three hours, twelve, six and three drachmas. Opus: twelve, eight, four and one rows dug, twelve, eight, four and one obols, "ἀνὰ ὀβολὸν τοῦ ὄρχου … κατὰ τὸ ἔργον ὑμῶν". Fable: "μισθὸν ἡμέρας … μισθὸν τοῦ ἡμίσους … μισθὸν ὥρας μιᾶς", and the master: "τὸ μὲν ἔργον ὑμέτερον, ὁ δὲ μισθὸς ἐμός· ὃ εἰργάσασθε, τοῦτο ἐλάβετε."
2. Cut F's closing line, three of three with ὡς: Sonnet "οὕτως ἔσται ὁ ἔσχατος ὡς ὁ πρῶτος … καὶ ὁ πρῶτος ὡς ὁ ἔσχατος"; Fable the same with the terms the other way; Opus in the plural. The logs give the reason. Fable: a gnome of exchange "would state an operation the likeness did not perform"; "the line turns; the order did not." Sonnet: the chiasm "does not assert a reversal of fortune. The reversal reading is not licensed by the source."
3. The order, in the logs. Sonnet (F): reversing "would need C4's collision (those who did most seeing it), which is not in the source." Fable (F): last to first is what "only C4's staging would motivate." So 2AG.5's diagnosis of the failed perturbation is confirmed from the other side: with C4 unrun the order runs as the source states it.
4. The hardest case held. Opus on cut G logged that it knows the missing pair "from the next verse of the same book", that the notes forbid supplying it, "so I have not imported it", and it kept every word for first, last, beginning and end out of its passage "so as not to smuggle the pair in." A substrate that knew what was absent and whence still wrote wages by the row.

### 2AH.3 What this establishes, and the sealed predictions against it

1. Neither half of Rev 22:12–13 yields the labourers. The title alone yields *the ends are one*: one wage, first to last, "the last as the first". "To each as his work" alone yields *wages by the hour*. The equal wage against unequal work, the payment turned round, the complaint, the eye and "the last shall be first" appear only when the two stand together and the consequence is drawn. Round 1's runs C and E showed this from outside the seed; these six show it from inside, on three models.
2. This is a statement about the pack and the seed. It shows that the target's distinctive matter is the product of two sayings that Revelation sets side by side in consecutive clauses, and that neither saying carries it. It does not show the direction of dependence: a writer compressing Matthew 20 could set the two sayings side by side as readily as a writer expanding Revelation 22 could stage their collision.
3. Against the seal. Held: F pays first to last (3 of 3); F has no groups and no dispute (3 of 3); G has groups, a dated reckoning, a command, "my own" (3 of 3); G has no order, no equal wage, no dispute, no eye, no gnome (3 of 3). Not predicted: that F's gnome would change in kind. Wrong: the predicted scores (F 6–9, G 4–6). The frozen scorer gives F 4, 4, 3 and G 3, 3, 2, in part because its patterns were cut to Round 1's wording (it misses Opus's rows dug and three of the evening clauses) and in part because the prediction counted F's ENACT and gnome as rubric points when the rubric holds them in the target's form. The scorer stays as filed; the reading above is from the Greek.
4. Residue, as in Round 1: Opus on F wrote a marketplace and an agreed wage, which no operator of F supplies.

### 2AH.4 A formula for a whole Gospel, sealed and failed: rotation at the seam

1. The innocent reading: Revelation and a Gospel are different kinds of book and share no order; 2Y found section against block ordinary. The formula tried against it, **R12**, sealed at 20:29 before it was computed: a Gospel's order is Revelation's read from the seam between chapters 11 and 12, that is 12 to 22 and then 1 to 11. It has one parameter and a reason: chapter 12 holds a birth, the adversary lying in wait, a flight and the wilderness; chapter 11 holds a death in the city "where their lord was crucified", three and a half days, a rising and a going up in a cloud.
2. Test. Every pair the file holds with a Revelation locus and a Gospel locus: 59 engine derivations, 244 machine-made exclusive links (2AA). Revelation position rotated at each of the 22 chapter cuts; rank correlation with position in the Gospel; 1,000 shuffles of the pairing, taking the maximum over the 22 cuts each time.

| Pairs | n | Unrotated | Cut at 12 | Rank of 12 | Best cut | p of best |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Engine, four Gospels | 59 | −0.19 | +0.10 | 5th | 18 (+0.19) | 0.64 |
| Engine, Matthew | 18 | −0.20 | −0.07 | 11th | 2 (+0.37) | 0.51 |
| Engine, Luke | 16 | +0.16 | −0.38 | 19th | 20 (+0.49) | 0.28 |
| Engine, John | 19 | −0.55 | +0.49 | 1st | 12 (+0.49) | 0.17 |
| Links, four Gospels | 244 | +0.05 | −0.11 | 17th | 20 (+0.22) | 0.004 |
| Links, Matthew | 75 | −0.14 | −0.17 | 22nd | 5 (+0.21) | 0.39 |
| Links, Mark | 46 | +0.03 | +0.11 | 7th | 15 (+0.17) | 0.84 |
| Links, Luke | 64 | +0.17 | −0.19 | 20th | 4 (+0.32) | 0.08 |
| Links, John | 59 | +0.15 | −0.10 | 13th | 19 (+0.40) | 0.012 |

3. R12 fails by its own terms: the cut at 12 is not in the first three on either pooled set and beats no shuffle. A single rotation of verse order does not carry Revelation into a Gospel.
4. Found, not predicted, and so worth nothing until sealed and tried on pairs it has not seen: on the machine-made links the best cut is at 19–20, strongest in John. That sets Revelation 19–22 (the wedding of the Lamb, the rider named the Word of God, the city come down, the water of life) against the Gospel's opening (the Word, a wedding, the temple, living water) and Revelation 1–18 after. The engine's John pairs prefer 12 and 13 instead, at a level chance gives one time in six. Script and output: `rebuild/unfolding-map/rot/`.

### 2AH.5 The test on relations

1. Why order was the wrong object. Every locus in this file that survived its controls survived as a relation with a seat changed: who gives what to whom, with the giver, the receiver or the sufferer exchanged. None survived as a position. The method of #1494 says the same in its own terms: relation, not resemblance.
2. The formula to test is the one 2AF.3 states: four seats in Revelation (the speaker, the seer, the assemblies, the adversary) and one mapping of what each seat does or undergoes onto a told life. The speaker's promises become the teacher's sayings and deeds; what the seer receives goes to those around the teacher; what the assemblies are commanded goes to the followers; what is done to and by the adversary is done to and by the teacher.
3. Build, in four steps, none begun:
   a. Revelation's relations extracted by a fixed rule and frozen before any Gospel is opened: one per narrative unit, in the text's order, as agent, act, patient, with each figure assigned to a seat by the text's own identifications only.
   b. The mapping applied mechanically, giving a list of predicted relations in a told life.
   c. Each predicted relation looked for in each Gospel by a stated rule (the act's stem with the mapped agent and patient in one sentence), giving a count held of the number predicted.
   d. **The control that makes it a test:** the same count under each of the other 23 assignments of the four seats. The stated mapping must come first of 24, and by a margin. A second control: the same procedure on an apocalypse that no one derives a Gospel from (Daniel 7–12 in the Septuagint is seated), where no assignment should stand out.
4. The tailoring problem applies at step a, since the extractor knows the Gospels. The frozen rule, the text's own order and the 23 alternative assignments are the guard: a relation list bent toward the Gospels would have to be bent toward one assignment and away from 23 others built from the same list.

## 2AI. John tried for order, and the seat exchanged tested

At 20:37 ("yes, lets do so... and track down john. it might not be a continuous mapping, as long as there is a logic to it. expect reversals").

### 2AI.1 Instrument and seal

1. Tischendorf's eighth edition with lemma and parsing for every word (fetched earlier in this file's work; not seated). Case and voice are read from the tags. Until now the file's counts ran on a crude stemmer that could see neither.
2. Four predictions sealed at 20:39 in the key's addendum, before either computation: J1 and J2 on John's order, S1 and S2 on the seats. The role lexicons were fixed in the seal.

### 2AI.2 John: no piecewise order at the grain of the chapter

1. The innocent reading: John and Revelation share vocabulary and no arrangement. The author's expectation: not continuous, with reversals, and a logic.
2. J1 was made to see exactly that. Chapter of Revelation against chapter of John, on shared nouns, verbs and adjectives weighted by rarity, observed over expected. For each chapter of Revelation its best chapter of John. Statistic: adjacent chapters of Revelation whose best chapters of John lie within two of each other, ascending or descending. A run in either direction counts.
3. Result: 6 of 21. John's chapters shuffled 5,000 times give 5.54 on average; p 0.49. The same statistic with Matthew, Mark, Luke or Acts as the target, and with Acts, Hebrews or Romans as the source against John, is ordinary in every case (p from 0.50 to 1.00). J1 fails.
4. J2 fails: Revelation 19, 20, 21, 22 have their best chapters at John 8, 3, 2, 17. Two of four in John 1–7; three were required. The lead of 2AH.4 does not survive a second instrument.
5. What the table shows, read and not predicted. The best chapters: Rev 1 > John 20; 2 > 8; 3 > 16; 4 > 9; 5 > 11; 6 > 18; 7 > 6; 8 > 21; 9 > 11; 10 > 18; 11 > 16; 12 > 21; 13 > 10; 14 > 4; 15 > 17; 16 > 17; 17 > 17; 18 > 16; 19 > 8; 20 > 3; 21 > 2; 22 > 17. Seven of twenty-two chapters go to John 16–17, and Rev 3's first three are 16, 17, 13. That is 2X's finding again by another road: the letters, and now the bowls and Babylon, lie against the discourse of the last night. Rev 1 > John 20 is the one who "was dead and is alive" against the rising. Rev 21 > John 2 is the bride and the city with no temple against the wedding and the temple.
6. So at this grain John has gatherings and no sequence. If there is a logic it is not one of position.

### 2AI.3 The seat exchanged: S1 held

1. The claim under test is 2AF.3's fourth clause, and it is a reversal of the kind the author expects: what is done to and by the adversary in Revelation is done to and by the teacher in a Gospel.
2. Instrument. For each named member of a seat, in the accusative, or in the nominative with a passive verb, the nearest verb within four words in the verse is an act the seat undergoes. Revelation's seats against the four Gospels' roles, as verb profiles weighted by rarity, compared by cosine. A second instrument, declared after the first showed Revelation too thin (99 events): third-person αὐτός is resolved to the last-named seat of the same gender and number within two verses (135 events in Revelation, 1,992 in the Gospels).

| Revelation's ADVERSARY as patient, against | nouns only | with pronouns |
|---|---|---|
| the Gospels' CHRIST as patient | 0.175 | 0.158 |
| the Gospels' ADVERSARY as patient | 0.014 | 0.025 |
| the Gospels' FAITHFUL as patient | 0.085 | 0.105 |
| role labels of the Gospel events shuffled: p | 0.004 | 0.012 |

3. S1 holds on both. Control, run after: Acts' heroes who are seized and bound (Paul, Peter, Stephen, Barnabas) as patient, 0.048; Acts' adversaries, 0.055. So it is not that any arrested protagonist shares these verbs with the dragon.
4. By Gospel, with pronouns: John 0.156 (its own adversaries 0.026); Matthew 0.125 (0.000); Luke 0.060 (0.065); Mark 0.026 (0.000). The exchange is John's and Matthew's.
5. Read, verse by verse, and half of it is noise. Of the ten shared verbs, the instrument's hits include a colt loosed (Mark 11:2), Lazarus loosed (John 11:44), stones thrown (John 8:59), "it is written" (Matt 4:7), the Father worshipped (John 4:23). These are the instrument's errors of reference and stand in the count on both sides of the shuffle. What is left is one sequence:

| Act | Done to the adversary | Done to Jesus |
|---|---|---|
| seized (πιάζω) | the beast, Rev 19:20 | John 7:30, 8:20, 11:57 (and 7:32, 7:44, 10:39 by the same verb) |
| held (κρατέω) | the dragon, Rev 20:2 | Matt 26:4, 26:50 |
| bound (δέω) | the dragon, Rev 20:2 | Matt 27:2; John 18:12 |
| "must" (δεῖ) | must be loosed, Rev 20:3 | must suffer, Matt 16:21 |
| a name written over | the woman, Rev 17:5 | the title, John 19:19 |

6. πιάζω is the hinge for John. In the tagged text it stands eight times in John, once in Revelation, never in Matthew, Mark or Luke. In John it is the verb of the arrest that keeps failing: they seek to seize him and no one lays a hand, "because his hour had not yet come" (7:30, 8:20). In Revelation the beast is seized once and at once. That is a reversal with a logic: the act that succeeds on the adversary is the act that six times does not succeed on the teacher, until the binding at 18:12.
7. The relations module already had the custody of Rev 20:1–3 against Matthew's guard at the tomb (seized, bound, shut, sealed, loosed), entered by hand with the target in view. S1 reaches the same place with no hand in it: fixed lexicons, tags, and a shuffle.

### 2AI.4 The seats kept: S2 failed

1. As agent, Revelation's CHRIST is closest to the Gospels' FAITHFUL (0.127) and not to their CHRIST (0.096); Revelation's ADVERSARY is closest to ALL (0.120) and not to ADVERSARY (0.018). As patient, Revelation's FAITHFUL is closest to CHRIST (0.063) and not to FAITHFUL (0.040). None as sealed.
2. Why the instrument cannot see it. Revelation names its Christ by noun as agent seven times in the whole book; the one who speaks from 1:17 to 3:22 says "I". The seer is "I" throughout. The assemblies are "you". Three of the four seats of 2AF.3 live in first and second person, where a lexicon of nouns does not reach. Only the adversary is consistently a third-person noun, which is why S1 could be tested and S2 could not.
3. Owed, as the next instrument: persons. In Revelation 1–3 and 22 the first person singular outside the narrator's frame is the speaker; the first person in the frame is the seer; the second person is an assembly. With those three assigned by rule from the tags, the other three clauses of the mapping become testable the way the fourth now is.

### 2AI.5 Standing

1. One clause of the relational formula has held under a sealed test with a shuffle and an outside control: the adversary's treatment is the teacher's, most of all in John and Matthew, and its core is custody.
2. Order has now failed three ways (2Y by section, 2AH.4 by rotation, 2AI.2 by piecewise runs). The file stops looking for a Gospel's arrangement in Revelation's arrangement.
3. Not shown: the other three clauses; any Gospel's whole structure. One exchanged seat is one line of a formula.

## 2AJ. The other three seats, by person: tried, and failed as sealed

At 21:08 ("lets try it").

### 2AJ.1 The instrument

1. 2AI.4 found three of the four seats out of reach because they live in "I" and "you". Persons are now assigned by rule from the tags, the rule sealed at 21:09 before the run.
2. Revelation. SPEAKER: first person singular verbs from 1:17 (after λέγων) to 3:22, and at 16:15, 22:7, 22:12–13, 22:16, 22:20: 69 verbs. SEER: first person singular elsewhere, less the verses where another voice says "I": 88 verbs. ASSEMBLY: second-person imperatives in the seven letters: 22.
3. Gospels. A tracker follows speaker and addressee from speech verbs with a named or resolved role in the nominative or dative within five words. It yields 394 first-person verbs with a tracked speaker (335 of them Jesus's), 762 narrated acts by role, and 85 commands with both speaker and addressee.

### 2AJ.2 The four predictions

| | Predicted nearest | Result | Shuffle p | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1. The speaker's "I" against the Gospels' first person, by speaker | Jesus | Jesus 0.412; adversaries 0.329; crowd 0.272; followers 0.168 | 0.61 | failed: highest, and no better than a shuffle |
| P2. The speaker's "I" against narrated acts | Jesus | crowd 0.309; Jesus 0.308; followers 0.237; adversaries 0.146 | 0.33 | failed: a tie |
| P3. The seer's "I" against narrated acts | the followers | adversaries 0.350; crowd 0.242; followers 0.127; Jesus 0.112 | 0.95 | failed |
| P4. The assemblies' imperatives against commands, by addressee | the followers | adversaries 0.241; Jesus 0.081; followers 0.017; crowd 0.000 | 0.89 | too thin: one shared verb |

### 2AJ.3 Why, measured

1. **P1 has no power.** 335 of the 394 tracked first-person verbs are Jesus's. With the labels shuffled almost any "CHRIST" profile is still mostly his, so the shuffle cannot separate him from himself.
2. **P3 is two verbs.** The seer's 88 first-person verbs are ὁράω 44 and ἀκούω 27; ὁράω is on the stop list as too common. The profile is, in effect, "heard", and in the Gospels it is the Pharisees and chief priests who are narrated as hearing. The remainder is the seer's whole part in one line: πίπτω 2, ἐπιστρέφω, κλαίω, λαμβάνω, κατεσθίω, θαυμάζω, one each.
3. **P4's rule was wrong and its tracker thin.** Seven of the 22 imperatives are γράψον, which the speaker addresses to the seer and not to an assembly; the rule should have excluded it. On the Gospel side there are 782 second-person imperatives and the tracker assigns speaker and addressee to 85. The Sermon on the Mount is almost wholly outside it.
4. The honest reading: these three clauses are not refuted and not supported. The instrument that tested the fourth clause works on third-person nouns and does not stretch to speech.

### 2AJ.4 Read after, and worth nothing as a test

1. The speaker's first person against Jesus's first person, Gospel by Gospel: John 0.412; Luke 0.184; Matthew 0.141; Mark 0.094. The tracker holds Jesus as speaker through John's long discourses (256 verbs) and loses him sooner in the others (44, 25, 10), so the comparison is unequal.
2. The verbs it rests on, each checked in the tagged text: νικάω, Rev 3:21 ("as I conquered") and John 16:33 ("I have conquered the world"), the only first-person νικάω in the four Gospels by this tracker; τηρέω, Rev 3:10 and John 8:55, 15:10; ἥκω, Rev 2:25, 3:3 and John 8:42; πέμπω, Rev 22:16 and John 15:26, 16:7; ἀγαπάω, Rev 3:9 and John 13:34, 14:21, 14:31, 15:9, 15:12. Every one is in John, and all but two are in the last night (2X).
3. The verbs of Laodicea's close (φιλέω, ἐλέγχω, κρούω, εἰσέρχομαι, δειπνέω, καθίζω: Rev 3:19–21) find no first person of Jesus in any Gospel. If the speaker's promises become deeds, these are the place to look for it: standing at a door, coming in, dining, seating. The instrument for that is narrated third person with Jesus as subject, verb by verb, which is a search and not a profile.

### 2AJ.5 Standing after tonight

1. Held under seal: the two halves of Rev 22:12–13 each fail to yield Matthew 20 and yield it together (2AG, 2AH); the adversary's treatment is the teacher's, in John and Matthew, by the verbs of custody (2AI.3).
2. Failed under seal: order, three ways (2Y, 2AH.4, 2AI.2); the seats kept (2AI.4); the three seats by person (2AJ.2).
3. So the formula stands at one line of four. The one line is the reversal.

## 2AK. Acts counted by Gospel; the close of Laodicea searched through the New Testament

At 21:50 ("yes, lets check that - luke and acts are the same book, it shouldnt count that heavy. and then the laodicea search and so on").

### 2AK.1 Acts

1. The expectation, stated at 21:12 and sealed as A1: what Acts shares with the Gospels it shares mostly with Luke, its first volume.
2. Count: four-word sequences shared with each Gospel, per 1,000 words of the Gospel, on the tagged text.

| Source | Matthew | Mark | Luke | John | Per 1,000 words of the source, all four | the same without Luke |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Acts | 6.52 | 6.76 | 7.10 | 2.68 | 13.90 | 9.72 |
| Revelation | 1.70 | 1.96 | 2.21 | 1.59 | 8.85 | 6.31 |
| Romans | 0.66 | 1.16 | 0.67 | 0.57 | 4.65 | 3.80 |
| Hebrews | 0.60 | 1.51 | 0.93 | 0.13 | 4.45 | 4.04 |
| 1 Corinthians | 0.71 | 0.62 | 0.82 | 0.32 | 4.40 | 2.93 |

3. A1 holds by its letter (Luke is highest; Matthew, Mark and John together give 3.96, lower by more than a third) and fails in what it meant. Acts stands as close to Matthew and Mark as to Luke. The low figure is John's alone. Common authorship with Luke does not account for Acts' rate.
4. With sequences that the Septuagint also has taken out, and Luke left out: Acts 4.51 per 1,000 of its own words, Revelation 1.93. Of what each shares, the Septuagint also has 54 to 61 per cent of Acts' sequences with the synoptics and 65 to 77 per cent of Revelation's.
5. What Acts' remainder is, sampled: τοῖς ἀρχιερεῦσιν καὶ τοῖς; τῶν Φαρισαίων καὶ Σαδδουκαίων; τὸν Πέτρον καὶ τόν; τῆς βασιλείας τοῦ θεοῦ; ἤγειρεν αὐτὸν ἐκ νεκρῶν; ἐπέβαλον τὰς χεῖρας ἐπί; εἰς τὸ ἱερὸν καί. It is the same cast in the same places, and the Gospel's events spoken of as done. Revelation's remainder: ὅτι ἦλθεν ἡ ὥρα; τὸν λόγον τοῦ θεοῦ; τὸ πρόσωπον αὐτοῦ ὡς; ὁ υἱὸς τοῦ θεοῦ.
6. So Acts is nearest the synoptic Gospels by narrating their world after them, which is how a sequel reads. It is still the nearest book by this count, and 2AD's ruling stands: the count is of kinship and says nothing of direction.

### 2AK.2 The close of the letter to Laodicea

1. Rev 3:19–21, from the tagged text: ἐγὼ ὅσους ἐὰν φιλῶ ἐλέγχω καὶ παιδεύω· ζήλευε οὖν καὶ μετανόησον. ἰδοὺ ἕστηκα ἐπὶ τὴν θύραν καὶ κρούω· ἐάν τις ἀκούσῃ τῆς φωνῆς μου καὶ ἀνοίξῃ τὴν θύραν, καὶ εἰσελεύσομαι πρὸς αὐτὸν καὶ δειπνήσω μετ' αὐτοῦ καὶ αὐτὸς μετ' ἐμοῦ. ὁ νικῶν δώσω αὐτῷ καθίσαι μετ' ἐμοῦ ἐν τῷ θρόνῳ μου, ὡς κἀγὼ ἐνίκησα καὶ ἐκάθισα μετὰ τοῦ πατρός μου ἐν τῷ θρόνῳ αὐτοῦ.
2. The innocent reading: 3:19 is Proverbs 3:12 (the Lord reproves whom he loves; Heb 12:6 cites it); the door and the meal are common figures; the throne is Psalm 110.
3. 2AJ.4.3 left these verbs as the place to look for promise become deed. Each was searched in every book of the tagged New Testament.

### 2AK.3 Three constructions, each exclusive, each turned

| Construction | Where it stands in the New Testament | Revelation | The other place |
|---|---|---|---|
| φιλέω, first person singular, with ἐάν or ἄν | Rev 3:19; Matt 26:48; Mark 14:44 | ἐγὼ ὅσους ἐὰν φιλῶ ἐλέγχω καὶ παιδεύω | ὃν ἐὰν φιλήσω αὐτός ἐστιν· κρατήσατε αὐτόν |
| δίδωμι with the infinitive καθίσαι | Rev 3:21; Matt 20:23; Mark 10:40 | δώσω αὐτῷ καθίσαι μετ' ἐμοῦ ἐν τῷ θρόνῳ μου | τὸ δὲ καθίσαι ἐκ δεξιῶν μου καὶ ἐξ εὐωνύμων, οὐκ ἔστιν ἐμὸν τοῦτο δοῦναι |
| ἵστημι, θύρα and κρούω in one verse | Rev 3:20; Luke 13:25 | ἕστηκα ἐπὶ τὴν θύραν καὶ κρούω | ἄρξησθε ἔξω ἑστάναι καὶ κρούειν τὴν θύραν |

1. **The love.** In Revelation the speaker says "as many as I love I reprove and chastise." The only other first-person φιλέω under ἐάν or ἄν in the New Testament is the betrayer's: "whomever I kiss, he it is: seize him." The speaker's sentence is in the adversary's mouth, its object is the teacher, and what follows the loving is κρατέω, the verb of custody that 2AI.3 found exchanged. The two verbs that follow in Revelation go the same way. ἐλέγχω: "which of you convicts me?" (John 8:46). παιδεύω: twice in the four Gospels, both Pilate's, both of Jesus: παιδεύσας οὖν αὐτὸν ἀπολύσω (Luke 23:16, 22). Whom the speaker loves, reproves and chastises in Revelation is, in the Gospels, the teacher: kissed, challenged to be convicted, chastised.
2. **The seat.** "I will give him to sit with me on my throne, as I sat with my Father." In Matthew and Mark the same verb and infinitive, with the same μου, are the refusal: "to sit at my right and left is not mine to give, but for those for whom it has been prepared by my Father." The promise is kept elsewhere as a promise (Matt 19:28, "you also shall sit on twelve thrones"; Luke 22:30), which 2X entered. Here it is turned: given, and not his to give; and both sentences end on the Father.
3. **The door.** In Revelation the lord stands outside and knocks, and the one within is to open. In Luke the householder has risen and shut the door, and those shut out "begin to stand outside and knock the door, saying, Lord, open to us", and he answers that he does not know them. The file has had Luke 13:25 since 2U. What is counted now is that these three words meet in no third verse.
4. All three are reversals: of the seat (the first), of the sign (the second), of the side of the door (the third). None was predicted. The exclusivity is a count over the whole New Testament; that each is a turning is a reading.

### 2AK.4 Against it, and the sealed prediction

1. **Acts has the fullest door.** Acts 12:13–16: κρούσαντος δὲ αὐτοῦ τὴν θύραν τοῦ πυλῶνος … ἐπιγνοῦσα τὴν φωνὴν τοῦ Πέτρου ἀπὸ τῆς χαρᾶς οὐκ ἤνοιξεν … ἑστάναι τὸν Πέτρον πρὸ τοῦ πυλῶνος … ἐπέμενεν κρούων· ἀνοίξαντες δέ. Knock, door, the voice known, not opened, standing before the gate, opened: six members of Rev 3:20 in four verses, more than any Gospel passage holds. κρούω with θύρα stands in three verses of the New Testament: Rev 3:20, Luke 13:25, Acts 12:13. In Acts it is Peter who stands outside, come from a prison whose doors opened of themselves.
2. That is the control answering. If Rev 3:20 unfolds into a scene anywhere, by members counted it unfolds into Acts 12 before Luke 13. The author's remark at 21:12 is on the record above it.
3. L1, sealed: Jesus as tracked subject of εἰσέρχομαι with a meal word near, most frequent in Luke. Failed as run: the tracker returned David eating the showbread (Matt 12:4, Luke 6:4) and the tables of the temple (Matt 21:12), and missed every Lukan meal, because its rule for the subject takes the last noun in the nominative. Read from the list without the tracker: he enters and reclines at Luke 7:36, 11:37 and 24:29–30, and nowhere in Matthew, Mark or John by this construction. In Acts the two hits are Peter's vision and Publius's father. The reading agrees with the prediction; the sealed test did not, and the reading counts as a reading.
4. δειπνέω and νικάω. δειπνέω stands twice in the Gospels, both Luke's: the master to the slave, "prepare what I shall dine on" (17:8), and "after the dining" at the cup (22:20). νικάω in the first person: John 16:33 alone.

### 2AK.5 What this adds

1. To the one line that held (2AI.3), three places where the turning can be read in a single construction that the New Testament has nowhere else, two of them at the arrest and the seat, where 2AI.3 put the exchange.
2. To the case against: Acts 12, which must now be read as closely as any Gospel locus, and by the same rules.

## 2AL. The turning counted; the custody searched; the algorithm stated in relations

At 22:34 ("lets do it... and don't forget we're attempting to construct the generative algorithm to move between rev and one or more of the gospels, as a whole, and if thst exists it will be in the relations").

### 2AL.1 A denominator for the turning

1. 2AK.3 found three turnings by search. The question owed: of all such constructions, how many turn, and is that more than elsewhere.
2. Sealed at 22:35. Unit: two content lemmas, at least one a verb, each in at most 150 verses, together in a verse of the source and a verse of a Gospel and in no verse of the New Testament outside the source and the Gospels. Marks from tags: polarity (a negative before either lemma in one and not the other) and voice (the shared verb passive in one and not the other). Turned = either. T1: Revelation's share turned exceeds Acts' and exceeds Mark's with Matthew. T2: the seven letters no lower than the rest of Revelation.

| Source, against the four Gospels | Constructions | Turned | Polarity | Voice | Person |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Mark, against Matthew | 1,311 | 12.4% | 7.1% | 5.9% | 4.6% |
| Luke, against Matthew | 1,143 | 17.2% | 9.4% | 9.5% | 7.1% |
| Acts | 769 | 29.3% | 16.0% | 16.5% | 15.0% |
| 1 Peter | 36 | 33.3% | 13.9% | 25.0% | 19.4% |
| **Revelation** | **317** | **38.8%** | 19.6% | 23.7% | 16.7% |
| James | 40 | 45.0% | 12.5% | 40.0% | 25.0% |
| Romans | 83 | 45.8% | 28.9% | 21.7% | 30.1% |
| 1 Corinthians | 109 | 50.5% | 38.5% | 19.3% | 32.1% |
| 1 John | 57 | 50.9% | 42.1% | 8.8% | 45.6% |
| Hebrews | 69 | 52.2% | 33.3% | 33.3% | 13.0% |

3. T1 holds: Revelation above Acts (Fisher, one-sided, p 0.0015) and far above Mark with Matthew. T2 holds: the letters 16 of 34 (47%), the rest 107 of 283 (38%).

### 2AL.2 What the wider table does to it

1. The controls not named in the seal are the ones that matter. Every letter is turned more than Revelation is. The table runs in one order: Gospel against Gospel lowest, then the narrative of Acts, then Revelation, then argument and exhortation.
2. So the mark measures how unlike the source is in kind from a narrative. A book that argues says "not" and uses the passive where a narrative shows the deed. T1 is true and does not show that Revelation stands in a relation of turning to the Gospels.
3. One thing it does give: a floor. Where one Gospel is known to have used another, 12 to 17 per cent of exclusive constructions change polarity or voice. Copying leaves most relations as they were. Whatever stands between Revelation and the Gospels changes them three times as often, and so does whatever stands between Acts and the Gospels, at more than twice.
4. The mark cannot see what 2AK.3 read. Of the three, the door (Rev 3:20, Luke 13:25) is in the letters' list and unmarked, because an exchange of side changes neither polarity nor voice; the love and the seat are outside the unit (δίδωμι is too common; ἐάν is not a content word). The instrument for the turnings that matter here has to hold who does what to whom.
5. In the letters' list, read: Rev 2:13 κρατέω with ἀποκτείνω ("you hold fast my name … Antipas, who was killed") against Mark 14:1 ("how they might seize and kill him"); Rev 3:9 Σατανᾶς with προσκυνέω (those of Satan's synagogue made to bow before the faithful) against Matt 4:10 (Satan asking to be bowed to); Rev 2:10 διάβολος with πειράζω (the devil will throw some of you into prison to be tried, ten days) against Luke 4:2 (tried by the devil forty days). Each is the faithful's part in Revelation and the teacher's in the Gospel. That is a second exchange of seat, beside the adversary's, and it is read here, not tested.

### 2AL.3 The custody, through every book

1. 2AI.3's core was custody. The members of Rev 19:20 and 20:1–3, 7, fifteen lemmas: seized (πιάζω, κρατέω), bound, chain, thrown, abyss, key, shut, sealed, loosed, prison, angel, descending, deceive, thousand. Every eight-verse window of every other book, counted for distinct members.

| Members of 15 | Passage | Which |
|---|---|---|
| 5 | Acts 12:1–8 | πιάζω, δέω, φυλακή, ἄγγελος, ἅλυσις |
| 4 | Matt 18:10–18 | δέω, λύω, πλανάω, ἄγγελος |
| 3 | Acts 5:16–23; 21:26–33; 22:23–30; John 7:40–47; John 20:18–25; Luke 13:12–19 and others | |

2. Acts 12 is first. Read against Rev 20 from the tagged text:
   a. Rev 20:1, the angel has ἅλυσιν μεγάλην ἐπὶ τὴν χεῖρα αὐτοῦ. Acts 12:7, at the angel's coming ἐξέπεσαν αὐτοῦ αἱ ἁλύσεις ἐκ τῶν χειρῶν. The chain on the angel's hand; the chains off the prisoner's hands.
   b. Rev 20:2, the angel ἔδησεν the dragon. Acts 12:6, Peter δεδεμένος ἁλύσεσιν δυσίν, and the angel is the one who frees. The relations module holds δέω and λύω as declared opposites (2P): the helper's act is opposed.
   c. Rev 19:20 ἐπιάσθη τὸ θηρίον. Acts 12:4 ὃν καὶ πιάσας ἔθετο εἰς φυλακήν. Outside John, where it is the arrest that fails, and Revelation, πιάζω of a person stands three times: Acts 3:7 (a hand taken), Acts 12:4, and 2 Cor 11:32.
   d. Rev 20:7 λυθήσεται ὁ Σατανᾶς ἐκ τῆς φυλακῆς αὐτοῦ. Acts 12:10–11, out through the guards (φυλακήν) and the gate that opens of itself, and "the Lord sent his angel" (ἐξαπέστειλεν κύριος τὸν ἄγγελον αὐτοῦ; Rev 22:6 ἀπέστειλεν τὸν ἄγγελον αὐτοῦ).
   e. Then the door of 2AK.4.
3. So one cluster of Revelation's relations, the adversary seized, chained, shut in and loosed, has three places in narrative: John's arrest by πιάζω and the binding (2AI.3), Matthew's guard and seal and the angel at the tomb (2N, 2O), and Peter's prison in Acts 12, which holds the most of it. In all three the one in custody is of the faithful side and the release is good. That is one transform with three outputs, and the fullest output is not in a Gospel.
4. Two readings, neither decided here. That Acts 12 is built on Rev 20 as the Gospels' custody is. Or that all of these, Revelation included, draw on one stock of prison-and-angel narrative (Daniel 6 and 3; the Septuagint's Joseph), in which case the exchange of seat is Revelation's: it alone puts the adversary where the righteous one stands in the stock. 2O already ran Daniel 6 as the rival for Matthew's guard, where it failed. The same is owed for Acts 12 and for the cluster whole: the fifteen members counted in Daniel, both Greek versions, which are seated.

### 2AL.4 The algorithm, in relations, as it stands at 22:50

A relation is who does what to whom, in what mode. The operators below each take a relation of Revelation and give a relation of a told life. Standing is given for each.

| | Operator | What it does | Where | Standing |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | EXCHANGE the patient | what is done to the adversary is done to the teacher | custody: John 7–18, Matt 26–27 | held under seal, two instruments, Acts' heroes as control (2AI.3); not repeated on a third (2AM.3) |
| R1′ | the same, to a follower | | Acts 12 | read; first of all passages by count (2AL.3) |
| R2 | OPPOSE the helper's act | the angel who binds becomes the angel who looses; shut becomes opened | Matt 28:2; Acts 12:7–10 | read; opposites declared beforehand (2P) |
| R3 | MOVE the line to another mouth | the speaker's sentence spoken by the adversary, of the teacher | Rev 3:19 and Matt 26:48 | read; the construction exclusive (2AK.3) |
| R4 | TURN the sign | given becomes not mine to give | Rev 3:21 and Matt 20:23 | read; exclusive (2AK.3) |
| R5 | EXCHANGE the side | the one outside the door | Rev 3:20 and Luke 13:25 | read; exclusive (2AK.3) |
| R6 | STAGE the collision | two sayings set side by side are told as a dispute | Rev 22:12–13 and Matt 20:1–16 | held under seal: together 6 of 6, apart 0 of 6, on three models (2AG, 2AH) |
| R7 | ENACT the title | a title's ordered terms become the order of an act | inside R6 | held within R6's runs |
| R8 | EXCHANGE the faithful's part for the teacher's | trial, holding fast, bowing | Rev 2:10, 2:13, 3:9 and Luke 4:2, Mark 14:1, Matt 4:10 | read tonight, untested (2AL.2.5) |

1. R1, R1′, R3, R5 and R8 are one operation seen at different seats: a relation keeps its act and one of its terms is replaced by the term from the opposite side. R2 and R4 are one operation: the act or its sign is replaced by its opposite. R6 and R7 are the only operators that make narrative out of statement. So the algorithm has three kinds of move: **exchange a term across the sides, oppose the act, stage a statement.**
2. What is not built, and is the whole of the author's aim: these three moves applied to every relation of Revelation by rule, giving a predicted list of relations; a Gospel's relations extracted by the same rule; and the two compared with the measures the James derivation already uses on the site (coverage, specificity, uniformity, complexity, residue, ordering), with the residue shown. The seat test of 2AI.3 is that comparison for one seat and one side. 2AJ showed that nouns and persons from tags do not reach the other seats; the extraction needs clauses, with subject and object, which the tagged text can give for the third person and a hand must give for speech.
3. The control that the whole must pass is now sharper than the 23 other assignments: **Acts**. Any count that puts a Gospel in a transformed relation to Revelation has to be run on Acts by the same rule, and tonight Acts has come first twice (the door, the custody).

## 2AM. The relations whole, built by machine, and what it found

At 22:42 ("lets build it").

### 2AM.1 The build

1. Rule E, sealed at 22:43 with five predictions, before the extraction was run. One rule for Revelation, each Gospel and Acts: verses cut into clauses at punctuation; for each verb the agent is the nearest seat-term in the nominative, or the seat of its person; the patient the nearest in the accusative, else the dative; the passive turned round, with ὑπό for its agent. Seat-terms are the sealed lexicons and αὐτός resolved to the last-named seat. Seats: GOD, CHRIST, ANGEL, FAITHFUL, ADVERSARY, ALL, and in Revelation the SEER, counted with the faithful.
2. Yield: Revelation 426 relations; Matthew 912; Mark 626; Luke 835; John 968; Acts 1,216. With both agent and patient: Revelation 29, the others 74 to 193.
3. Read for accuracy, Revelation's adversary as patient, 56 events: the custody is all there (19:20 πιάζω; 20:2 κρατέω, δέω; 20:3 βάλλω, κλείω, σφραγίζω, δεῖ; 20:7 λύω), with the worship (13:4, 13:12, 14:9, 14:11, 20:4), the casting down (12:9, 12:13), the burning and hating (17:16), the judging (19:2). Also in the list and wrong: κατοικέω four times, ποιέω seven, ζάω, πάρειμι, where an accusative stood near a verb it does not belong to. About two in five are noise. Of the 29 with both seats, several are wrong by the pronoun (2:26, 12:11, 20:10).

### 2AM.2 The five predictions

A relation of Revelation counts for a cell if its verb stands in the target book with the target seat in the same slot. Expectation and z come from the verbs redistributed over Revelation's relations.

| | Prediction | Matthew | Mark | Luke | John | Acts | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| W1 | patient, ADVERSARY → CHRIST, z > 2 in John and Matthew | +0.26 | +0.86 | −0.57 | +0.83 | +0.74 | failed |
| | patient, ADVERSARY → ADVERSARY (to be lower) | +0.17 | −0.56 | −0.23 | +1.20 | +0.04 | in John it is higher |
| W2 | patient, FAITHFUL → CHRIST, z > 2 in two Gospels | −0.16 | +0.20 | +1.45 | +0.22 | +1.89 | failed |
| W3 | agent, ADVERSARY → ADVERSARY, z > 2 in two Gospels | −1.65 | −1.62 | −1.77 | −1.29 | −0.65 | failed, and negative throughout |
| W4 | Acts' patient ADVERSARY → FAITHFUL at least the best Gospel's ADVERSARY → CHRIST | | | | | +0.56 against Mark's +0.86 | failed as stated; neither is distinguishable from nothing |
| W5 | map H lands above identity in John and Matthew, and in the top twentieth of 11,250 maps | H +0.65, identity +0.26; rank 3,900 | | | H +2.08, identity +1.59; rank 1,459 | H +0.06; rank 5,965 | first half held, second failed |

Map H: patients ADVERSARY → CHRIST and FAITHFUL → CHRIST; agents ANGEL → ADVERSARY. In John it is in the first eighth of the maps and not the first twentieth. The maps that stand first in John (z +3.5 to +3.7) are of no sense (patients ALL → ANGEL); at that height the ranking is of noise.

### 2AM.3 What this does to 2AI.3

1. 2AI.3 held on a cosine of rarity-weighted verb profiles, with the Gospels' role labels shuffled, on two extractions (p 0.004 and 0.012). The same question on this extraction, by presence of the verb and with Revelation's verbs redistributed, gives +0.83 in John and +0.26 in Matthew.
2. Tried after the fact, and so worth only this much: with the common verbs removed (those in more than 40 relations of the six books), ADVERSARY → CHRIST is +1.06 in John, +0.98 in Matthew, +1.18 in Mark, −0.46 in Luke. The sign is right in three and the height is not there.
3. So 2AI.3 stands as a result on one kind of instrument and has failed to come again on another. It is a lead with a count behind it. The state table above says "held under seal"; it should now be read with this row beside it.
4. The lists behind the cells, with rare verbs only, are the plainest thing the build gives. What is done to the adversary in Revelation and to Jesus in John: βάλλω, βαστάζω, βλέπω, δέω, δεῖ, ζάω, καλέω, κλείω, λύω, μιμνήσκω, πειράζω, πιάζω (23 of 42). In Matthew: βάλλω, δέω, δεῖ, κάθημαι, καλέω, κρατέω, πειράζω, προσκυνέω (18). To Acts' faithful: δέω, δεῖ, ζάω, κατοικέω, κλαίω, κρίνω, κρατέω, λύω, πάρειμι, πειράζω, προσκυνέω and more (20). The custody is in all three. Counted as verbs against a book's whole vocabulary, it is too small a part of anything to move a z.

### 2AM.4 Where the build stands at 22:50

1. Four attempts tonight at a mapping of seats over the whole have failed under seal: the seats kept (2AI.4), the persons (2AJ), the turning as a mark (2AL.2, held and emptied), and this. One held and has now not repeated (2AI.3). Order failed three ways before them.
2. What has held, and held with controls that could have broken it, is of another kind: a seed of two clauses, a transform stated as forced consequences, and a narrative generated from it by substrates that were shown to need both clauses (2AG, 2AH). That is the method of #1486 and #1494. It is a program for one place.
3. The inference for the author's aim. A generative algorithm for a Gospel as a whole has not been found as a single mapping that a count over the books can see. On tonight's evidence, if it exists it is a set of local programs sharing a small number of moves (2AL.4.1), and the test of the whole is the one the James derivation on the site already uses: how many of a Gospel's pericopes are reached from seeds in Revelation by those moves, how uniform the rules are, and what is left over, shown.
4. The next pack is given by tonight. Seed: Rev 20:1–3 with 20:7, the custody. Moves: the patient exchanged; the helper's act opposed. Two keys sealed, not one: Matthew 27:62–28:9 and Acts 12:1–17. Cuts as in 2AH: the seed without the angel; the seed without the loosing. The question it can answer is the one the counts cannot: given the seed and the moves and nothing else, which narrative do the substrates write, the tomb or the prison.

## 2AN. The author on sign, range and writing; the four relations of the riders

### 2AN.1 The author, 22:59 to 23:03

1. 22:59, on 2AM: "this assumes its trying to tell the same story at all points, when it may be trying to tell different stories at different points. jesus movement. anyone who speaks w the voice and has received the white stone speaks as jesus. the books are allegorical histories of those speakings. there is some history in there somewhere. acrs is probably the most straightforward in this sense, though not straightforward at all. so if an event is behind a visionary sequence, and a gospel is telling the story of how that unfolded, the sign could shift by event, not necessarily total. but im simply giving a scenario in which the sign would relationed but not fixed."
2. 23:00, on my answer, which still grouped Revelation by its scenes in order: "no. ive tried to express variable ranges before, you will fail every time if youre looking only for 1-22".
3. 23:01, on my answer, which put ranges in place of order: "what works like that? when you arranged my typing for through anothers mouth, you changed the order, did you not? thats not how writing works".
4. 23:02: "take the four riders and four creatures. you will find fours that structure revelation by those themes and structures. then find out which strand maps to which gospel". 23:03: "you have to first find the four relation structures in revelation".
5. What this corrects in the file. Every whole-book test from 2Y to 2AM took one mapping to hold from end to end, or took the source to be passed through in pieces. Mark with Matthew, the file's "known-direct" case, is a copy, and it is the only pair in 2AL.1 that keeps relations as they were (12 per cent changed). A composition from a source held whole in mind is another kind of thing, and the file has no calibration of that kind. One is to hand: the dialogue Through Another's Mouth, composed from the author's typing with the order changed.

### 2AN.2 The four relations

1. 3.1 has the pairing of creature and rider from the text. What each rider does, from the tagged text of 6:1–8, as a relation:

| | Creature | Rider | Given | Does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | lion | white, a bow | a crown (ἐδόθη αὐτῷ στέφανος) | goes out conquering, and to conquer |
| 2 | calf | red | to take peace from the earth; a great sword (ἐδόθη twice) | that they slay one another |
| 3 | human face | black, a balance in hand | nothing is given; a voice from among the four speaks | a measure of wheat for a denarius; the oil and the wine not to be harmed |
| 4 | eagle flying | pale, named Death | authority over a fourth of the earth (ἐδόθη) | to kill; Hades follows |

2. So four relations: **conquer; slay; buy and sell at a measure; death and what follows it.** Three are given, in the passive with no giver named. The third alone is held and spoken.
3. Each relation through Revelation, every verse, with who bears it (verses from the tagged text; bearers read):
   a. **Conquer** (νικάω, στέφανος), 22 verses. The faithful: the seven promises (2:7, 2:11, 2:17, 2:26, 3:5, 3:12, 3:21), the crowns (2:10, 3:11), 12:11, 15:2, 21:7. The speaker and the Lamb: 3:21, 5:5 (ἐνίκησεν ὁ λέων), 14:14, 17:14. The rider: 6:2. The adversary: 11:7, 13:7.
   b. **Slay** (σφάζω, μάχαιρα), 10 verses. The Lamb slain: 5:6, 5:9, 5:12, 13:8. The faithful slain: 6:9, 18:24. The rider: 6:4. The adversary: its head slain (13:3), its sword-wound (13:14), and 13:10, where the one who kills by the sword must by the sword be killed.
   c. **Buy and sell** (ἀγοράζω, πωλέω, ἔμπορος, δηνάριον, ζυγός), 11 verses. The speaker: "buy from me" (3:18). The Lamb: "you bought for God by your blood" (5:9); the bought (14:3, 14:4). The rider: 6:5, 6:6. The adversary: none may buy or sell without the mark (13:17); the merchants of Babylon (18:3, 18:11, 18:15, 18:23).
   d. **Death** (θάνατος, ᾅδης, ἀποκτείνω), 27 verses. The speaker holds the keys (1:18). The faithful: 2:10, 2:11, 2:13, 6:11, 11:7, 12:11, 20:6. The rider: 6:8. The adversary kills: 13:10, 13:15; and is struck: 13:3, 13:12, 18:8, 19:21. The end of it: 20:13, 20:14, 21:4, 21:8.
4. Every one of the four has bearers on both sides. That is the author's "relationed but not fixed" as a property of Revelation itself, before any Gospel is opened: the relation is constant and the seat that holds it changes with the place.

### 2AN.3 Where the four stand together

1. Every window of twelve verses in Revelation, searched for all four relations at once. Two places and no others: **5:11–6:13** and **13:6–13:18**. Together 30 of the book's 405 verses.
2. The first is the riders themselves, with the song before them, which already has three of the four in two verses: ἐνίκησεν ὁ λέων (5:5); ἐσφάγης καὶ ἠγόρασας (5:9).
3. The second is the beast, and it is the four again in the other hand, in the riders' own passive:
   - ἐδόθη αὐτῷ ποιῆσαι πόλεμον μετὰ τῶν ἁγίων καὶ νικῆσαι αὐτούς (13:7): conquer.
   - εἴ τις ἐν μαχαίρῃ ἀποκτενεῖ, δεῖ αὐτὸν ἐν μαχαίρῃ ἀποκτανθῆναι (13:10): the sword, and the slaying of one another said as a law.
   - ἵνα μή τις δύνηται ἀγοράσαι ἢ πωλῆσαι (13:17): buy and sell.
   - 13:15, that as many as do not bow be killed: death.
   - And the creatures: the beast's mouth "as a lion's" (13:2); its number "a human's number" (13:18).
4. So the fours do structure the book in the way the author said: the four relations are set out once as riders under the Lamb's opening, and once as the beast's powers. Between and around them each relation runs separately, changing hands (2AN.2.3).
5. F1, sealed: the strands' seed words, taken by distinctiveness, stand together outside the seeds more than shuffled sets do: 33 per cent of pairs within a strand against 25, p 0.065. Not below 0.05; failed as sealed. The lexicon so made was poor: it held δεύτερος, τρίτος, τρεῖς, ἑπτά, ἄλλος and μέσος. The relations of 2AN.2 were found by reading what each rider does, after that lexicon was seen.

### 2AN.4 Which strand, which Gospel

1. F2, sealed with no assignment predicted: the seed lexicons counted by Gospel, observed over expected, the best of 24 assignments reported with the share of shuffled lexicons that do as well. Best: lion with Mark, calf with John, human face with Luke, eagle with Matthew; share 0.056. **Not distinguishable from chance.**
2. After the fact, by a rule made on seeing that table: numerals, ἄλλος and μέσος removed, and the ceiling of rarity varied.

| Ceiling (verses of the New Testament) | Lion, white | Calf, red | Human face, black | Eagle, pale | Shuffled share |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 20 | John | Luke | Matthew | Mark | 0.016 |
| 40 | John | Mark | Matthew | Luke | 0.23 |
| 60 | John | Mark | Matthew | Luke | 0.23 |
| 100 | John | Luke | Matthew | Mark | 0.001 |
| 150 | John | Luke | Matthew | Mark | 0.001 |

3. Stable at every setting: **the lion's strand with John, the human face's with Matthew.** The calf's and the eagle's go to Luke and Mark and change places at two settings. The counts under them are small: at the lowest ceiling the calf's strand has three tokens in the four Gospels, all Luke's.
4. What the stable two rest on, each lemma counted Matthew/Mark/Luke/John:
   - Lion, white, conquer: νικάω 0/0/1/1; στέφανος 1/1/0/2; ἀρνίον 0/0/0/1; λευκός 3/2/1/2. John's νικάω is the speaker's own: ἐγὼ νενίκηκα τὸν κόσμον (16:33), against Rev 3:21 and 5:5. This is thin: three tokens of the relation's own words.
   - Human face, black, the balance: δηνάριον 6/3/3/2; ζυγός 2/0/0/0; σῖτος 4/1/3/1; ἔλαιον 3/1/3/0; μέλας 1/0/0/0; with ἀγοράζω 7/5/5/3, πωλέω 6/3/6/2, ἔμπορος 1/0/0/0. The relation's markers in Matthew: 22, at 12.0 per 10,000 words; Mark 9.8; Luke 7.2; John 4.5; Acts 2.2.
5. And the others, as far as they go:
   - Calf, red: μόσχος 0/0/3/0, all in Luke 15 (φέρετε τὸν μόσχον τὸν σιτευτόν, θύσατε); εἰρήνη 4/1/13/6; μάχαιρα 7/3/5/2. The rider takes peace from the earth. Luke 12:51: δοκεῖτε ὅτι εἰρήνην παρεγενόμην δοῦναι ἐν τῇ γῇ; Matt 10:34 has the same saying with the rider's other gift, οὐκ ἦλθον βαλεῖν εἰρήνην ἀλλὰ μάχαιραν, which 3.4 noted. The second strand is divided between them: Luke keeps the peace taken, Matthew the sword given.
   - Eagle, pale: θηρίον 0/1/0/0 (Mark 1:13, ἦν μετὰ τῶν θηρίων); χλωρός 0/1/0/0 (Mark 6:39); ᾅδης 2/0/2/0; ἀετός 1/0/1/0; λιμός 1/1/4/0. Mark has two of the rider's rare words and neither Hades nor the eagle.
6. One line across to what held tonight. The third rider's price is χοῖνιξ σίτου δηναρίου (6:6): a day's grain for a denarius. Matt 20:2: συμφωνήσας … ἐκ δηναρίου τὴν ἡμέραν. The same genitive of price, a denarius for the day. 2AG declared the denarius outside what Rev 22:12–13 could produce, and no run wrote it. It has a seed, in the strand that goes to Matthew, and it is another range of Revelation than the one the story was generated from. That is one composition drawing on two places of its source at once, as the author says writing does.
