7. The assignment at three settings of five (John, Luke, Matthew, Mark in the creatures' order) is the one I recall as Irenaeus's at 3.11.8. That is from memory and unverified in the seat (3.3, 2AD.4); if it is his, the earliest reader of the four Gospels off the four creatures and a count of the riders' words agree, and the count was not made to agree, since its sealed form gave something else.

### 2AN.5 Standing, and what follows

1. Found, by reading and then counted: four relations, one to a rider, each running through Revelation in both hands, meeting twice.
2. Not shown: the assignment to Gospels. Two of four are stable after the fact and rest on few words.
3. The author's order of work is now the file's: a relation structure of Revelation first, found in Revelation; then where it goes. The next instance is to hand in 2AN.3: the beast's chapter is the riders turned. Each of its four (13:7, 13:10, 13:15, 13:17) is a relation whose other bearers are already listed. The question for each Gospel is which bearer of each relation it tells: for the third, whether Matthew's buying and selling is the Lamb's (bought by blood), the speaker's (buy from me), the rider's (a denarius), or Babylon's (the merchants); and so for each.

## 2AO. Irenaeus in the seat; the third relation bearer by bearer

At 23:13 ("yup. further").

### 2AO.1 Irenaeus, found

1. 3.3 and 2AD.4 left Adv. haer. 3.11.8 as "not yet found" in the seat. It is in `irenaeus-adversus-haereses`, Harvey's second volume, Greek fragment with the Latin under it (lines 4622–4790 of the seated OCR; the Greek is broken by the scan and is given here where it is whole).
2. The creatures, in Revelation's order: τὸ μὲν γὰρ πρῶτον ζῶον, φησὶν, ὅμοιον λέοντι· τὸ ἔμπρακτον αὐτοῦ καὶ ἡγεμονικὸν καὶ βασιλικὸν χαρακτηρίζον· τὸ δὲ δεύτερον ὅμοιον μόσχῳ, τὴν ἱερουργικὴν καὶ ἱερατικὴν τάξιν ἐμφαῖνον· τὸ δὲ τρίτον ἔχον πρόσωπον ἀνθρώπου … τὸ δὲ τέταρτον ὅμοιον ἀετῷ πετωμένῳ.
3. The Gospels: John for the first (the generation from the Father, Ἐν ἀρχῇ ἦν ὁ Λόγος); τὸ δὲ κατὰ Λουκᾶν, ἅτε ἱερατικοῦ χαρακτῆρος ὑπάρχον, ἀπὸ τοῦ Ζαχαρίου τοῦ ἱερέως θυμιῶντος τῷ Θεῷ ἤρξατο. Ἤδη γὰρ ὁ σιτευτὸς ἡτοιμάζετο μόσχος; Ματθαῖος δὲ τὴν κατὰ ἄνθρωπον αὐτοῦ γέννησιν κηρύττει; Μάρκος δὲ ἀπὸ τοῦ προφητικοῦ πνεύματος, the winged image.
4. So: lion, John; calf, Luke; human face, Matthew; eagle, Mark. It is the assignment 2AN.4 reached at three settings of five, and I had it right from memory. Two things in it bear on the count. He reads the Gospels off the creatures of Revelation 4:7, by ordinal, as the file does. And his ground for Luke is the fatted calf of Luke 15, which is the whole of what the count has for the calf at its strictest setting (μόσχος 0/0/3/0). Count and reader rest on the same three words there, so the agreement on Luke is one observation and not two.

### 2AO.2 Buying and selling: four bearers in Revelation

From 2AN.2.3 c, each with its relation as the text has it:

| | Bearer | Verse | The relation |
|---|---|---|---|
| a | the speaker | 3:17–18 | to one who says "I am rich" and is poor: buy from me gold, that you may be rich; white garments; salve |
| b | the Lamb | 5:9; 14:3–4 | you bought for God by your blood; the bought from the earth |
| c | the rider | 6:5–6 | a balance; a measure of wheat for a denarius, three of barley for a denarius; the oil and the wine unharmed |
| d | the adversary | 13:15–17; 18:3–23 | an image; none may buy or sell without the mark; the merchants, and their cargo no one buys |

### 2AO.3 Each bearer, through every book

Each construction below was searched in every verse of the tagged New Testament. The lists are complete.

**a. The speaker's.** ἀγοράζω or πωλέω within three verses of πλούσιος, πλουτέω, θησαυρός or πτωχός: Rev 3:18, 13:17; Matt 13:44, 13:46, 19:21; Mark 10:21; Luke 12:33, 14:18–19, 18:22; John 13:29. No letter, and not Acts by these verbs.
   - Rev 3:17–18: λέγεις ὅτι πλούσιός εἰμι … σὺ εἶ … πτωχός … συμβουλεύω σοι ἀγοράσαι παρ' ἐμοῦ χρυσίον … ἵνα πλουτήσῃς.
   - Matt 19:21: ὕπαγε πώλησόν σου τὰ ὑπάρχοντα καὶ δὸς πτωχοῖς, καὶ ἕξεις θησαυρὸν ἐν οὐρανοῖς; and 19:23, πλούσιος δυσκόλως εἰσελεύσεται.
   - The rich one who is poor is told to buy; the rich one is told to sell and give to the poor. The act opposed (2AL.4, the second move), the end the same: to be rich in what the speaker has.
   - And where it stands in Matthew. 19:21 opens the stretch that 19:27 continues ("what then shall we have?"), which is the occasion of the labourers. In that stretch are 19:28, καθίσεσθε καὶ αὐτοὶ ἐπὶ δώδεκα θρόνους (Rev 3:21, the seat given), and 20:23, the seat not his to give (2AK.3). So Matthew 19:16–20:28 holds, from the close of the letter to Laodicea alone: the rich one who must trade (3:17–18), the seat given (3:21), the seat refused (3:21 turned); and between them the labourers, from Rev 22:12–13, paid in the third rider's coin. Three places of Revelation in one stretch of a Gospel.

**b. The Lamb's.** ἀγοράζω within one verse of αἷμα: **Rev 5:9 and Matt 27:7, and nowhere else.**
   - Rev 5:9: ἐσφάγης καὶ ἠγόρασας τῷ θεῷ ἐν τῷ αἵματί σου ἐκ πάσης φυλῆς.
   - Matt 27:6–8: τιμὴ αἵματός ἐστιν … ἠγόρασαν ἐξ αὐτῶν τὸν ἀγρὸν τοῦ κεραμέως εἰς ταφὴν τοῖς ξένοις … ἀγρὸς αἵματος.
   - By the same blood: the Lamb buys people of every tribe for God; the chief priests buy a field for the burial of strangers. The buyer is exchanged across the sides (the first move) and what is bought is the dead for the living.
   - Only Matthew has it. Acts has the field of blood with another verb and buyer (1:18–19, ἐκτήσατο χωρίον, Judas), and a people got by blood with another verb (20:28, περιεποιήσατο).

**c. The rider's.** His verse, 6:6, has five things: a measure of wheat, a denarius, barley, oil, wine.
   - δηνάριον outside Revelation is in the Gospels only (Matthew 6, Mark 3, Luke 3, John 2).
   - **The denarius as the price of a day:** Matt 20:2, ἐκ δηναρίου τὴν ἡμέραν (2AN.4.6). With it Matthew alone of the Gospels has the rider's ζυγός: ἄρατε τὸν ζυγόν μου … ὁ γὰρ ζυγός μου χρηστός (11:29–30).
   - **The oil and the wine:** οἶνος with ἔλαιον in one verse stands at Rev 6:6, Rev 18:13 and Luke 10:34, and nowhere else: ἐπιχέων ἔλαιον καὶ οἶνον, with δύο δηνάρια in the next verse. 2AE.5 predicted this place from the law of keeping; here it is counted. Luke 10:34 also has ἐπὶ τὸ ἴδιον κτῆνος, and Rev 18:13 runs οἶνον καὶ ἔλαιον … καὶ κτήνη.
   - **The barley:** κριθή or κρίθινος within three verses of δηνάριον stands at Rev 6:6 and John 6:9, and nowhere else: διακοσίων δηναρίων ἄρτοι οὐκ ἀρκοῦσιν (6:7); πέντε ἄρτους κριθίνους (6:9). Barley is in no other book of the New Testament.
   - So the one verse is divided among three Gospels: the coin for the day to Matthew, the oil and wine to Luke, the barley to John. In each the price is undone: a full day's coin for an hour; oil and wine poured out and two denarii left with more promised; bread for five thousand where two hundred denarii would not buy it. "Do not harm the oil and the wine" is, in Luke, oil and wine on wounds.

**d. The adversary's.**
   - ἀγοράζω with πωλέω in one verse: Rev 13:17; Matt 13:44, 21:12, 25:9; Mark 11:15; Luke 17:28, 22:36. No verse outside Revelation and the Gospels. Rev 13:17, none may buy or sell but one who has the mark; Matt 21:12, ἐξέβαλεν πάντας τοὺς πωλοῦντας καὶ ἀγοράζοντας ἐν τῷ ἱερῷ. The beast shuts out of the market all but its own; the teacher shuts the market out of the temple.
   - εἰκών within three verses of buying, selling or a denarius: Rev 13:14–15; Matt 22:20; Mark 12:16; Luke 20:24, and nowhere else. The image that all must bow to, and the mark for buying; τίνος ἡ εἰκὼν αὕτη καὶ ἡ ἐπιγραφή; of the coin.
   - The merchant with the pearl (ἔμπορος and its family within a verse of μαργαρίτης): Rev 18:11–12, 18:15–16; **Matt 13:45**, and nowhere else. Babylon's merchants weep that no one buys their cargo of gold and precious stone and pearls; Matthew's merchant, seeking fine pearls, sells all he has and buys one. ἔμπορος itself is in Matthew and Revelation alone; its family adds Matt 22:5 (ἐπὶ τὴν ἐμπορίαν, in the wedding, whose seed 2V took from Rev 19), John 2:16 (οἶκον ἐμπορίου), James 4:13, 2 Peter 2:3.

### 2AO.4 By Gospel

| Bearer | Matthew | Mark | Luke | John | Acts |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| a. the speaker's: trade and be rich | 19:21; 13:44, 13:46 | 10:21 | 12:33; 18:22 | 13:29 | by other verbs: 4:34, selling and laying down |
| b. the Lamb's: bought by blood | 27:6–8 | | | | by other verbs: 1:18–19; 20:28 |
| c. the rider's: the price | 20:2; 11:29–30 | | 10:34–35 | 6:7–9 | |
| d. the adversary's: the market, the image, the merchant | 21:12; 22:20; 13:45; 22:5 | 11:15; 12:16 | 17:28; 20:24; 22:36 | 2:14–16 | |

1. Matthew tells all four bearers, and alone tells the second. This is the ground under 2AN.4's stable pairing of the human face's strand with Matthew, and it is firmer than the count of words: it is every bearer of the relation, each by a construction with a complete list behind it.
2. It answers 2AN.5.3. Matthew's buying and selling is all four bearers at once, each turned its own way: the speaker's act opposed (buy, sell), the Lamb's buyer exchanged (the priests), the rider's price undone (a day's coin for an hour), the adversary's market emptied (the temple) and its merchant turned (one pearl). The sign is set per bearer, as the author said it would be.
3. What this is and is not. The lists are counts, complete over the tagged text, and anyone can rerun them. That each pair is a turning is a reading. The search was made with the strand in view, and 2Z showed that pairings exclusive to Revelation and the Gospels are common (about sixty to a hundred verses), so no single one of these is surprising. What is not common is one relation of Revelation having every one of its bearers answered in one Gospel. The control owed is the same table for the other three relations and for Acts, made by the same rule, before it is said of Matthew that it is the third strand's Gospel.

## 2AP. Revelation to Matthew, whole: the ledger and the map

At 23:31 ("and we are tracking these relations carefully as we go? fine on the control - but we need a structural mapping of rev to matthew"; "as a whole").

### 2AP.1 On tracking

1. Checked before answering. Until this section the relations found tonight (2AK to 2AO) were in the prose of this file and in no data. The engine held 73 derivations entered earlier, 18 with a target in Matthew; the relations module held the pairs of 2J to 2P. Nothing found since 2AF had been entered in either.
2. Made now: `rebuild/unfolding-map/map/rev-matthew-ledger.json`. One row to a relation: the place in Matthew; the place or places in Revelation (any range, any number of them); the relation as Revelation has it; the relation as Matthew has it; the move; the strand or cluster; the standing, with the section that established it; and the lemmas that must stand in both places.
3. Checked by `ledger.py` on every run: each required lemma is looked for in the tagged text of both places. 34 rows entered by hand, 34 pass. One failed on first entry (the name Ἡρῴδης, spelt otherwise in the tagged text) and was re-entered on παιδίον and ἀπόλλυμι. The 18 engine rows carry the engine's own verdicts.
4. From here a relation is entered in the ledger when it is found, and the file's prose cites the row.

1. **The two ends.** Chapter 2 has two rows, both from Revelation 12 (the child, the adversary seeking it, the flight; the one who is to shepherd). Chapters 27 and 28 have ten, from Revelation 1, 5, 6, 11, 16 and 20 (as dead, fear not; bought with blood; the crown; the witnesses' death and rising; the earthquake; bound, sealed, the angel descending). 2AH.4 tried this as an order for the whole and it failed. As two places in Matthew's frame it is in the ledger row by row.
2. **The middle is the letters, the riders and the city.** Chapters 10 and 11: the second and third riders (the sword for peace; the yoke), the confession (3:5), the twelve names (21:14). Chapters 16 and 17: Revelation 1 twice (the keys; the face as the sun) and the wage. Chapters 19 and 20: the close of Laodicea three times, the city, the wage, the third rider's coin.
3. **Jerusalem is the beast, the harvest, Babylon and the wedding.** Chapter 21: the market (13:17), the winepress (14:19). Chapter 22: the wedding from 17–20, the image on the coin (13). Chapter 23: Babylon's cup and blood. That is Revelation 13 to 19 in Matthew 21 to 23, and it is the one stretch where the parts of Revelation come in something like their own sequence.
4. **The passion gathers every hand.** Chapter 26: the speaker's line in the betrayer's mouth (3:19), the beast's law of the sword as the teacher's saying (13:10), the dragon's seizing (20:2), the time near (1:3). Chapter 27: the Lamb's purchase done by the priests (5:9), the first rider's crown as thorns (6:2), the dragon's binding (20:2) and seal (20:3), the witnesses' death (11). What Revelation divides among the Lamb, the faithful, the riders and the adversary is in these two chapters all done to one person.
5. **What has no row.** Chapters 1, 6, 7, 9, 12, 14, 15: the genealogy; most of the Sermon; the healings; the controversies over the sabbath and Beelzebul; the feedings; the Canaanite. Chapters 3, 5 and 8 have one row each, and chapter 3's came from the machine layer (2AP.4.3). Roughly, the narrative Matthew shares with Mark and the body of the Sermon are unreached.

2. By part of Revelation, per verse of that part: chapter 20 highest (2.13), then 6 (1.47), 19 (1.14), 8–11 (1.00); the city lowest (0.31). By chapter of Matthew, per verse: 3 and 18 (0.71), 4 (0.68), 17 (0.65), 7 (0.59), 24 (0.55), 23 (0.50), 13 (0.45); the lowest are 12, 28, 6, 11, 19.
3. Its largest cells are single relations repeated, and two of them were not in the ledger:
   - **Thrown into the fire.** βάλλω with πῦρ in one verse stands in Revelation (8:5, 8:7, 8:8, 19:20, 20:10, 20:14, 20:15) and the Gospels (Matt 3:10, 7:19, 13:42, 13:50, 18:8, 18:9; Mark 9:22; Luke 3:9, 12:49; John 15:6) and in no other book. Six of the ten Gospel verses are Matthew's. In Revelation what is thrown into the fire is the beast, the devil, death and Hades, and whoever is not written; in Matthew it is the tree without fruit, the lawless within the kingdom, and "you", by hand or eye. The patient is exchanged from the adversary to the hearers. Two rows entered from this (20:14–15 with 13:41–42; 8:7 with 3:10), which gives chapters 3, 7, 13 and 18 a relation the reading had not.
   - **Seated on the throne.** Rev 4–5 with Matt 23:22, eight pairings, all θρόνος with κάθημαι: "who swears by heaven swears by the throne of God and by him who sits on it." One verse.
   - The wedding (Rev 19 with Matt 22, six), which the engine has.
4. The machine layer and the ledger agree where both speak (the wedding; chapter 24; the fire) and the machine layer speaks where the ledger is silent (chapters 3, 7, 18). It is coarse: it counts a relation once for every verse on either side.

### 2AP.5 What the whole is, at 23:35

1. A map of Matthew against Revelation now exists as data, in Matthew's order, each row a relation with its move and its standing. It reaches about a ninth of Matthew by rows that have been read and checked, about a fifth with the machine's.
2. Its shape, as far as it goes: Matthew begins from Revelation 12 and ends from Revelation 11 and 20; its teaching in the middle draws on the letters, the riders and the city; its Jerusalem chapters draw on Revelation 13 to 19; and its passion turns onto one person what Revelation distributes among all its bearers.
3. By standing: one row held under a sealed generative test (the labourers). The custody held on one instrument and not another. Some fifteen rest on constructions exclusive to Revelation and the Gospels, with complete lists. The rest are read. The eighteen engine rows passed their controls when entered.
4. What it is not. It is not a derivation of Matthew. Four-fifths of Matthew has no row. No rule yet says which place of Revelation a given place of Matthew will draw on, or which move will be applied; the rows record that a move was applied. And the direction of every row is open: each is a relation between two texts.
5. The residue is the next work, and it is specific: the Sermon (5–7), the miracles (8–9, 14–15), the controversies (12). The author at 13:09 on the first of these to be tried: "in john the seven curses reverse into the miracles." The bowls and trumpets against Matthew 8–9 and 14–15 is that test for Matthew, and the machine layer already shows Revelation 8–11 as the part with the most pairings in Matthew 7, 13, 17 and 18.

## 2AQ. The transform, stated: T_Μ for Matthew, version 0.3

At 23:39 ("and then a transform algorithm, a description of the textual transform that generated matthew from rev").

### 2AQ.0 What this is

1. A description, in the form of #1494's signature and the T_Θ pack, of the transform that takes Revelation to Matthew, written from the ledger of 2AP. Each clause cites the rows it is drawn from and says how it stands. It is version 0.3: version 0.1 was the pack for one seed (2AF), version 0.2 the three moves of 2AL.4.
2. It is induced. The rows were found first and the rules read off them, and the rows reach 119 of Matthew's 1,067 verses. One clause (F1) has been run forward under seal and held. The rest describe; they have not yet generated.
3. The direction is the programme's. Every row is a relation between two texts, and the same table read from right to left is a description of how a seer would compress Matthew. Nothing here decides between them (2AH.3.2).

### 2AQ.1 The source and its state

**S.** The source is Revelation, held whole and open. It is not passed through. Any place of it may be drawn on at any place of the output, several at once, and the same place more than once (2AN.1). Its unit is the relation: who does what to whom, in what mode, with the bearer it has at that place. One relation has several bearers in the source (2AN.2.3), and the transform acts on a relation with one of its bearers.

### 2AQ.2 Two parameters

**P1. The speaker is given a past.** The one who speaks from heaven in the first person, coming, becomes a teacher who went about a land, with followers, and was killed there. Consequence, and it governs everything below: there is now one person to whom things can be done. Whatever the source has done to anyone, on either side, can be told of him.

**P2. The host is a told life in a named land, anchored to scripture.** Third-person past; the adversary given the land's names (the dragon who waits for the child is Herod, 2:13–16; the image is Caesar's on a coin, 22:20; those who seize and bind are the chief priests and the governor); and where the source's wording is scripture's, it is cited as written (Rev 12:5, "who is to shepherd", as Micah at 2:6).

### 2AQ.3 The conditions and their moves

For each relation drawn on, the move is fixed by the kind of relation it is in the source.

| | If, in Revelation, the relation is | then, in Matthew | Rows |
|---|---|---|---|
| **M1** | a saying of the speaker in the first person, a promise, a warning or a formula | **KEEP it, as a saying of the teacher.** The words cross; the person does not change | the thief (3:3 at 24:43); the confession (3:5 at 10:32); to each as his work (22:12 at 16:27); who has ears (2:7 at 13:9); to sit on thrones (3:21 at 19:28); the sword's law (13:10 at 26:52); hunger and thirst no more (7:16 at 5:6). 14 rows keep |
| **M2** | a deed done to the adversary | **EXCHANGE the patient: it is done to the teacher,** by the adversary's people in the land. Where the deed is the last judgment it is done to the hearers who fail | seized (20:2 at 26:50); bound (20:2 at 27:2); sealed, and the angel descending (20:1–3 at 27:63–28:2); thrown into the fire (20:14–15 at 13:41–42); the millstone in the sea (18:21 at 18:6) |
| **M3** | a gift or act of the speaker or the Lamb toward the faithful, of something of price | **it is done by the adversary to the teacher, or it is refused.** The line moves to the other mouth, or the sign turns | whom I love (3:19) as the betrayer's kiss (26:48); bought by blood (5:9) as the priests' purchase (27:6–8); I will give to sit (3:21) as not mine to give (20:23) |
| **M4** | a rider's charge, given in the passive | **the teacher takes the rider's seat and says it of himself,** or it is turned on him | not peace but a sword (6:4 at 10:34); my yoke (6:5 at 11:29–30); the crown, of thorns (6:2 at 27:29); the price, as a wage (6:6 at 20:2) |
| **M5** | commerce, an image, or wealth trusted in (the adversary's; or the rich assembly's own) | **OPPOSE it by the teacher's act or word,** keeping its terms | the market cast out (13:17 at 21:12); the image, and what is God's (13:14–17 at 22:19–21); the merchant who sells all for one pearl (18:11–12 at 13:45–46); the rich one told to sell (3:17–18 at 19:21) |
| **M6** | the faithful's part: trial, bowing, the seer's seeing and falling | **the trial goes to the teacher; the seeing and falling go to the followers** | tried by the devil (2:10 at 4:1); who bows to whom (3:9 at 4:9–10); the face as the sun, fell, fear not (1:16–17 at 17:2–7; 1:17 at 28:4–5) |

1. M2, M3 and M6 are one consequence of P1 seen from three sides. Once there is one person with a past, the adversary's sufferings, the Lamb's costly acts and the faithful's trial all have somewhere to land, and they land on him. This is what 2AP.3.4 saw in chapters 26 and 27.
2. M1 and M4 differ by the grammar of the source. What the speaker says in the first person stays a saying. What a rider is given in the passive, with no giver named, becomes the teacher's "I came" or "my".
3. M5 is the only move in which the teacher acts against something. Everywhere else he says, or it is done to him.

### 2AQ.4 The forms

A move gives what is told. The form gives how.

| | If the source has | the output has | Rows | Standing |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **F1 STAGE** | two sayings side by side that cannot both hold in a told economy | a likeness in which they are two voices | wage and first-last (22:12–13) as the labourers (20:1–16) | held under seal: together 6 of 6 runs, apart 0 of 6, three models (2AG, 2AH) |
| **F2 ENACT** | a title or an inscription | an act done in its order, or a list | first and last as the order of payment (20:8); twelve names on twelve stones (21:14) as the names of the twelve (10:2) | the first inside F1's runs; the second read |
| **F3 LIKEN** | a scene of the end: a wedding, a harvest, a throne, a fire | "the kingdom is like", with the scene's members kept and divided | the wedding (17–19 at 22:1–14); the harvest and its angels (14:15 at 13:39); the throne (20:11 at 25:31) | engine, with controls |
| **F4 FORETELL** | a sign in heaven, seen | the same sign, said by the teacher of days to come | the sixth seal (6:12–13 at 24:29); with the clouds (1:7 at 24:30); the eagles (24:28) | read; engine |
| **F5 HAPPEN** | a sign in heaven or earth, seen | an event in the narrative | the earthquake at the tomb (16:18 at 28:2); the storm (6:12 at 8:24); the witnesses' death and rising at the cross (11 at 27:50–55) | engine, with controls |

### 2AQ.5 Composition

**C1. The frame is the source's centre.** The output begins from Revelation 12 (the birth, the adversary, the flight, the shepherd) and ends from Revelation 11 and 20 with 1 (the death in the city, the custody and its opening, "fear not"). Rows at 2:6, 2:13–16; 27:50–28:9.

**C2. One stretch, many places.** A stretch of the output gathers every place of the source that holds its relation, whatever their distance. Matthew 19:16–20:28: the close of Laodicea (3:17–21) three times, the city (21:5–14), the wage (22:12–13), the third rider's coin (6:6). Matthew 22:1–14: Revelation 17:14, 18:8, 19:7–9, 19:14, 19:21, 20:2–3.

**C3. A relation is told with all its bearers.** Where the output takes up one of the four relations it answers each bearer the source gives it, each by its own move. Shown for buying and selling, all four bearers (2AO). Not yet shown for the other three.

**C4. One place, divided.** A single verse of the source may be parted among the Gospels, each taking a member. Revelation 6:6: the day's coin to Matthew, the oil and wine to Luke, the barley to John (2AO.3 c). The witnesses of Revelation 11 are parted among all four (2H, 2I).

**C5. The vision's furniture goes.** Horses, bowls, trumpets, living creatures, the counted thirds do not cross; the rare word that is not Revelation's own does. This is 2AE's law of keeping, learned from 59 derivations with no target in view, and it is #1494's fourth clause.

### 2AQ.6 The program, in order

1. Hold Revelation open. Retype its speaker (P1) and fix the host (P2).
2. Set the frame from 12 at the beginning and from 11, 20 and 1 at the end (C1).
3. For each stretch of the life to be told, gather the places of the source that hold its relation (C2), each with its bearer.
4. For each relation gathered, apply the move its kind requires (M1 to M6).
5. Give it its form (F1 to F5). Where two gathered sayings collide, stage them.
6. Let the vision's furniture go and keep the rare word (C5). Name the adversary from the land; cite what is scripture as written (P2).

### 2AQ.7 What it does not generate

1. The genealogy; the body of the Sermon; the healings and feedings; the controversies (chapters 1, 6, 7, 9, 12, 14, 15 have no row, and 5 and 8 one each). Step 3 says "each stretch of the life to be told" and nothing here says what those stretches are or in what order they come, beyond the frame. The narrative Matthew shares with Mark is outside the transform as it stands.
2. The choice among moves where a relation fits two conditions. The crown is a rider's (M4) and is turned on the teacher as M2 would have it; the seat is kept at 19:28 (M1) and refused at 20:23 (M3). The table records both and does not say why each falls where it does.
3. The wording. Outside F1 the transform says which relation and which terms; it does not produce Matthew's sentences. F1 did produce a passage, and the residue it could not produce is known (2AG.4).

### 2AQ.8 What it predicts

Each is a statement the residue can break, and none has been looked for.

1. **From M1.** The speaker's first-person sayings in Revelation 2–3 and 22 that are not yet in the ledger have each a saying of the teacher in Matthew with their terms kept. To be counted: all such sayings listed by rule from the tagged text, the share found, against the same count for Acts' speeches.
2. **From M5 and the author at 13:09.** The plagues done to the earth and its dwellers (the first four trumpets and the first four bowls: the earth and sores; the sea; rivers and springs; the sun) are opposed in the healings and sea-miracles of Matthew 8–9 and 14–15: each act met by its opposite on the same term, by a table of opposites declared beforehand, as δέω and λύω were (2P).
3. **From F1.** A second pair of sayings that stand side by side in Revelation and cannot both hold in a told economy will, as a pack with cuts, yield a second likeness of Matthew's. The candidate from the ledger: "buy from me … that you may be rich" beside "as many as I love I reprove" (3:18–19), for the stretch of the rich one and what follows.

## 2AR. Three reviews of v0.45, checked; the standalone begun

*Entered 26 November 2026, after 23:50.*

### 2AR.1 What was received

1. Three reports on v0.45, pasted whole: Gemini, DeepSeek, ChatGPT. All three recommend that the Matthew material be written as a standalone paper. ChatGPT proposes the title "How to Write Matthew from Revelation", an order of composition in eight parts, a parallel text in four layers, and a bibliography.
2. The ruling given with them: three deposits proposed (this file at v0.45 as the research record; the standalone; the hologram with the findings integrated); the standalone to be begun now, generating Matthew before the reader, the ledger as its evidence and not its order, the scholarship entering at each decision. Nothing is deposited by this entry.

### 2AR.2 The reports checked

1. DeepSeek gives this file as about 30,000 words in 23 versions. By count it was about 89,200 words at its forty-fifth version.
2. ChatGPT dates Bauckham's study of the parousia parables to 1993 and lists Mathewson and Gurtner as books. By bibliographic record: Bauckham, "Synoptic Parousia Parables and the Apocalypse", *New Testament Studies* 23 (1977), and "Synoptic Parousia Parables Again", *NTS* 29 (1983) 129–134; Mathewson, *Tyndale Bulletin* 62.1 (2011) 89–108, an article; Gurtner, *Bulletin for Biblical Research* 22.4 (2012), an article. (That the 1977 article is reprinted in *The Climax of Prophecy*, 1993, is from memory and would account for the date.) Wenkel, *Expository Times* 132 (2020) 259–270, and Goulder, *Midrash and Lection in Matthew* (1974), are as given.
3. Gemini says the transform accounts for Matthew's special material better than the received sources do. This file does not establish a comparison. It has a reach (2AP: 11 per cent of Matthew's verses by the ledger, 21 with the machine layer) and has not measured any rival account's reach on the same verses.
4. The reports' other statements about this file were not checked one by one in this entry. The three items above are those that were checked and found wrong or unestablished; the recommendation itself needs no check, being a judgment.
5. What is taken from them: the title; the order by acts of composition; the scholarship placed at the decisions. Not taken: the four-layer parallel text as a fixed format (the draft uses two columns and a line for the move, which is what the ledger's rows hold).

### 2AR.3 Counts rerun for the draft

Each over the Tischendorf tagged text, every book. The sword twice in one verse: Matt 26:52, Rev 13:10, and no other. ζυγός: Matt 11:29, 11:30; Acts 15:10; Gal 5:1; 1 Tim 6:1; Rev 6:5; no other Gospel. θερισμός with ἄγγελος in a verse: Matt 13:39, Rev 14:15. βάλλω with πῦρ in a verse: Revelation 7, Matthew 6, Luke 2, Mark 1, John 1, no other book. ἔμπορος within a verse of μαργαρίτης: Matt 13:45; Rev 18:11, 18:15. ἀγοράζω within a verse of αἷμα: Matt 27:7, Rev 5:9. Barley: John 6:9, 6:13; Rev 6:6. ἔλαιον with οἶνος: Luke 10:34; Rev 6:6, 18:13. The ledger's rows whose move begins KEEP: 14.

### 2AR.4 Corrected in the draft before it was rendered

The first writing said Daniel 6 "supplies the stone and the seal"; 2O has it supplying one member of seven, and the draft now says that. It described the level-3 run as paying "by sheaves counted" and the Sirach run as "a vineyard, a same-day wage"; neither is in the record, and the draft now gives what 2AG.2 has (proportional pay, first to last, 1 of 13; no order, 2 of 13). It gave cut F's source as 22:12a with 22:13; the pack gave 22:13 alone. It did not say that three of the six full-pack runs had the pair exchanged; it now does, with the control's failure to discriminate.

### 2AR.5 What the draft does not yet do

1. It has a teaching practice only as sayings kept (its section I) and an adversary only at the passion. The ruling's list also names a geography and a childhood; the draft has the flight and the named king and nothing of Galilee.
2. Gundry, Davies and Allison, Sim and Aune are named as owed and are not engaged. Bauckham, Gurtner and Wenkel were consulted by abstract and first page. None of this reading is behind a claim in the draft.
3. Its section V rests on runs by three models of one maker. 2AG.6 already says so; the draft says so in a clause.
4. The three predictions of 2AQ.8 are not run. If the second holds (the plagues opposed in the miracles), the draft gains a section on chapters 8, 9, 14 and 15, which are now among its unreached.

## 2AS. The items of 2AR.5 worked: the miracles; the land, the childhood, the teaching, the adversaries; the programme; two figures

*Entered 27 November 2026, 00:05 to about 00:40.*

### 2AS.1 The miracles prediction (2AQ.8, second): sealed, run, failed

1. Sealed at 00:05 as section I of the addendum, before any count: four domains (earth and sores; sea; waters; sun), each with its terms from Rev 8:7–12 and 16:2–9 and its opposites; a hit = term and opposite in one verse or adjacent verses, in Matthew 8–9, 14–15. S1 held only if (a) three of four domains hit, (b) the matched table beat all 23 permuted tables, (c) the rate beat Acts'. My expectation was written with it: the sea hits; sores and sun probably have no term in those chapters; Acts 27 probably takes (c).
2. One correction of spelling after the first run, recorded here: the tagged text spells two lemmas without the iota subscript (σώζω, ἀποθνήσκω). The table was not otherwise touched. Both runs are in `plague/plague.out` and the first is described here: without σώζω the totals were 11 and the same tie.
3. Result, strict table: D1 one hit (χόρτος with χορτάζω, 14:19); D2 nine (8:24–27; 14:24–33); D3 two (14:28–29); D4 none. Three domains of four: (a) held, thinly for D1. Matched table 12, best permuted table 12: **(b) failed, by a tie**, and the tie is the table's own fault, since the sea's and the waters' opposites share σώζω and περιπατέω. Rate in the four chapters 81.6 per 1,000 verses against Acts 4.0: (c) held, against my expectation; Acts 27 gave three hits.
4. What decides it is not in S1's clauses. Over whole books: Matthew 16.9 per 1,000, Mark 14.9, Revelation 19.8, John 8.0, Luke 3.5. The sea stories are Mark's as much as Matthew's. Where the opposition exists it does not distinguish Matthew.
5. Loosened table (declared loosened; it cannot rescue S1): four domains hit, matched 19 against a best permuted 18.
6. S2 as counted: ἀποθνήσκω within a verse of ὕδωρ at Matt 8:32, John 4:47, Rev 8:9, 8:11, 16:3. John breaks exclusivity, as the seal said it might. S3 as counted: βλασφημέω within six verses of glory given, at Matt 9:3–8, Mark 2:7–12, Acts 13:45–48, Rev 16:9, 16:11. Luke 5 does not have it (the noun, not the verb).
7. Standing: **S1 failed.** The miracles stay in the residue. Three rows entered, each marked as known beforehand: the herd dying in the waters (the plague's act, patient exchanged); Peter on the waters with "save"; the blasphemy answered by glory.

1. Thirteen held, three failed as written, one too loose, one not counted. J19 (the name of living over the dead; the tombs) was recorded as read.
2. Of the ten I expected to be Matthew's alone among the Gospels (J1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14), seven are (J1, 2, 3, 6, 10, 12, 14).
3. What these are worth. They were written by a reader who knows both books, with Matthew in view. A held line shows that the memory was right and gives the count over the whole New Testament; it does not show that a reader without Matthew would have gone from the place in Revelation to the place in Matthew. That needs packs and blind runs, as at 2AG. The candidates for a pack, by this table: the gates (J10), the mountain (J4), the cargo (J3).
4. Counted beside them, not sealed: ἀνατολή within a verse of king or magus, at Matt 2:1, 2, 9 and Rev 16:12 only. δίδωμι in the aorist passive with ἐξουσία, at Rev 6:8, 9:3, 13:5, 13:7 and Matt 28:18 only. ἐρημόω at Matt 12:25, Luke 11:17, Rev 17:16, 18:16 (Tischendorf's numbering), 18:19 only. μακάριος: Luke 15, Matthew 13, Revelation 7. οὐαί: Luke 15, Revelation 14, Matthew 13, Mark 2.

### 2AS.3 The ledger

1. Twenty-six rows entered by `map/ledger.py` (the v0.45 script is kept as `ledger.v45.py`), each with its required lemmas checked in the tagged text: none fails. One row's span was widened after a failed check (ἅγιος stands at Rev 21:10 and 22:11, outside the first span given).
2. 78 rows. 166 of Matthew's 1,067 verses, 15.6 per cent (119 and 11 per cent at v0.45). Chapters with no row: 1 and 15 (seven at v0.45). The union with the machine layer was not recounted.
3. A caution on the chapter count. Chapters 6, 9 and 11 have one row each and chapters 7, 8, 12 and 14 two. A chapter with a row is not a chapter generated.
4. By kind, as drawn in the figure: kept 20 rows; exchanged 23 lines; opposed or turned 10; given a form 9; the engine's 18.

### 2AS.4 What this does to T_Μ (2AQ)

1. No new move was needed. The childhood is P2 with F5 and M5; the land is P2 with M2 and M6; the adversaries are M2 applied to a kind of relation 2AQ.3 did not list: **the speaker's reproof of an assembly, and the speaker's exposure of false claimants, said by the teacher of the adversaries in the land** (Rev 2:2, 2:9, 3:1, 3:9, 3:17 to Matt 23). Entered as a seventh condition for the next version of the table and not yet written into 2AQ.
2. Composition C2 (one stretch, many places) has two new cases as clear as the wedding: the Sermon's close (Matt 7:6–20 from Rev 21:21–22:15, 13:11, 8:7) and the woes (Matt 23 from Rev 2:2, 2:9, 3:1, 3:17, 16:6, 18:24, 2:23).
3. The frame gains its last verse: "all authority was given to me" (28:18) is the passive charge of the rider and the beast, in the first person (M4).

### 2AS.5 The reading

1. Read for this entry, by what is publicly obtainable: Bauckham 1977 and 1983 by first page and notes (the 1977 article is reprinted in *The Climax of Prophecy*, 1993, 92–117: confirmed, and ChatGPT's date is the reprint's; 2AR.2 item 2 stands corrected to that extent). Bauckham's note names Vos, *The Synoptic Traditions in the Apocalypse* (Kampen, 1965), as "the only systematic treatment": the received-direction study, not read. Gundry 1982 through three reviews (France; Carson; Moo): Matthew used Mark and Q and embellished deliberately in the manner of midrash, the birth narrative, the Sermon and Peter on the water among the cases. Sim 1996 through the publisher's summary and Weber's review: eight characteristics of apocalyptic eschatology found throughout Matthew, compared with Revelation among others.
2. Not read and declared: Davies and Allison; Aune; the body of Goulder; the body of every article above. The draft's claims about these are limited to what the abstract, first page or review says.

### 2AS.6 The programme and the figures in the draft

1. A section P is placed first in the draft: the thesis and its three strengths; the epistles as the easier case (the seven letters; the Muratorian fragment's *sequens prodecessoris sui Johannis ordinem*, read in the seat, Tregelles's line 3210, MS spelling "semptaem"; the missing ancestor); the site's five transforms and the James derivation with their grades, from `unfolding/transforms.json` and `unfolding/james/derivation.json` as read in the repository; what is provisional or failed for the Gospels; why the Gospel is the harder case; why it matters; why it will be resisted, stated as costs.
2. Two figures, both generated by script. `REV-TO-MATTHEW-BEARERS.svg`: seven bearers in Revelation, five in Matthew, fourteen lines each with a checked example (`paper/diagram2.py`; hand-specified). `REV-TO-MATTHEW-MAP.svg`: every row of the ledger from its verse position in Revelation to its verse position in Matthew, 105 lines for 78 rows, coloured by kind of move (`paper/diagram.py`; drawn from the ledger's JSON). Palette checked by the validator: passes separation; four of five colours are below 3:1 against white, so the ledger table is the table view.

### 2AS.7 Still owed

1. Packs with cuts for the gates, the mountain and the cargo, so that at least one of this entry's rows is tested as 2AG tested the labourers.
2. The first and third predictions of 2AQ.8.
3. The seventh condition written into 2AQ's table, and the table rerun against the 78 rows for conflicts.
4. The union of ledger and machine layer recounted.

### 2AT.2 Scripture, read in the seat (sealed K5–K7: each as expected)

1. Isaiah 60:6: ἥξουσιν φέροντες χρυσίον, καὶ λίβανον οἴσουσιν; kings at 60:3. Gold and frankincense brought by those who come. Nearer to Matt 2:11 than Rev 18:12–13, where they are cargo. J3's count stands for the New Testament and decides nothing against Isaiah.
2. Exodus 2:15: ἐζήτει ἀνελεῖν Μωυσῆν· ἀνεχώρησεν. Exodus 4:19: τεθνήκασιν γὰρ πάντες οἱ ζητοῦντές σου τὴν ψυχήν. Matt 2:20 is 4:19 with πάντες dropped and σου made τοῦ παιδίου. The wording of the pursuit is Exodus's.
3. Numbers 24:17: ἀνατελεῖ ἄστρον. The rising is there; the noun is ἄστρον, where Matthew and Revelation have ἀστήρ.
4. Hosea 11:1 in the Septuagint: τὰ τέκνα αὐτοῦ. Matthew's τὸν υἱόν μου is another form of the text.
5. Micah 5:4 ποιμανεῖ; Psalm 2:9 ποιμανεῖς αὐτοὺς ἐν ῥάβδῳ σιδηρᾷ.
6. Ezekiel 40:2: ἔθηκέν με ἐπʼ ὄρος ὑψηλὸν σφόδρα, a city on it; 40:4 δεικνύω. Deuteronomy 34:1: the land shown. Isaiah 48:2, 52:1: the holy city.
7. What this leaves. In the infancy and the trial, scripture supplies most of what the rows of 2AS claimed for Revelation, and Matthew cites scripture openly there. Left to Revelation: the configuration of 12:1–6 (sign, birth, the hunted newborn, flight) and the joining of trial, holy city and mountain. Both are read and untested. Four rows carry the rival on their standing in the ledger.

### 2AT.3 Reach by kind of material (sealed K8: held)

Proxy: a verse of Matthew is MARK if it shares three content lemmas (or half, at least two) with a verse of Mark; else DOUBLE on the same test against Luke; else OWN. 458, 184, 425 verses. Reached by the ledger of 80 rows: MARK 50 (10.9%); DOUBLE 34 (18.5%); OWN 84 (19.8%). Of the 62 hand rows, the required lemmas also stand together in a verse of Luke for 32 (25 of them in Mark too), in Mark only for 3, in neither for 27. The proxy over-assigns to Mark any verse with common narrative words; a synopsis in the seat would replace it.

### 2AT.4 The city and the Sermon (sealed K1–K4)

K1 held: ὁράω, future, third plural, with God or the face: Matt 5:8 and Rev 22:4 (Acts 2:17 a false hit). Entered as a row. K2 as expected, common stock: murder, fornication and falsehood within twenty verses at Matt 5:21, 15:19, 19:9, Mark 10:19, Luke 18:20, 1 Tim 1:9, Rev 21:8, 22:15; not entered. K3: mourning with comfort or wiping at Matt 5:4, Luke 6:25, Rev 21:4; Isaiah 61:2 (παρακαλέσαι πάντας τοὺς πενθοῦντας) is nearer; entered as read. **K4 failed**: content lemmas of Rev 21:1–22:15 found per 1,000 content tokens: Matt 10, 105; Matt 23, 95; Matt 24–25, 92; Matt 13, 87; **Matt 5–7, 82**; Matt 8–9, 75; Matt 26–27, 68. The Sermon is fourth of the five sealed stretches.

### 2AT.5 The draft at v0.3

About 13,200 words with Appendix A. 174 Greek quotations checked against the seats (Nestle, Swete, Philo), all exact. Section 0 first, then P. New: II.7–8, III.6, V.3a, V.5a–b, VI.7, VII.9, VIII.8, XIII.0, XIII.3–4, XIV.1a, XIV.5a, Appendix A.

## 2AU. Philo as a second source for what the ledger does not reach: a first test

*Entered 27 November 2026, 00:40 to about 01:10. Sealed as section L before the instrument was built.*

### 2AU.1 The instrument

Philo is seated unlemmatized (31 treatises, 7,901 sections). Josephus (12,423 sections) is the control and the Septuagint (29,277 verses) the filter. A word is reduced to its first five letters, accents and case removed; words under five letters are dropped, which loses γῆ, θεός and the like. A stem is rare if it stands in at most 60 verses of the New Testament. A pair of a Gospel verse is Philo's if both stems stand in one section of Philo and in no one verse of the Septuagint.

### 2AU.2 The general result

| Verses | n | with a pair Philo has and Josephus lacks | with a pair Josephus has and Philo lacks | ratio |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Matthew, unreached, MARK | 408 | 34.6% | 30.9% | 1.12 |
| Matthew, unreached, DOUBLE | 150 | 37.3% | 26.7% | 1.40 |
| Matthew, unreached, OWN | 341 | 29.3% | 22.3% | 1.32 |
| Matthew 5–7, unreached | 93 | 34.4% | 24.7% | 1.39 |
| Matthew, all | 1,068 | 33.8% | 27.6% | 1.22 |
| **Mark, all** | 674 | 34.6% | 26.6% | **1.30** |
| Luke, all | 1,149 | 39.5% | 26.5% | 1.49 |

**L1 failed** (OWN is below MARK). **L3 failed**: Mark shows the excess, and Luke shows more. By the seal that empties L2 as well, though L2 holds by its letter (1.39 in the Sermon against a ratio of sections of 0.64). Philo's seated text runs to more words than Josephus's, and by words the excess is not there. The instrument measures the overlap of Philo's Greek with a Gospel's and does not single out Matthew or its unreached part.

### 2AU.3 The named places (L4)

1. **(a) The oath: found, and it is a construction.** Spec. 2.2: ὁ γὰρ τοῦ σπουδαίου, φησί, λόγος ὅρκος ἔστω. Spec. 2.5: if something must be added, not the highest cause, ἀλλὰ γῆν, ἥλιον, ἀστέρας, οὐρανόν. Decal. 84: κάλλιστον … τὸ ἀνώμοτον. Matt 5:37: ἔστω δὲ ὁ λόγος ὑμῶν ναὶ ναί; 5:34–35: not by heaven, not by earth. λόγος as subject of ἔστω in a unit with an oath word: Philo, Spec. 2.2, and a fragment under Philo's name printed in the edition's apparatus at Decal. 83 (ἔστω οὖν ὁ λόγος ἰσότιμος ὅρκῳ); Josephus none; Septuagint none; New Testament, Matt 5:37 only. Moves: KEEP the sentence; OPPOSE the allowance. Revelation's row at this place (10:6, the angel's oath by the maker of heaven) supplied the oath and the heaven and nothing of the positive half. The two sources meet in three verses. (My stem search for "swear, heaven, earth" returned nothing because γῆ is under five letters; the place was found by phrase and read.)
2. **(b)** "The one who sees God", ὁρῶν (τὸν) θεόν and the like: 54 sections of Philo, one of Josephus, no verse of the Septuagint. Beside Matt 5:8 and Rev 22:4. Read; no construction beyond the two words.
3. **(c)** εἰρηνοποιός: once in Philo, of God (Spec. 2.192, τοῦ εἰρηνοποιοῦ θεοῦ καὶ εἰρηνοφύλακος); the verb once in the Septuagint (Prov 10:10) and once in the New Testament (Col 1:20); the noun in the New Testament at Matt 5:9 only, where the peacemakers are called sons of God. Read.
4. **(d)** Treasure with heaven: seven sections of Philo, none De praemiis 104 as I had it from memory, and the Septuagint has it (Deut 28:12). Not Philo's.
5. **(e)** Desire with adultery: six sections of Philo, one of Josephus, none of the Septuagint. Decal. 129 read: the adulterer sated of desire. Looser than Matt 5:28. Not counted as a gap closed.

### 2AU.4 Candidates the instrument listed in the unreached Sermon, unread

Each is a pair Philo has in one section and Josephus and the Septuagint lack. None has been read, and section numbers restart in each book of a treatise, so the reference names the treatise only: 5:22 (liable, council); 5:25 (adversary, officer); 5:29–30 (right, members); 5:44 (love, enemies); 5:48 (you shall be, perfect); 6:6 (father, inner room); 6:24 (hold to, serve); 6:26 (barn, differ; sow); 6:27 (stature, add); 7:14 (lead, find); 7:19 (cut down, fruit); 7:24 (rock, liken). The list is in `philo/philo.out`.

### 2AU.5 Standing

Philo closes one gap by a shared construction, at the oaths, and stands beside two blessings. The general claim that Philo supplies the unreached material is not supported by this instrument, which cannot tell Matthew from Mark. What would test it properly: Philo lemmatized, so that constructions and not stems are counted; the twelve candidates read; and the question put the other way, from Philo's treatment of the commandments (De decalogo; Spec. 1–4) to the antitheses in their order.

## 2AV. Philo to Revelation, with Matthew locating the places: the right edge

*Entered 27 November 2026, 00:53 to about 01:25. Sealed as section M before Philo was searched.*

### 2AV.0 The correction, and a ruling

1. 2AU ran Philo to Matthew. The author at 00:49: "no, not philo to matthew. philo to rev … with matthew locating passages for the comparison. triangulate." 2AU is kept as run. Its three finds (the oath, seeing God, the peacemaker) are taken up here on the right edge.
2. Read before building: In the Midst of the Seven (#1758): section 0 (method and limits), 9 (the lampstand, the seven and the dividing Logos in Quis heres), 16.7 (the dependence argued: specificity, clustering, transformation, order, with the alternatives heard first), 17 (established and not), and the socket (4.4a, 7b.10). Not read in this session: its Parts on the Ginza, the Haran Gawaita and the filing.
3. The ruling at 00:52: rows where Matthew shares material with Revelation and the Septuagint are left. "That changes what the overlap can show, it doesn't change a structured clustering of shared rev matthew Septuagint material from being significant." 2AT.2 item 7 said scripture "leaves" Revelation only the configuration; that is narrowed by this ruling. The rows stand with the rival on their standing, and the clustering of three-way material at the birth and at the trial is itself to be explained. The draft's II.8 and III.6 now say so.

### 2AV.1 The nine places and what the seat has

Seven found, one failed, one half.

### 2AV.2 The three triangles that close

1. **The city.** Philo reads Jerusalem as vision of peace, says vision of peace and vision of God are one thing, and puts the city in a person. Revelation builds the city Jerusalem, with a river, without a temple, its people seeing the face. Matthew has "they shall see God" and "the peacemakers … sons of God" as adjacent blessings (5:8, 5:9) and "a city set on a mountain" said of the hearers (5:14). The move the draft called Matthew's (the city becomes an ethics) is Philo's, stated. 2AU's two "beside the blessings" (the one who sees God, 54 sections; εἰρηνοποιός of God) are the two halves of this name.
2. **The wealth that sees.** Philo's pair, blind wealth and seeing wealth. Revelation 3:17–18: rich, poor and blind; buy, and salve to see. Matthew 6:19–23: treasure in heaven, then at once the eye as lamp, sound or bad; in Luke the two are a chapter apart (12:33–34; 11:34). An unreached stretch of the Sermon gets its join from the letter, and the letter's join is Philo's. Entered as a row: Rev 3:17–18 to Matt 6:19–23 (lemma check passes). The ledger is at 81 rows, 173 verses, 16.2%; by class MARK 11.1%, DOUBLE 20.7%, OWN 19.8%. K8's predicted order (OWN highest) held at 80 rows and does not hold at 81: the new row lies in material shared with Luke.
3. **The coin.** Philo's soul as coin under God's seal. Revelation's two strikings of persons, the seal and the mark that licenses buying. Matthew's νόμισμα, image and inscription, each given back to its striker.

And one that closes in thought and not in count: **the two equalities** (Her. 141–146) as the two clauses of Rev 22:12–13 and the dispute of Matt 20:12.

### 2AV.3 A member Matthew has from Philo that Revelation does not show

At the birth: no mortal husband, God the one who sows, the virgin (Cher. 43–50) against Matthew 1:18–23. Revelation 12 has a mother and no word of this. This is the pattern of 2D.2 (complementary inheritance) and of the manna at 2B.7: the later book keeps a member the source dropped. It tells for Philo reaching Matthew by a way other than Revelation at that place, or for Revelation holding Philo more fully than its words show. Recorded, not decided.

### 2AV.4 Clustering

De somniis 2: the twelve stars (6, 111–113), Sodom as blindness (192), the city of God (246–254). Quis heres: the seventh in the midst (215–225, #1758), equality and the divider (141–206), blind wealth (48, 76). De fuga: the Logos's parents (109, with the robe of #1758 at 110), the seeing wealth and the false coin (19). De plantatione: beginning and end (93), the coin under the seal (18). By the criterion that carried the lampstands, this is the result that matters most in the entry, and it is not yet tested against a control: whether nine places chosen from any author of Philo's size would fall into four treatises as often.

### 2AV.5 Standing, and what it is worth

1. The places were named by a reader who knows Philo, before the search. A found place confirms a memory and gives the counts. It is no blind derivation.
2. The alternatives, to be heard as #1758 hears them: Plato for blind wealth (Laws 631c, from memory, unchecked); the common etymology of Jerusalem (whether "vision of peace" is attested outside Philo before him is not checked); astral twelve as common stock; Wisdom of Solomon for the mother. None is examined here.
3. Owed: a control for the clustering (2AV.4); the alternatives of item 2; the ledger given a third column for Philo; packs for the city (Philo's section and Revelation 21–22 as source, the blessings as target) and for the wealth that sees; the rest of #1758 read.

## 2AW. T_Μ version 0.4: the third parameter, written and tested

*Entered 27 November 2026, 01:04 to about 01:30. Sealed as section N before any search.*

### 2AW.1 Why a parameter and not a second source

1. The author at 01:01: "it seems like this is a joint transform - philo + rev --> matthew." The evidence of 2AV for joint and against a simple chain: in four places Matthew holds a member of Philo's that Revelation's words do not carry (the peacemakers beside seeing God; νόμισμα; "let your word be"; no mortal husband and the virgin), and each stands where a row from Revelation already stood. Co-location is the evidence.
2. The evidence against Philo as a free second source: 2AU. Set against Matthew without the constraint of a Revelation place, Philo's overlap is the same for Mark.
3. So the statement is: Revelation selects the place and the relation; Philo's reading of that place, and the scripture under both, stand open beside it. The Septuagint already behaves this way under the ruling of 2AV.0 item 3.

### 2AW.2 T_Μ v0.4, the change from v0.3 (2AQ)

- **S** as before: the source held whole; the unit is the relation with its bearer.
- **P1, P2** as before.
- **P3, new.** At each place of the source the layers it was made from stand open beside it: the scripture, and Philo's reading of the scripture. A member the source compressed may be restored in the telling. Philo enters only through a place the source holds and a row has located.
- **M1–M6** as before. **M7** (entered at 2AS.4): the speaker's reproof of an assembly, or exposure of a false claimant, said of the adversaries in the land.
- **F1–F5, C1–C5** as before. C2 (one stretch gathers many places) now gathers across layers as well: Matt 5:33–37 has Rev 10:6 and Spec. 2.2–5; Matt 5:8–9 has Rev 22:4, 21:7, Somn. 2.250–254 and Conf. 145–146.
- What P3 does not license: a parallel between Philo and Matthew with no place of Revelation between them. Such a thing is recorded as read and is outside the transform.

### 2AW.3 The seven sealed predictions

| | Revelation (from #1758) | Philo, in the seat | Matthew | Scripture checked | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| N1 | 2:17, the hidden manna | Leg. 3.174–176: the manna by Deut 8:3 in full, τουτέστι καὶ διὰ παντὸς τοῦ λόγου τραφήσεται | 4:4, with ἀλλ' ἐπὶ παντὶ ῥήματι ἐκπορευομένῳ διὰ στόματος Θεοῦ; Luke 4:4 as seated ends at "bread alone" | Deut 8:3 has the words | **held**, three-way |
| N1b | same | Leg. 3.162–163: τὸ τῆς ἡμέρας εἰς ἡμέραν, not all at once; Exod 16:4 also has ὅπως πειράσω αὐτούς | 6:11; 6:34 | Exod 16:4 | read; only "day" is shared; not counted. The manna given as a test joins the trial |
| N2 | 1:16; 2:12, the two-edged sword | Her. 130, 140: the Logos the cutter; Her. 225: ψυχὴ γὰρ τριμερὴς μέν ἐστι, δίχα δὲ ἕκαστον τῶν μερῶν … τέμνεται; τὸ δίχα τέμνειν | 10:34–35: a sword; διχάσαι; three pairs divided | Mic 7:6 has the pairs; διχάζω in the New Testament at Matt 10:35 only (διχοτομέω at Matt 24:51, Luke 12:46) | **held** |
| N3 | 1:13, 1:16 | Fug. 110: the head shone round with light; the garments put on | 17:2: garments white as the light; φῶς with ἱμάτιον in one verse of the Gospels here only | Ps 103:2 | **held**, three-way |
| N4 | 22:16; 16:12 | Conf. 62–63: ἰδοὺ ἄνθρωπος ᾧ ὄνομα ἀνατολή; ἀνέτειλε | 2:2, 2:9: ἐν τῇ ἀνατολῇ; singular with article in the Gospels here only (Luke 1:78 without article) | Zech 6:12; Num 24:17 | **held**, modest |
| N5 | 2:17; 19:12–13 | Mut. 121: Ἰησοῦς δὲ σωτηρία κυρίου | 1:21 | Sir 46:1 | **held**, three-way |
| N6 | 21:3 | Mut. 265: ὁ δὲ κύριος ἐν ἡμῖν (Num 14:9) | 1:23; 28:20 | Isa 7:14; 8:8, 8:10 | found; Matthew's wording is Isaiah's and Philo's differs: **weak** |
| N7 | 3:14; 21:7 | Conf. 145: υἱοὶ θεοῦ προσαγορεύονται; 146: ἀρχή … ὁ ὁρῶν, Ἰσραήλ | 5:8–9 | Hos 1:10; Deut 14:1 | **held**; the join with seeing is Philo's |

By the rule sealed with them (four of seven), P3 is written into the transform as tested at these places. My expectation was N1, N5, N7; N2 through N4 held as well.

### 2AW.4 What the six are and are not

1. Five are three-way. Scripture has the words in each; Philo supplies the join that brings them to this place. This is the structure the ruling of 2AV.0 named, now with a third layer.
2. They are predictions by a reader who knows Philo, Revelation and Matthew. Under seal they are a memory confirmed with counts. Each is owed a pack and a blind run.
3. The clustering still has no control (2AV.4). It has grown: De confusione 62–63 and 145–147; De mutatione 121 and 265; Legum allegoriae 3.162–176 and 3.203–206; Quis heres 130–146 and 215–225; De fuga 19 and 108–110; De somniis 2.

### 2AW.5 The ledger

Five rows entered (the name; God with them, at 1:23 and 28:20; the manna; the sword divided). 86 rows; 178 of 1,067 verses, 16.7%. By class: MARK 11.1%, DOUBLE 21.7%, OWN 20.5%. Chapter 1 has rows for the first time; chapter 15 alone has none.

### 2AW.6 Next, in order

1. The test P3 was written for: De decalogo and Spec. 1–4 against the antitheses in their order, admitted only where a place of Revelation stands between (the excluded of the city, 21:8 and 22:15, are the candidate place; 2AT.4 found them common stock by themselves, and P3 says to open them with Philo beside).
2. A control for the clustering of Philo's places by treatise.
3. Packs: the city (Somn. 2.246–254 with Rev 21–22, target the blessings); the name (Mut. 121 with Rev 19:12–13, target Matt 1:21).
4. The transform's table of 2AQ.3 rewritten whole as v0.4 and run against the 86 rows for conflicts.

### 2AX.2 Round 3

1. Sealed at 01:16 (section O). Packs in `tm/round3/`: C, the level-3 pack unchanged. H, C with P3 and Quis heres 141, 143, 144, 145 as far as ἀλλ' ἀναλογίᾳ (192 words). I, C with P3 and Quis heres 219, 221–224 (204 words). Runs: H and I on Fable, Opus, Sonnet; C on Opus and Sonnet beside Round 1's C on Fable. Each reader read one file and wrote one.
2. Read from the Greek:

| Run | Workers unequal in work | Pay | Dispute | Order | Line on first and last |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C Fable (Round 1) | yes | in proportion | none | first to last | none |
| C Opus | yes | in proportion (sheaves counted); idlers nothing | the idlers | first to last (alpha to omega) | none |
| C Sonnet | yes | in proportion (furrow measured) | none | first, second, last | none |
| I Fable | yes | in proportion; the sleeper nothing | none | first to last | none |
| I Opus | yes | in proportion; the sleeper an obol | the sleeper | first to last | none |
| I Sonnet | not told | "to each as his work was"; "no one complained" | none | — | none |
| **H Fable** | yes: ten cubits, two cubits, allotted "to each according to his strength" | **a shekel to each alike**; half to one who did half his lot | "πῶς ἴσον ἔλαβεν ὁ τοὺς δύο τῷ τοὺς δέκα;" answered οὐκ ἐν ἀριθμῷ … ἀλλὰ ἀναλογίᾳ | first to last | none |
| **H Opus** | yes: a weak old worker digs a third, a strong young one a half | **full wage to the old, half to the young** | "πλεῖον ἐγὼ ἔσκαψα τοῦ πρεσβύτου" | alpha to omega | none |
| **H Sonnet** | yes | in proportion | "οὐκ ἴσον ἔλαβον", answered οὐκ ἐν ἀριθμῷ, ἀλλ' ἀναλογίᾳ | not told | none |

3. Against the seal. **O1** (H: at least two of three pay the same for unequal work; C and I at most one each): C 0 of 3, I 0 of 3, as sealed. H: one run pays the same; one pays the lesser worker more. By the letter one of three, so **O1 fails by its letter**; in substance two of three detach pay from the amount of work. **O2 held** (an objection on ἴσος in H Fable and H Sonnet, and the answer in H Opus). **O3 held** (no reversal). The emptying condition did not occur: I gave no such pay.
4. The control used its layer. All three I runs set the master ἕβδομος ἐν μέσῳ of six, three on each side; two lit lamps. So "any Philo declared as the layer" is taken up by the readers and does not produce the conflict. The equalities passage does.
5. What H did not produce: the hours, the order turned, the saying. And H Fable's allotment "ἑκάστῳ κατὰ τὴν ἰσχὺν αὐτοῦ" is close to the talents (Matt 25:15, "to each according to his own power"): either a memory of that parable or the proportion of Philo's passage arriving at the same place. Not decided.
6. Reading. Round 1 and 2 showed the two verses with six forced consequences give the plot. Round 3 shows that one of those consequences, the conflict between pay by work and equal standing, need not be told if Philo's passage is open: the readers find it. The other consequences (the order as the title enacted; the dispute placed on those who did most; the closing line) still need telling. P3 reduces the instruction by that much, at this place, with these readers.
7. Limits as before: three models of one maker; they know Matthew; one place.

### 2AX.3 The five places that tell restoration from independent inheritance

Each has, in Matthew, something Revelation made and something Revelation's words lack that Philo has.

| Place | From Revelation | From Philo |
|---|---|---|
| the sword | peace, earth, sword in one verse: Rev 6:4 and Matt 10:34 only (counted) | διχάσαι (Her. 225) |
| the city | pearls, dogs, gate, entering (five members, 2AS); seeing | peace beside seeing; the city as persons (Somn. 2.250–254) |
| the oath | the oath naming heaven and earth | "let your word be" (Spec. 2.2) |
| the coin | an image with buying and selling | νόμισμα (Plant. 18) |
| the name | name with "called" (19:13) | the gloss by saving (Mut. 121) |

The wealth that sees is of the second kind (reworked and passed on). The manna, the garments and the sons of God have Philo's member and scripture's words, and what they keep from Revelation is the place only.

### 2AX.4 Owed

The four-procedure comparison as packs at the wealth and the eye, the oath and the two blessings. A critical apparatus for Rev 13:10. Vos and Bauckham. And from 2AW.6, unchanged: the antitheses through Rev 21:8 and 22:15 with De decalogo and Spec. 1–4; the control for the clustering.

## 2AY. The three seats; what was read of Vos and Bauckham

2AY.1. The author's rulings, 27 November 01:32. The relations go in a new record, a hologram in the frame of #1753, beside the framework hologram (#1755) and pointed to from it. This file is that record's notebook: hosted beside the record, replaced as it grows, each replaced copy kept by date, never ingested into the record's text. *How to Write Matthew from Revelation* is its own deposit: Lee Sharks, *Grammata: Journal of Operative Philology*. What can be read of the owed scholarship is read before deposit. Nothing is deposited at this version.

2AY.2. The frame was read before building (#1753, sections 0–5). Its rule 1: a kernel points at claims; every claim is a quotation in a record, bound by the record's hash. The Matthew relations are held in this file and in the standalone, and neither is a record. So the order of deposit is forced: the standalone first, then the hologram, whose builder rebinds each relation to the paper's record and hash. Until then each Matthew relation points to its ledger row by the file's hash and to the numbered paragraph of the draft that quotes both places, and says that the paper is not a record.

2AY.3. The relations hologram, draft v0.2 (EA-HK-REVFIRST-02; the number is proposed). Built by `holo/build_hk_relations.py` and checked by `holo/verify_and_map.py`; nothing a registry or data file holds is typed by hand. It reads the 24 arrows from the framework kernel, the 86 rows from the ledger, identifiers and hashes from the archive's lookup.

| Family | Relations |
|---|---|
| the letters and transforms to the epistles | 14 |
| arrows at the founding grain, and sayings observed | 10 |
| Matthew, the ledger | 86 |
| layers under Revelation (Philo; scripture) | 13 |
| planned, and tested and failed | 10 |
| **all** | **133** |

Sixteen standings, each with what it licenses and what it withholds. Seventeen of the 26 other books are reached above the founding grain. Mark, John, Acts and Jude are named or planned only. Five have no relation: 1 Timothy, Titus, Philemon, 2 John, 3 John. (The framework kernel counted Jude among the books without an arrow; it has one here because the seven-within-seven is entered as planned.) Sixty-two of the 86 Matthew rows are bound to a numbered paragraph of the paper by the places the paragraph quotes; the other 24 are in the ledger and were not located by that method. Thirteen structural tests, all passing. One failed on the first run and was the test's own wording. Back-projection has not been run. There are no captures at these addresses.

2AY.4. What the first compression (REV-NT-RELATIONS v0.1, 99 relations) got wrong. It typed the epistle relations from the site's files and so held 13 where the framework kernel holds 24 arrows; it had no standings beyond a label; it pointed at nothing. It is replaced by the kernel.

2AY.5. Vos and Bauckham. Searched again, twice. Vos (1965): no open copy; a snippet view only. Bauckham's chapter (*Climax of Prophecy*, 92–117): a full copy is posted on a document-sharing site and the session's fetcher could not retrieve it. Read instead, and marked as such in the paper:

| Source | Route | What it gives |
|---|---|---|
| Bauckham 1977 | the publisher's first page and notes | opens from the debate on prophets as makers of sayings; Revelation's first-person sayings the one undisputed first-century specimen; Bultmann (HST 127) cited for the process visible at Rev 16:15; Vos called the only systematic treatment, his conclusion of a fixed tradition something of an overreaction |
| Bauckham 1993 reprint | passages returned by public search | conclusions numbered: (1) parousia parables widely used; (2) collected and paired early; (3) deparabolized through use, 3:20 furthest |
| Bauckham 1983 | first page | the evidence outside the Gospels listed: 1 Thess, 2 Pet, Rev, Didache, Thomas |
| the review in AUSS 33 (1995) | open | Rev 3:3, 20 and 16:15 held dependent on the two parables |
| Tripaldi, Bologna thesis, 1.2.3 | open | Vos: twenty-five sayings, form stable, oral; direct and indirect use; occasioned by Koester; two limits (canonical only; no portrait of Jesus); a probable agraphon at Rev 2:10b (Vos 192–193) |

2AY.6. What the reading changed. (a) The direction worked in the paper's first section has a precedent in the received scholarship that the draft had not named: form criticism held that sayings of the risen one through prophets entered the tradition as sayings of Jesus, and used Revelation as its exhibit. The paper's difference is stated in one line: the exhibit is taken as the source. (b) Vos, as reported, finds an unwritten saying of Jesus at Rev 2:10b. That is the crown of life, the pair unique in the census and the seed of James. The received account has no extant source there and posits one. (c) A datum both directions owe: outside the Gospels and Thomas the thief is a saying four times and a story never. I.6 is rewritten on these; VII.8 gains a sentence; the works cited say by which route each thing was read.

2AY.7. The standalone at v0.7. A paragraph for the machine reader at the head, in the third person; Appendix B with the executive symbolon, tooth, canary, minimum composition answer, confidence registers, canonical and negative edges, test queries (none run), indexing metadata and JSON-LD. A count corrected in making it: the transform has twenty-one rules in five groups, not nineteen in four. 221 quotations verified, none inexact.

2AY.8. Owed, added to the list of 2AX. The paper bindings by a better method than shared loci. Back-projection on scope A by a reader who has not seen the records. The 24 rows not located in the paper either placed in it or left to the ledger by decision. Bauckham's chapter in full, by whatever route opens. The byline of the hologram: proposed Damascus Dancings, after #1755, and not ruled.

## 2AZ. Residue first: forty rows, and reach from 178 to 246

2AZ.0. Deposited between 2AY and this section, at the author's word of 01:48 ("the word."): *How to Write Matthew from Revelation* as #1812 (AXN:0D8B.GENERATIVE, Lee Sharks, *Grammata*) and the relations hologram as #1813 (AXN:0D8D.MPAI, Damascus Dancings), both live and both stamped. This file is hosted beside #1813 and replaced as it grows. What follows is after both deposits and is in neither.

2AZ.1. The review. It was pasted at 02:49 and proposes starting from what in Matthew is unexplained: Matthew 18 from the letters, Matthew 10 from the witnesses, the Baptist from Revelation 14, the healings from Revelation's restorations, Matthew 15 from the dogs outside the city, the last commission from the first. It says of itself that its correspondences are leads and were not run. They were run here. Sixteen lines were written to the sealed file at 02:51, from memory of the Greek, before the tagged text was opened for them.

2AZ.5. Seven of them, stated.

(a) Matthew 18 is the assembly's chapter and it has the letters' words. ἐκκλησία stands in the four Gospels at two places, both Matthew's; one is 18:17, in the steps of correction, with the one use of ἐλέγχω in Matthew two verses before. The same chapter has the angels of the little ones who see the Father's face (18:10; Rev 1:20 with 22:4), the stumbling block (18:7; Rev 2:14, the only one in Revelation) joined to the millstone and the sea (18:6; Rev 18:21), binding and loosing (18:18; Rev 20:2–3), "there am I in the midst" (18:20; Rev 1:13; 2:1), and at its end a servant who falls and worships and a fellow servant who falls before a fellow servant (18:26–29). σύνδουλος stands in the Gospels only in Matthew. Falling, worship and fellow servant stand within three verses at Matt 18:26–29, Rev 19:10 and Rev 22:8–9 and nowhere else in the New Testament: the seer falls to worship the angel and is told, I am your fellow servant. Eleven of the chapter's 34 verses now have a row; it had one.

(b) The ten virgins are made from three letters. θύρα, κλείω, ἀνοίγω, ἀγοράζω, γρηγορεύω, ὥρα: five or six of the six stand within eighteen verses only in Matthew 24–25 and Revelation 2–3. Sardis: you shall not know the hour. Philadelphia: he opens and none shuts, a door opened. Laodicea: buy from me; I stand at the door. With the wedding of Rev 19 (already a row) that is the parable's whole furniture except the oil, and λαμπάς stands in the Gospels only here and at John 18:3, and in Revelation at 4:5 and 8:10. This is the gates knot of 2AS again: one stretch of Matthew gathered from places of the source that lie close together.

(c) "He began to show his disciples that he must." Mark 8:31 has "to teach". Matthew 16:21 has δεικνύειν ... ὅτι δεῖ. δεικνύω with δεῖ stands in the New Testament at Matt 16:21 and at Rev 1:1, 4:1 and 22:6: "to show his servants what must happen" is the first sentence of the book. And δεῖ with ἀποκτείνω stands only in the three passion predictions and at Rev 11:5 and 13:10. A change Matthew makes to Mark, toward the opening words of Revelation.

(d) The false prophet. ψευδοπροφήτης with πλανάω: Matt 24:11, 24:24, Rev 19:20, 20:10 and nowhere else (Mark 13:22 has another verb). Great signs with leading astray: Matt 24:24 and Rev 13:13–14 only.

(e) The demons' question. "Have you come here before the time to torment us?" (8:29). "Before the time" is Matthew's; Mark's demon asks only not to be tormented. βασανίζω with καιρός stands here alone. In Revelation the devil knows he has a short time (12:12) and shall be tormented (20:10). The adversary speaks what the source says he knows.

(f) The only healing in Revelation is the beast's. θεραπεύω stands there twice, 13:3 and 13:12, of the wound healed, with the whole earth marvelling and with authority given and exercised. Matthew 10:1: he gave them authority, to heal (the two words in one verse only at Matt 10:1, Luke 9:1, Rev 13:12). Matthew 15:30–31: he healed them, so that the crowd marvelled, seeing. That gives Matthew 15 its first row, and it gives the miracles a relation the failed prediction of 2AS did not look for: the adversary's wonder done by the teacher. It is entered as read, and it is one reading of two words.

(g) Sayings Matthew shares with Mark that stand, outside the Gospels, only in Revelation. A mountain thrown into the sea (21:21; Rev 8:8). Scorched by the sun: καυματίζω in four verses of the New Testament, the sower's two and the fourth bowl's two (13:6; Rev 16:8–9). The fig tree, three verses after the stars fall, as in Rev 6:13. Seized and bound and put in prison, of John (14:3; Rev 20:2, 7). The son of man seated, with a cloud (26:64; Rev 14:14). Watch, and the hour (26:40; Rev 3:3). These are Mark's as much as Matthew's and are marked so. They bear on the question the review raises and this file has left alone: where Mark's material came from.

2AZ.6. What did not yield. The dogs of Matthew 15:26: Matthew has the diminutive, the lemma check fails, and no row was entered; the reading (the boundary of the city tried in a story) is recorded here as a reading. The Sabbath: nothing. Prayer and incense: nothing looked for. Keeping to the end, and "in the midst" taken alone: common. The lost sheep and Thyatira share two common verbs in different senses; not entered. The powers of the witnesses against the powers of the Twelve: one common word. The name of the Father at 28:19 stands with Rev 14:1 among nineteen verses and is entered as read only.

2AZ.7. Reach.

| verses of Matthew reached | 178 (16.7%) | 246 (23.1%) |
| of the 458 shared with Mark (proxy) | 51 (11.1%) | 76 (16.6%) |
